## 1. Gen4P: Video Generators are 4D Perceivers

**中文标题**: Gen4P: Video Generators are 4D Perceivers

**作者**: Yitong Xia, Zeren Jiang, Chuanxia Zheng

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22371v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22371v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22371v1)

**项目主页**: https://gen4p.github.io

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We introduce Gen4P, a unified framework for recovering 4D geometry and motion from monocular video. We show that a pretrained video generator can be repurposed into a feed-forward 4D perceiver without iterative denoising. Building on on-demand querying, Gen4P processes clean video latents in a single encoder pass and predicts the requested 4D attributes through a lightweight cross-attention decoder. The on-demand query formulation enables flexible spatial sampling, and our proposed continuous-time queries further provide temporal flexibility. To strengthen geometric knowledge, we propose a straightforward plug-in solution by incorporating a pretrained video-depth LoRA as a frozen geometry prior, improving overall performance without adding extra trainable parameters. Gen4P achieves state-of-the-art or competitive performance on tasks of 3D point tracking, video depth and camera pose estimation. Qualitative results on in-the-wild videos illustrate its capability of tracking complex motion while maintaining the delicate structure. Together, these results demonstrate the potential of video generative pretraining for unified 4D perception. See https://gen4p.github.io for more visual results.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We introduce Gen4P, a unified framework for recovering 4D geometry and motion from monocular video. We show that a pretrained video generator can be repurposed into a feed-forward 4D perceiver without iterative denoising. Building on on-demand querying, Gen4P processes clean video latents in a single encoder pass and predicts the requested 4D attributes through a lightweight cross-attention decoder. The on-demand query formulation enables flexible spatial sampling, and our proposed continuous-time queries further provide temporal flexibility. To strengthen geometric knowledge, we propose a straightforward plug-in solution by incorporating a pretrained video-depth LoRA as a frozen geometry prior, improving overall performance without adding extra trainable parameters. Gen4P achieves state-of-the-art or competitive performance on tasks of 3D point tracking, video depth and camera pose estimation. Qualitative results on in-the-wild videos illustrate its capability of tracking complex motion while maintaining the delicate structure. Together, these results demonstrate the potential of video generative pretraining for unified 4D perception. See https://gen4p.github.io for more visual results.

</details>

## 2. Which Tokens to Merge? Diffusion Dynamics for Efficient Image Generation

**中文标题**: Which Tokens to Merge? Diffusion Dynamics for Efficient Image Generation

**作者**: SeungJu Cha, Ye-Chan Kim, HyunGee Kim, Sungho Koh, Dong-Jin Kim

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22370v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22370v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22370v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/SeungJuCha/DaTMe.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Token merging has emerged as an effective training-free strategy for accelerating diffusion models by reducing redundant token computation while minimizing generation degradation. A key challenge is determining which tokens can be safely merged and which should be preserved for subsequent computation. Existing method addresses this by preserving prompt-aligned foreground regions, thereby protecting semantically relevant structures. However, this text-relevance criterion overlooks background objects, local details that are not explicitly mentioned in the prompt, leading to degraded structural coherence. In this work, we propose Dynamics-aware Token Merging (DaTMe), a training-free framework that grounds token merging in the underlying diffusion dynamics. Our key insight is that token importance should reflect whether a token remains unresolved during denoising and whether it carries structural information. To this end, DaTMe constructs a dynamics- aware importance map from the Tweedie denoised estimate. The map combines a temporal signal, measuring inter-step changes in the predicted clean image, with a structural signal, capturing local high-frequency content such as boundaries and fine details. Using this map, DaTMe adaptively assigns tokens into merging roles under a given compression ratio: important tokens are preserved as anchors, while tokens that are both temporally stabilized and structurally homogeneous are selected as safe merge candidates. Experiments on PixArt-a and FLUX demonstrate that DaTMe maintains visual fidelity across the entire image, highlighting diffusion dynamics as an effective token-level merging decision in efficient generation. Our code is available at https://github.com/SeungJuCha/DaTMe.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing method addresses this by preserving prompt-aligned foreground regions, thereby protecting semantically relevant structures. However, this text-relevance criterion overlooks background objects, local details that are not explicitly mentioned in the prompt, leading to degraded structural coherence. In this work, we propose Dynamics-aware Token Merging (DaTMe), a training-free framework that grounds token merging in the underlying diffusion dynamics. Our key insight is that token importance should reflect whether a token remains unresolved during denoising and whether it carries structural information. To this end, DaTMe constructs a dynamics- aware importance map from the Tweedie denoised estimate. The map combines a temporal signal, measuring inter-step changes in the predicted clean image, with a structural signal, capturing local high-frequency content such as boundaries and fine details. Using this map, DaTMe adaptively assigns tokens into merging roles under a given compression ratio: important tokens are preserved as anchors, while tokens that are both temporally stabilized and structurally homogeneous are selected as safe merge candidates. Experiments on PixArt-a and FLUX demonstrate that DaTMe maintains visual fidelity across the entire image, highlighting diffusion dynamics as an effective token-level merging decision in efficient generation. Our code is available at https://github.com/SeungJuCha/DaTMe.

</details>

## 3. Perceive, Predict, and Plan: a Unified World Action Model for Egocentric Dexterous Manipulation

**中文标题**: Perceive, Predict, and Plan: a Unified World Action Model for Egocentric Dexterous Manipulation

**作者**: Zirui Wu, Chen Guo, Tianjian Jiang, Zeren Jiang, Zeming Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22363v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22363v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22363v1)

**项目主页**: https://p3-wam.pages.dev/

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We introduce P$^{3}$-WAM, a unified world action model for egocentric dexterous manipulation that learns 3D perception, action-conditioned prediction, and goal-directed planning through three complementary modes: Perceive, which reconstructs articulated hand motion and camera trajectories from video; Predict, which generates visual futures under prescribed actions; and Plan, which jointly generates action trajectories and their anticipated visual consequences conditioned on an initial image, a language instruction, and an optional visual goal. Our model integrates a compact, structured 3D action representation into a pretrained video diffusion transformer through a mixture-of-transformers architecture with shared base weights, modality-specific adaptation, and bidirectional visual-action attention. Randomized modality visibility lets video and 3D actions serve as either conditioning context or prediction targets across the three modes. Experiments show that unified training improves all three capabilities over single-mode training, including gains in planning quality under a vision-language model evaluation. Through unified training, our model outperforms specialized baselines on 3D hand reconstruction and action-conditioned video prediction across four datasets, while producing goal-directed manipulation videos with stronger instruction adherence and action progression than video-generation baselines. Project Page: https://p3-wam.pages.dev/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We introduce P$^{3}$-WAM, a unified world action model for egocentric dexterous manipulation that learns 3D perception, action-conditioned prediction, and goal-directed planning through three complementary modes: Perceive, which reconstructs articulated hand motion and camera trajectories from video; Predict, which generates visual futures under prescribed actions; and Plan, which jointly generates action trajectories and their anticipated visual consequences conditioned on an initial image, a language instruction, and an optional visual goal. Our model integrates a compact, structured 3D action representation into a pretrained video diffusion transformer through a mixture-of-transformers architecture with shared base weights, modality-specific adaptation, and bidirectional visual-action attention. Randomized modality visibility lets video and 3D actions serve as either conditioning context or prediction targets across the three modes. Experiments show that unified training improves all three capabilities over single-mode training, including gains in planning quality under a vision-language model evaluation. Through unified training, our model outperforms specialized baselines on 3D hand reconstruction and action-conditioned video prediction across four datasets, while producing goal-directed manipulation videos with stronger instruction adherence and action progression than video-generation baselines. Project Page: https://p3-wam.pages.dev/

</details>

## 4. DK-VLM: Geometry as Position for Spatial Reasoning in Vision-Language Models

**中文标题**: DK-VLM: Geometry as Position for Spatial Reasoning in Vision-Language Models

**作者**: Han Su, Meng Tian, Tianyu Huang, Mingyu Gao, Lennox Wei et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22356v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22356v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22356v1)

**项目主页**: https://sumuru789.github.io/DK-VLM-website/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language models still struggle to understand 3D spatial relations across views and update spatial judgments as the observer moves. A common approach fuses 3D reconstruction features into visual tokens, but aligning these features with pretrained semantic representations remains challenging under limited spatial supervision. We propose DK-VLM with Dual-Kernel Attention, which computes attention from both a visual token's input position and its geometric position in the scene. A semantic kernel retains the pretrained model's native attention and positional encoding, while a separate geometric kernel uses estimated 3D points and camera poses to directly modulate attention through point displacements and relative view transformations. Then, a learned gate fuses their outputs. Geometry thus enters attention as position rather than as fused reconstruction features. We further introduce Reference-Frame Composition (RC) supervision to train the model to update its reference frame across successive judgments. It provides stepwise turn and heading targets derived from verified single-step annotations. Among the compared methods, DK-VLM-8B achieves the highest average score across six spatial benchmarks and the highest overall scores on VSI-Bench (74.33) and ReVSI-32 (58.28). RC supervision further improves multi-step spatial reasoning, while the full model preserves general video understanding. Project page: https://sumuru789.github.io/DK-VLM-website/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language models still struggle to understand 3D spatial relations across views and update spatial judgments as the observer moves. A common approach fuses 3D reconstruction features into visual tokens, but aligning these features with pretrained semantic representations remains challenging under limited spatial supervision. Among the compared methods, DK-VLM-8B achieves the highest average score across six spatial benchmarks and the highest overall scores on VSI-Bench (74.33) and ReVSI-32 (58.28). RC supervision further improves multi-step spatial reasoning, while the full model preserves general video understanding. Project page: https://sumuru789.github.io/DK-VLM-website/.

</details>

## 5. Speculative Forcing for Efficient Autoregressive Video Generation

**中文标题**: Speculative Forcing for Efficient Autoregressive Video Generation

**作者**: Hyunwook Choi, Seungryong Kim, Dongyoon Han, Sangdoo Yun

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22347v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22347v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22347v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Few-step autoregressive video generators enable streaming generation, yet they run every token through the final denoising step, regardless of how much refinement it needs. We find this uniform computation is often unnecessary: many tokens already closely match their eventual full-step outputs in the initial steps, while others require refinement until the end. We introduce Speculative Forcing, a framework that treats intermediate clean estimates as drafts and predicts when individual tokens can be safely accepted early. Once accepted, a token is frozen as its final output and undergoes no further denoising, while its cached keys and values remain available as context for the remaining tokens. We explore two alternative verifier designs: a training-free one based on similarity to clean content and stability across steps, and a learned one that reads the frozen generator's intermediate features to estimate each token's remaining gap to its full-step output. Across short-video, long-video, and world generation, Speculative Forcing preserves the quality of full-step sampling while substantially reducing computation. On LongLive, it matches the four-step generator with ~1.7 denoising steps per token, running over 1.5x faster at 41.7 and 37.8 fps. On Lingbot-World 2.0, where camera motion continually changes the visible content, it maintains quality with ~2.5 denoising steps per token and runs 1.35x faster at 24.5 fps.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We find this uniform computation is often unnecessary: many tokens already closely match their eventual full-step outputs in the initial steps, while others require refinement until the end. On LongLive, it matches the four-step generator with ~1.7 denoising steps per token, running over 1.5x faster at 41.7 and 37.8 fps. On Lingbot-World 2.0, where camera motion continually changes the visible content, it maintains quality with ~2.5 denoising steps per token and runs 1.35x faster at 24.5 fps.

</details>

## 6. AVC-Reward: Event-Centric Audio-Video Consistency Evaluation

**中文标题**: AVC-Reward: Event-Centric Audio-Video Consistency Evaluation

**作者**: Yuruo Wu, Kaihang Pan, Kai Shen, Jiacheng Li, Shuyuan Tu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22321v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22321v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22321v1)

**项目主页**: https://tsuki0512.github.io/AVC-Reward.github.io/

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

As joint audio-video generation models become more capable, they can generate audio-visual scenes exhibiting three forms of complexity: multi-event composition, multi-layer audio mixture, and asymmetric cross-modal relations. However, current evaluations largely reuse metrics from earlier simple and unidirectional tasks. They typically evaluate an audio-video clip as a whole and rely on simple predefined relations, making them unsuitable for these increasingly complex scenes. We propose an event-centric evaluation framework that disentangles audio and visual events at the event level, analyzes each event independently, cross-validates relations across modalities, and aggregates the judgments into a coarse-to-fine, three-level consistency metric. The metric assesses global narrative harmony, event-level temporal synchronization, and fine-grained attribute alignment, providing a general formulation for audio-video consistency evaluation across diverse generation settings and cross-modal relations. We instantiate the metric as AVC-Reward, a trainable audio-video reward model. To evaluate the agreement between automatic judgments and human perception across generation paradigms and relation complexities, we construct AVC-Benchmark, which is primarily built from diverse positive and negative samples generated by various types of audio-video generation models. AVC-Reward achieves state-of-the-art alignment with human judgment among automatic methods, provides interpretable rationales that localize failures, and improves the audio-video consistency of a joint generation model when used as an online reinforcement-learning signal. Project page: https://tsuki0512.github.io/AVC-Reward.github.io/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

As joint audio-video generation models become more capable, they can generate audio-visual scenes exhibiting three forms of complexity: multi-event composition, multi-layer audio mixture, and asymmetric cross-modal relations. However, current evaluations largely reuse metrics from earlier simple and unidirectional tasks. We propose an event-centric evaluation framework that disentangles audio and visual events at the event level, analyzes each event independently, cross-validates relations across modalities, and aggregates the judgments into a coarse-to-fine, three-level consistency metric. AVC-Reward achieves state-of-the-art alignment with human judgment among automatic methods, provides interpretable rationales that localize failures, and improves the audio-video consistency of a joint generation model when used as an online reinforcement-learning signal. Project page: https://tsuki0512.github.io/AVC-Reward.github.io/

</details>

## 7. Velox: Spatial Memory Makes World Models Efficient

**中文标题**: Velox: Spatial Memory Makes World Models Efficient

**作者**: Qiuhong Shen, Shizun Wang, Xinchao Wang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22311v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22311v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22311v1)

**项目主页**: https://velox-world.com/velox/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video world models turn actions and camera motion into long visual rollouts, making video a natural interface for interactive simulation. Such interaction requires responsive generation, yet coherent rollouts must retain visual memory of what has already been generated. Chunked video diffusion models usually keep this context in growing KV caches, so later chunks become progressively slower. To address this bottleneck, we introduce Velox, a training-free attention router for region-level context selection using frustum slab correspondences from streaming geometry. Velox represents historical blocks as camera frustum slabs with depth intervals and obtains current-block depth through recency-priority multiwarp. For well-covered regions, frustum slab overlap softly modulates each layer's model-derived block importance. Uncovered regions retain the model-derived route without geometric bias. The fixed token-block granularity matches FlashAttention-style tiled execution and reduces the attention term that grows with rollout length. Frustum slab construction runs asynchronously with denoising on the same H200. At chunk 48, Velox accelerates attention by $4.36\times$ and denoising by $2.43\times$. On the full 158-case WBench navigation split, it remains within 0.15 API-free average points of dense LingBot-Fast-Cam. Please see our project page at https://velox-world.com/velox/ for more details.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video world models turn actions and camera motion into long visual rollouts, making video a natural interface for interactive simulation. Such interaction requires responsive generation, yet coherent rollouts must retain visual memory of what has already been generated. </details>

## 8. Routing Policy Optimization for Online Diffusion Reinforcement Learning

**中文标题**: Routing Policy Optimization for Online Diffusion Reinforcement Learning

**作者**: Qiuhong Shen, Yiding Yang, Yuxin Zhang, Xinwei Huang, Minxuan Lin et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22282v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22282v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22282v1)

**项目主页**: https://velox-world.com/rpo

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reinforcement learning can align visual generators with reward signals beyond their pretraining objectives, but existing score-function methods typically introduce stochasticity by perturbing the reverse denoising trajectory, leading to costly rollout-based optimization. We instead ask whether stochastic exploration can be introduced inside the generator while preserving its original sampler. Routing Policy Optimization (RPO) answers this question by turning attention routing into a stochastic exploration action. At a few selected denoising levels, each query block keeps its own block and samples further key blocks in proportion to the generator's native attention mass, or keeps dense attention. The sampled route perturbs the velocity of that transition, and a Gaussian likelihood around the dense velocity scores the perturbation with a score-function objective. RPO further uses a controlled two-rollout estimator: two rollouts share the same prompt, initial latent, routed denoising levels, and dense anchor layers, differing only in their sampled attention routes. Their reward difference provides an advantage signal for the routed transitions. Because the likelihood differentiates the dense velocity itself, the learned updates remain effective after routing is removed. At inference, RPO restores dense attention and retains the original deterministic sampler. Experiments on text-to-image and text-to-video generation show that RPO improves generation quality while achieving nearly 4x higher training efficiency than Flow-GRPO-style methods. Please see our project page at https://velox-world.com/rpo for visual results.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reinforcement learning can align visual generators with reward signals beyond their pretraining objectives, but existing score-function methods typically introduce stochasticity by perturbing the reverse denoising trajectory, leading to costly rollout-based optimization. Please see our project page at https://velox-world.com/rpo for visual results.

</details>

## 9. FutureLift: Lifting Trajectory Proposals into Bidirectional Future Graphs for Motion Forecasting

**中文标题**: FutureLift: Lifting Trajectory Proposals into Bidirectional Future Graphs for Motion Forecasting

**作者**: Daniel Grimm, J. Marius Zöllner

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22215v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22215v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22215v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Accurate motion prediction is critical for safe autonomous driving, yet even strong forecasters leave residual errors in their multimodal proposals. Existing refiners compress complete proposals or update their segments sequentially, preventing the full proposed future from serving as explicit relational context for correction. We introduce FutureLift, a backbone-agnostic refiner that represents each multimodal proposal as a heterogeneous future graph, with spatially grounded temporal nodes connected to agent histories, map elements, alternative modes, and, for joint prediction, other agents future trajectories. Bidirectional temporal edges allow later evidence to revise earlier segments in a single pass, and the updated nodes predict residual coordinate and confidence corrections. Requiring only proposals, scores, and scene context, FutureLift provides a common refinement interface for structurally different forecasting backbones. On Argoverse2 and the Waymo Open Motion Dataset, it consistently improves frozen proposals and outperforms native and specialized refiners across marginal and joint forecasting. On the WOMD motion-prediction leaderboard, FutureLift ranks first in minADE (0.5049m) and minFDE (0.9942m) among the compared standard-input methods, without LiDAR or ensembling, while achieving a competitive 0.4874 Soft mAP.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 10. Memory as Weights: Internalizing Long-Term History for Streaming Videos

**中文标题**: Memory as Weights: Internalizing Long-Term History for Streaming Videos

**作者**: Zhuoming Liu, Jinhong Lin, Lin Zhang, Yiwu Zhong, Yin Li

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22070v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22070v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22070v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Streaming vision-language models (VLMs) must continuously integrate information from the past while remaining responsive to current observations and user queries. Existing memory mechanisms typically compress, retrieve, or cache historical observations and reintroduce them into the input context, forcing historical memory and current observations to compete for limited context. We propose MaLoW, a streaming memory framework that instead internalizes historical context into evolving lightweight parameter updates. MaLoW uses a recurrent HyperNetwork to incrementally consolidate incoming video observations into fixed-size LoRA weights, allowing historical information to be carried forward without revisiting the full video stream. In doing so, MaLoW represents history as weights, while keeping the current observation as input. To support training, we further construct MaLoW-Instruct, a large-scale streaming video dataset containing 381K question-answer pairs over 56K videos. We conduct extensive experiments to demonstrate that MaLoW achieves strong performance on OVO-Bench, StreamingBench, and StreamArena. On the backward split of OVO-Bench, MaLoW outperforms prior memory approaches by 3.9% to 11.8% across different video backbones while incurring only modest latency overhead. Beyond streaming video understanding, MaLoW also generalizes to vision-language-action models, improving the action success rate on Simpler WidowX by 9.3%.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Streaming vision-language models (VLMs) must continuously integrate information from the past while remaining responsive to current observations and user queries. Existing memory mechanisms typically compress, retrieve, or cache historical observations and reintroduce them into the input context, forcing historical memory and current observations to compete for limited context. We propose MaLoW, a streaming memory framework that instead internalizes historical context into evolving lightweight parameter updates. MaLoW uses a recurrent HyperNetwork to incrementally consolidate incoming video observations into fixed-size LoRA weights, allowing historical information to be carried forward without revisiting the full video stream. In doing so, MaLoW represents history as weights, while keeping the current observation as input. To support training, we further construct MaLoW-Instruct, a large-scale streaming video dataset containing 381K question-answer pairs over 56K videos. We conduct extensive experiments to demonstrate that MaLoW achieves strong performance on OVO-Bench, StreamingBench, and StreamArena. On the backward split of OVO-Bench, MaLoW outperforms prior memory approaches by 3.9% to 11.8% across different video backbones while incurring only modest latency overhead. Beyond streaming video understanding, MaLoW also generalizes to vision-language-action models, improving the action success rate on Simpler WidowX by 9.3%.

</details>

---

## 11. Temporal Periodic Image Registration with Implicit Neural Representations

**中文标题**: Temporal Periodic Image Registration with Implicit Neural Representations

**作者**: Mathias Lowes, Kristine Aavild Sørensen, Maxime Sermesant, Rasmus R. Paulsen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21845v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21845v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21845v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/MMLowes/Temporal_INR.

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Implicit Neural Representations (INRs) have recently gained popularity for their ability to model functions using simple and lightweight networks. These methods have also proven effective in deformable image registration. In this work, we extend INRs for deformable image registration to the domain of temporal image registration, focusing on periodic temporal image sequences. Our approach, Temporal-IDIR, optimizes a single INR to model deformations across all frames in a temporal image sequence simultaneously, allowing for self-regularization through its own deformation predictions. To achieve this, we introduce a temporal consistency loss that penalizes discrepancies between direct source-to-target transformations and those traversing intermediate frames. We evaluate our framework on the DIR-LAB dataset, using the target registration error (TRE) between annotated and moved landmarks as the metric. Here, we achieve a TRE of 1.03 mm, outperforming other INR-based registration methods. Additionally, our framework supports smooth interpolation between time frames by estimating deformations between the given input frames. Code is publicly available at https://github.com/MMLowes/Temporal_INR.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Implicit Neural Representations (INRs) have recently gained popularity for their ability to model functions using simple and lightweight networks. These methods have also proven effective in deformable image registration. In this work, we extend INRs for deformable image registration to the domain of temporal image registration, focusing on periodic temporal image sequences. Our approach, Temporal-IDIR, optimizes a single INR to model deformations across all frames in a temporal image sequence simultaneously, allowing for self-regularization through its own deformation predictions. To achieve this, we introduce a temporal consistency loss that penalizes discrepancies between direct source-to-target transformations and those traversing intermediate frames. We evaluate our framework on the DIR-LAB dataset, using the target registration error (TRE) between annotated and moved landmarks as the metric. Here, we achieve a TRE of 1.03 mm, outperforming other INR-based registration methods. Additionally, our framework supports smooth interpolation between time frames by estimating deformations between the given input frames. Code is publicly available at https://github.com/MMLowes/Temporal_INR.

</details>

## 12. Beyond Source Prompts: Towards Visually Grounded AI-Generated Video Quality Assessment

**中文标题**: Beyond Source Prompts: Towards Visually Grounded AI-Generated Video Quality Assessment

**作者**: Kehao Zhou, Yixuan Li, Yuxuan Jiang, Fan Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21758v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21758v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21758v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/lolo123-123/VGCap.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent advances in generative artificial intelligence have accelerated video generation technologies. However, AI-generated videos (AIGVs) remain susceptible to various distortions, highlighting the need for dedicated video quality assessment methods. Content semantics play an important role in AIGV quality assessment, with source prompts typically used as textual references. However, these prompts may not accurately reflect the generated video content, resulting in a discrepancy between the intended textual description and the generated result. To address this limitation, this paper proposes VGCap, an AIGV quality assessment framework guided by visually grounded captions that align better with generated content. Specifically, we introduce two forms of captions: a global caption obtained by minimally revising the source prompt based on multi-frame visual evidence, and prompt-independent segment captions that describe localized temporal content. Together, these captions provide complementary textual evidence for assessing spatial quality, temporal quality, and text-video alignment. Experimental results on the LGVQ and FETV datasets show that VGCap outperforms representative baselines across all three dimensions. Further ablation studies on caption sources and temporal components confirm the effectiveness of visually grounded captions for each quality dimension, while highlighting the benefits of segment-level features for temporal quality prediction. The source of VGCap will be available at https://github.com/lolo123-123/VGCap.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Specifically, we introduce two forms of captions: a global caption obtained by minimally revising the source prompt based on multi-frame visual evidence, and prompt-independent segment captions that describe localized temporal content. Together, these captions provide complementary textual evidence for assessing spatial quality, temporal quality, and text-video alignment. Experimental results on the LGVQ and FETV datasets show that VGCap outperforms representative baselines across all three dimensions. Further ablation studies on caption sources and temporal components confirm the effectiveness of visually grounded captions for each quality dimension, while highlighting the benefits of segment-level features for temporal quality prediction. The source of VGCap will be available at https://github.com/lolo123-123/VGCap.

</details>

## 13. Edit4D: Editable 4D Scene Generation with Textual Control

**中文标题**: Edit4D: Editable 4D Scene Generation with Textual Control

**作者**: Chanwoo Kim, Tackgeun You, Hwasup Lim

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21668v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21668v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21668v1)

**项目主页**: https://chanwo0kim.github.io/Edit4D/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We introduce Edit4D, an editable text-to-4D scene generation framework that constructs 4D scenes from separate background and dynamic character representations. Recent advances in generative models have enabled text-driven dynamic scene synthesis, but editable 4D scene generation remains challenging due to the need to preserve spatial geometry and temporal dynamics. Existing approaches typically generate holistic scene videos, where the background and dynamic characters are coupled, making object-level editing and flexible camera control difficult. Edit4D addresses these limitations through a stage-specific generation pipeline. Given a user instruction, Edit4D first decomposes it into stage-specific prompts for background, dynamic character, and scene-character composition. Using these prompts, we synthesize multi-view scene images with text-to-image and camera-conditioned generative priors and reconstruct a background 3D Gaussian splatting representation. We then generate a character image, animate it with an image-conditioned video prior, reconstruct a 4D mesh, and bind Gaussian primitives to obtain a character representation. Finally, both components are composed through text-guided segmentation and contact-aware optimization. A global scene refinement is then performed to improve harmonization and rendering consistency. By integrating stage-specific components, Edit4D enables controllable camera motion, flexible object-level editing, and the practical construction of editable 4D scenes. Project page: https://chanwo0kim.github.io/Edit4D/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We introduce Edit4D, an editable text-to-4D scene generation framework that constructs 4D scenes from separate background and dynamic character representations. Recent advances in generative models have enabled text-driven dynamic scene synthesis, but editable 4D scene generation remains challenging due to the need to preserve spatial geometry and temporal dynamics. Existing approaches typically generate holistic scene videos, where the background and dynamic characters are coupled, making object-level editing and flexible camera control difficult. Edit4D addresses these limitations through a stage-specific generation pipeline. Given a user instruction, Edit4D first decomposes it into stage-specific prompts for background, dynamic character, and scene-character composition. Using these prompts, we synthesize multi-view scene images with text-to-image and camera-conditioned generative priors and reconstruct a background 3D Gaussian splatting representation. We then generate a character image, animate it with an image-conditioned video prior, reconstruct a 4D mesh, and bind Gaussian primitives to obtain a character representation. Finally, both components are composed through text-guided segmentation and contact-aware optimization. A global scene refinement is then performed to improve harmonization and rendering consistency. By integrating stage-specific components, Edit4D enables controllable camera motion, flexible object-level editing, and the practical construction of editable 4D scenes. Project page: https://chanwo0kim.github.io/Edit4D/

</details>

## 14. Digital Typhoon Dataset V3: Benchmarking for Multi-spectral Extension and Analyzing Systematic Bias in Intensity Regression

**中文标题**: Digital Typhoon Dataset V3: Benchmarking for Multi-spectral Extension and Analyzing Systematic Bias in Intensity Regression

**作者**: Asanobu Kitamoto, Victor Le Louarn, Noelia Falczuk, Ngoc Anh Tong, Anja Veleva

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21654v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21654v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21654v1)

**项目主页**: http://agora.ex.nii.ac.jp/digital-typhoon/dataset/

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/kitamoto-lab/digital-typhoon/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

This paper presents the Digital Typhoon Dataset V3, a new version of the longest typhoon satellite image dataset spanning nearly 50 years, designed to benchmark machine learning models on long-term spatio-temporal data. The latest addition to Dataset V3 is multispectral satellite data, enabling multi-channel models. We investigate data-splitting strategies and present benchmarking results for the Northern and Southern Hemispheres. Experimental results show that, under the current dataset construction and experimental setting, multi-channel inputs do not clearly outperform single-channel inputs for pressure regression. Among the evaluated model architectures, image types, and spectral channels, ResNet-18, full-resolution images, and Channel 1 generally provide the best single-channel performance for pressure regression. Performance also differs between the WP and AU datasets by regression target, with lower RMSE on WP for pressure regression and on AU for wind regression. These results highlight the importance of considering dataset-specific and task-specific characteristics when interpreting benchmark performance. Additionally, we analyzed systematic bias in intensity regression, which leads to underestimation of intensity for strong typhoons. We tested imbalance-aware weighting strategies, including inverse-density weighting inspired by BalancedMSE, and showed that these approaches are not sufficient to remove the bias. The dataset is publicly available at http://agora.ex.nii.ac.jp/digital-typhoon/dataset/ and https://github.com/kitamoto-lab/digital-typhoon/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The latest addition to Dataset V3 is multispectral satellite data, enabling multi-channel models. We investigate data-splitting strategies and present benchmarking results for the Northern and Southern Hemispheres. Experimental results show that, under the current dataset construction and experimental setting, multi-channel inputs do not clearly outperform single-channel inputs for pressure regression. Among the evaluated model architectures, image types, and spectral channels, ResNet-18, full-resolution images, and Channel 1 generally provide the best single-channel performance for pressure regression. Performance also differs between the WP and AU datasets by regression target, with lower RMSE on WP for pressure regression and on AU for wind regression. These results highlight the importance of considering dataset-specific and task-specific characteristics when interpreting benchmark performance. Additionally, we analyzed systematic bias in intensity regression, which leads to underestimation of intensity for strong typhoons. We tested imbalance-aware weighting strategies, including inverse-density weighting inspired by BalancedMSE, and showed that these approaches are not sufficient to remove the bias. The dataset is publicly available at http://agora.ex.nii.ac.jp/digital-typhoon/dataset/ and https://github.com/kitamoto-lab/digital-typhoon/.

</details>

## 15. FilterFusion: Prior-Guided Adaptive Filter Fusion for Efficient Video Denoising

**中文标题**: FilterFusion: Prior-Guided Adaptive Filter Fusion for Efficient Video Denoising

**作者**: Wenlong Yang, Weihang Yuan, Canran Jin, Jiahao Li, Henrik Turbell et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21505v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21505v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21505v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Real-world video denoising for communication systems must handle mixed degradations such as sensor noise, compression artifacts, and motion-induced inconsistency while remaining deployable under tight latency budgets. Purely deep models often achieve high accuracy but come with significant computational cost, whereas classical filters are robust and efficient but insufficiently adaptive. We present FilterFusion, a prior-guided multi-frame denoising framework that combines temporally-aware learned features with an interpretable bank of denoising branches. The model predicts a low-cost guidance signal and uses it to adaptively mix a compact set of denoising responses at the original resolution. The lightweight design uses only 109K parameters and explicitly injects temporal variance and spatial variance cues into the low-resolution controller. On CRVD (real sensor noise), it reaches 34.72 dB PSNR and 0.9330 SSIM with only 0.18 GFLOPs (network-only). Because the filter-bank operations are simple and regular (Gaussian blur, median, weighted sum), a straightforward compiled C implementation of the synthesis path achieves 17.9ms per frame (55.9 FPS) on a single CPU thread at 1080p---exceeding real-time and 122--252 times faster than lightweight CNN baselines that require over 2 seconds per frame. The results demonstrate a strong quality-efficiency-speed trade-off and a practical route to real-time denoising through low-resolution control and explicit filter-based synthesis.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Real-world video denoising for communication systems must handle mixed degradations such as sensor noise, compression artifacts, and motion-induced inconsistency while remaining deployable under tight latency budgets. Purely deep models often achieve high accuracy but come with significant computational cost, whereas classical filters are robust and efficient but insufficiently adaptive. We present FilterFusion, a prior-guided multi-frame denoising framework that combines temporally-aware learned features with an interpretable bank of denoising branches. The model predicts a low-cost guidance signal and uses it to adaptively mix a compact set of denoising responses at the original resolution. The lightweight design uses only 109K parameters and explicitly injects temporal variance and spatial variance cues into the low-resolution controller. Because the filter-bank operations are simple and regular (Gaussian blur, median, weighted sum), a straightforward compiled C implementation of the synthesis path achieves 17.9ms per frame (55.9 FPS) on a single CPU thread at 1080p---exceeding real-time and 122--252 times faster than lightweight CNN baselines that require over 2 seconds per frame. The results demonstrate a strong quality-efficiency-speed trade-off and a practical route to real-time denoising through low-resolution control and explicit filter-based synthesis.

</details>

## 16. Where, How, and When: Query-Guided Grounding for Video Understanding

**中文标题**: Where, How, and When: Query-Guided Grounding for Video Understanding

**作者**: Yogesh Kumar

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21422v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21422v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21422v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Understanding video content requires reasoning at multiple levels of granularity, from identifying objects to interpreting their interactions and localizing when specific actions occur. This thesis studies three query-guided grounding tasks that answer where key objects are (object-level grounding), how they interact (relationship-level grounding), and when specific moments occur (moment-level grounding). For object-level grounding, we present QDETRv, a query-guided extension of the Detection Transformer for one-shot localization of unseen objects in videos, which combines query-context fusion, a memory module for temporal consistency, and unsupervised pretraining on synthetic object trajectories. We further propose the Temporal Object-Aware Vision Transformer (TOViT) for few-shot video object detection, which builds on a language-aligned open-set vision encoder and propagates high-confidence object features across frames. For relationship-level grounding, we introduce few-shot referring relationships in videos: given a (subject, predicate, object) query and a few support videos sharing the predicate, the entities are localized by optimizing a T-partite random field using a meta-learned relation network with query-conditioned translational embeddings, Global Semantic Aggregation, Local Localization Aggregation, and belief-propagation-based message passing. For moment-level grounding, we propose the Moment Alignment Transformer (MATR) for video-to-video moment retrieval, which uses dual-stage Dynamic Time Warping for pre- and post-fusion alignment, learnable queries for boundary prediction, and self-supervised pretraining with temporal augmentations; MATR also extends to text-to-video moment retrieval with performance comparable to, or better than, the state of the art. These contributions advance query-guided video understanding across spatial, relational, and temporal grounding.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

This thesis studies three query-guided grounding tasks that answer where key objects are (object-level grounding), how they interact (relationship-level grounding), and when specific moments occur (moment-level grounding). For object-level grounding, we present QDETRv, a query-guided extension of the Detection Transformer for one-shot localization of unseen objects in videos, which combines query-context fusion, a memory module for temporal consistency, and unsupervised pretraining on synthetic object trajectories. We further propose the Temporal Object-Aware Vision Transformer (TOViT) for few-shot video object detection, which builds on a language-aligned open-set vision encoder and propagates high-confidence object features across frames. For relationship-level grounding, we introduce few-shot referring relationships in videos: given a (subject, predicate, object) query and a few support videos sharing the predicate, the entities are localized by optimizing a T-partite random field using a meta-learned relation network with query-conditioned translational embeddings, Global Semantic Aggregation, Local Localization Aggregation, and belief-propagation-based message passing. For moment-level grounding, we propose the Moment Alignment Transformer (MATR) for video-to-video moment retrieval, which uses dual-stage Dynamic Time Warping for pre- and post-fusion alignment, learnable queries for boundary prediction, and self-supervised pretraining with temporal augmentations; MATR also extends to text-to-video moment retrieval with performance comparable to, or better than, the state of the art. These contributions advance query-guided video understanding across spatial, relational, and temporal grounding.

</details>

## 17. Dusting the Phantom: Gaussian Allocation for Fading Dynamic Content

**中文标题**: Dusting the Phantom: Gaussian Allocation for Fading Dynamic Content

**作者**: Minhyeok Roh, Minseo Lee, Jun Young Jeong, Byeonghyeon Lee, Lucas Yunkyu Lee et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21302v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21302v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21302v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dynamic Gaussian representations have made high-quality, real-time volumetric video increasingly practical, yet challenging motion can still break their reconstruction: small fast-moving objects may disappear, and fine textures in highly dynamic regions are often lost. In particular, simply training longer or using more Gaussians may still fail to recover small, fast-moving objects. We argue that the key bottleneck is not representational capacity itself, but where and when Gaussian primitives are allocated. Existing allocation strategies rely on current Gaussian locations and often overlook the spatiotemporal destinations of relocated Gaussians. To address these limitations, we introduce two complementary strategies. First, Seed Injection identifies under-reconstructed dynamic regions using temporal appearance changes and reconstruction errors, and initializes new Gaussians at geometrically plausible 3D locations through multi-view observations, independent of existing Gaussian locations. Second, Temporal Biased Relocation uses time-resolved optimization signals to guide the spatiotemporal placement of relocated Gaussians. Both strategies maintain a fixed Gaussian budget without modifying the underlying representation. Experiments across multiple dynamic-scene datasets demonstrate improved reconstruction quality, particularly in highly dynamic regions, recovering small, rapidly moving objects and fine-grained appearance details.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Dynamic Gaussian representations have made high-quality, real-time volumetric video increasingly practical, yet challenging motion can still break their reconstruction: small fast-moving objects may disappear, and fine textures in highly dynamic regions are often lost. In particular, simply training longer or using more Gaussians may still fail to recover small, fast-moving objects. We argue that the key bottleneck is not representational capacity itself, but where and when Gaussian primitives are allocated. Existing allocation strategies rely on current Gaussian locations and often overlook the spatiotemporal destinations of relocated Gaussians. To address these limitations, we introduce two complementary strategies. First, Seed Injection identifies under-reconstructed dynamic regions using temporal appearance changes and reconstruction errors, and initializes new Gaussians at geometrically plausible 3D locations through multi-view observations, independent of existing Gaussian locations. Second, Temporal Biased Relocation uses time-resolved optimization signals to guide the spatiotemporal placement of relocated Gaussians. Both strategies maintain a fixed Gaussian budget without modifying the underlying representation. Experiments across multiple dynamic-scene datasets demonstrate improved reconstruction quality, particularly in highly dynamic regions, recovering small, rapidly moving objects and fine-grained appearance details.

</details>

## 18. ReState: Noise-Level-Aware State Correction for Training-Free Object Removal in Few-Step Diffusion Models

**中文标题**: ReState: Noise-Level-Aware State Correction for Training-Free Object Removal in Few-Step Diffusion Models

**作者**: Donghwan Lee, Sanghyun Jo, Sihyun Kim, Ziseok Lee, Kyungsu Kim

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21287v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21287v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21287v1)

**项目主页**: https://tdrlee.github.io/ReState

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Training-free object removal commonly redirects self-attention away from the target region, but on few-step distilled diffusion models this often leaves object-shaped residuals. We identify a temporal mismatch: a structured prediction forms inside the mask at the first forward, and the short trajectory offers few later chances to revise it, while attention rerouting acts mainly in later steps. Across four distilled backbones, magnitude-matched single-forward interventions show that background-directed state correction affects the final result more than attention rerouting. We therefore propose ReState, which corrects the masked clean prediction toward background evidence retrieved from the input using a noise-level-aware weight that vanishes at the final forward. ReState retains object-excluding attention as a complementary constraint and adds no denoiser evaluations. Across two paired benchmarks and two few-step backbones, ReState achieves the best masked PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS among training-free methods, improving PSNR by 3.17 dB on average over the strongest baseline. On OBER-Test, it also outperforms the 50-step attention-forcing reference on all three metrics at 6.7x lower latency. The same configuration transfers to two additional distilled backbones without retuning. Project page: https://tdrlee.github.io/ReState

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Training-free object removal commonly redirects self-attention away from the target region, but on few-step distilled diffusion models this often leaves object-shaped residuals. We identify a temporal mismatch: a structured prediction forms inside the mask at the first forward, and the short trajectory offers few later chances to revise it, while attention rerouting acts mainly in later steps. Across four distilled backbones, magnitude-matched single-forward interventions show that background-directed state correction affects the final result more than attention rerouting. We therefore propose ReState, which corrects the masked clean prediction toward background evidence retrieved from the input using a noise-level-aware weight that vanishes at the final forward. ReState retains object-excluding attention as a complementary constraint and adds no denoiser evaluations. Across two paired benchmarks and two few-step backbones, ReState achieves the best masked PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS among training-free methods, improving PSNR by 3.17 dB on average over the strongest baseline. On OBER-Test, it also outperforms the 50-step attention-forcing reference on all three metrics at 6.7x lower latency. The same configuration transfers to two additional distilled backbones without retuning. Project page: https://tdrlee.github.io/ReState

</details>

## 19. Monotone Piecewise-Linear Harmonization of Multitemporal Multispectral Surface Reflectance With Unsupervised Deep Multiview Pseudoinvariant Features Learning

**中文标题**: Monotone Piecewise-Linear Harmonization of Multitemporal Multispectral Surface Reflectance With Unsupervised Deep Multiview Pseudoinvariant Features Learning

**作者**: Lino Garda Denaro, Chia-Hsiang Lin

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21183v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21183v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21183v1)

**类别**: eess.IV, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Surface reflectance products derived from optical satellite may exhibit systematic inconsistencies across acquisition dates, because atmospheric correction is generally performed independently for each image. Such residual biases distort the temporal behavior of invariant ground targets and reduce the reliability of multitemporal surface reflectance analysis. This study proposes Deep Multitemporal Generalized Canonical Correlation Analysis (DeepMGCCA), an unsupervised deep multiview pseudoinvariant feature learning framework that integrates Deep Generalized Canonical Correlation Analysis (GCCA) with Multivariate Alteration Detection (MAD) to identify deep-pseudoinvariant features (DIFs) for the removal of surface reflectance biases across multitemporal satellite observations. Unlike conventional MAD-based approaches that rely on linear GCCA, the proposed framework replaces date-specific linear projections with a shared convolutional encoder. The framework learns deep invariant features (DIFs) directly from pixel-weighted canonical variates via iterative reweighting strategy, while a reconstruction regularizer preserves surface reflectance information and prevents degenerate collapse from suppressing genuine temporal variability. Experiments were conducted on four annual Landsat-8/9 and Sentinel-2 acquisitions across four regions of interest (ROIs) to evaluate four DIF-based harmonization transforms. A monotone piecewise-linear transform derived from DIFs quantiles consistently achieved the largest bias reduction, lowering the between-date spectral angle by up to 2.54 and RMSE by 0.015, and raising PSNR by up to 4.04~dB. These findings demonstrate the potential of deep invariant feature learning for multitemporal surface reflectance bias removal while highlighting the importance of preserving physically meaningful temporal and spectral information during nonlinear representation learning.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Surface reflectance products derived from optical satellite may exhibit systematic inconsistencies across acquisition dates, because atmospheric correction is generally performed independently for each image. Such residual biases distort the temporal behavior of invariant ground targets and reduce the reliability of multitemporal surface reflectance analysis. This study proposes Deep Multitemporal Generalized Canonical Correlation Analysis (DeepMGCCA), an unsupervised deep multiview pseudoinvariant feature learning framework that integrates Deep Generalized Canonical Correlation Analysis (GCCA) with Multivariate Alteration Detection (MAD) to identify deep-pseudoinvariant features (DIFs) for the removal of surface reflectance biases across multitemporal satellite observations. Unlike conventional MAD-based approaches that rely on linear GCCA, the proposed framework replaces date-specific linear projections with a shared convolutional encoder. The framework learns deep invariant features (DIFs) directly from pixel-weighted canonical variates via iterative reweighting strategy, while a reconstruction regularizer preserves surface reflectance information and prevents degenerate collapse from suppressing genuine temporal variability. Experiments were conducted on four annual Landsat-8/9 and Sentinel-2 acquisitions across four regions of interest (ROIs) to evaluate four DIF-based harmonization transforms. A monotone piecewise-linear transform derived from DIFs quantiles consistently achieved the largest bias reduction, lowering the between-date spectral angle by up to 2.54 and RMSE by 0.015, and raising PSNR by up to 4.04~dB. These findings demonstrate the potential of deep invariant feature learning for multitemporal surface reflectance bias removal while highlighting the importance of preserving physically meaningful temporal and spectral information during nonlinear representation learning.

</details>

## 20. Sparse Attention for Video Generation Acceleration via Growing Sparsity and Reduced Search

**中文标题**: Sparse Attention for Video Generation Acceleration via Growing Sparsity and Reduced Search

**作者**: Yun Dai, Yuyun Chen, Jiarui Wen, Huiping Zhuang, Cen Chen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21129v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21129v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21129v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) dominate video generation but suffer from high inference cost due to the quadratic complexity of self-attention. Sparse attention mitigates this by skipping unnecessary computation in the attention matrix, but existing methods apply a fixed sparsity ratio throughout denoising and struggle to accurately determine which computations to skip. To address these limitations, we analyze attention dynamics through attention entropy and mean attention distance, and find that attention becomes increasingly concentrated across denoising steps. We further observe that blocks selected early continue to capture a large fraction of the total attention weight at later steps. These findings suggest two acceleration opportunities: the sparsity ratio should grow as denoising progresses, and earlier search results can be reused to reduce the search space. Based on these findings, we propose Growing Sparsity and Reduced Search (GSRS), a training-free framework that retains more computation early and progressively reduces it later, while restricting subsequent searches to an early candidate set to reduce search overhead. GSRS adopts Top-p selection for adaptive sparsity control, supported by a precise evaluation scheme that computes exact attention weights within the FlashAttention computation at negligible overhead. Experiments on HunyuanVideo and Wan 2.1 validate that GSRS achieves 1.55$\times$--2.21$\times$ speedup while maintaining generation quality comparable to full attention.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) dominate video generation but suffer from high inference cost due to the quadratic complexity of self-attention. Sparse attention mitigates this by skipping unnecessary computation in the attention matrix, but existing methods apply a fixed sparsity ratio throughout denoising and struggle to accurately determine which computations to skip. To address these limitations, we analyze attention dynamics through attention entropy and mean attention distance, and find that attention becomes increasingly concentrated across denoising steps. We further observe that blocks selected early continue to capture a large fraction of the total attention weight at later steps. These findings suggest two acceleration opportunities: the sparsity ratio should grow as denoising progresses, and earlier search results can be reused to reduce the search space. Based on these findings, we propose Growing Sparsity and Reduced Search (GSRS), a training-free framework that retains more computation early and progressively reduces it later, while restricting subsequent searches to an early candidate set to reduce search overhead. GSRS adopts Top-p selection for adaptive sparsity control, supported by a precise evaluation scheme that computes exact attention weights within the FlashAttention computation at negligible overhead. Experiments on HunyuanVideo and Wan 2.1 validate that GSRS achieves 1.55$\times$--2.21$\times$ speedup while maintaining generation quality comparable to full attention.

</details>

## 21. ResStreamGS: Residual-Sensitive Dual-Stream Gaussian Splatting for Mobile Volumetric Video Streaming

**中文标题**: ResStreamGS: Residual-Sensitive Dual-Stream Gaussian Splatting for Mobile Volumetric Video Streaming

**作者**: Haoran Liu, Di Wu, Wang Luo, Hengyuan Na, Miao Hu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21121v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21121v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21121v1)

**类别**: cs.GR, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Real-time mobile volumetric video enables immersive free-viewpoint experiences, but delivering high-quality 3D content under constrained bandwidth and computation remains challenging. Recent 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) systems, such as V$^3$, enable efficient mobile streaming by encoding time-varying Gaussian attributes with commodity video codecs and hardware decoding. However, existing pipelines treat all Gaussians as frame-varying signals, which is poorly suited to full-scene content where large portions remain temporally stable. Our empirical study shows that frame-wise adaptation and video coding of all Gaussians cause quality degradation in complex full-scene content, particularly in temporally stable regions. To address this issue, we propose ResStreamGS, a residual-sensitive dual-stream framework that processes Gaussians according to their temporal behavior. ResStreamGS identifies residual-sensitive primitives through lightweight probe adaptation and reuses stable Gaussians within each Group of Pictures (GOP). Stable Gaussians are transmitted once per GOP, while only residual-sensitive Gaussians remain in the frame-wise hardware-decodable video stream. Experiments on complex full-scene volumetric videos show that ResStreamGS improves post-codec PSNR by 7.51--8.35~dB over a controlled V$^3$-style all-Gaussians baseline, reduces amortized payload by up to 56.5%, and achieves 30.883~FPS on a Qualcomm Snapdragon 778G platform.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Real-time mobile volumetric video enables immersive free-viewpoint experiences, but delivering high-quality 3D content under constrained bandwidth and computation remains challenging. Recent 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) systems, such as V$^3$, enable efficient mobile streaming by encoding time-varying Gaussian attributes with commodity video codecs and hardware decoding. However, existing pipelines treat all Gaussians as frame-varying signals, which is poorly suited to full-scene content where large portions remain temporally stable. Our empirical study shows that frame-wise adaptation and video coding of all Gaussians cause quality degradation in complex full-scene content, particularly in temporally stable regions. To address this issue, we propose ResStreamGS, a residual-sensitive dual-stream framework that processes Gaussians according to their temporal behavior. ResStreamGS identifies residual-sensitive primitives through lightweight probe adaptation and reuses stable Gaussians within each Group of Pictures (GOP). Stable Gaussians are transmitted once per GOP, while only residual-sensitive Gaussians remain in the frame-wise hardware-decodable video stream. Experiments on complex full-scene volumetric videos show that ResStreamGS improves post-codec PSNR by 7.51--8.35~dB over a controlled V$^3$-style all-Gaussians baseline, reduces amortized payload by up to 56.5%, and achieves 30.883~FPS on a Qualcomm Snapdragon 778G platform.

</details>

## 22. Twice Upon a Time: Complementary Representations for Time-Series Reasoning

**中文标题**: Twice Upon a Time: Complementary Representations for Time-Series Reasoning

**作者**: Hedi Zisling, Liran Nochumsohn, Ilan Naiman, Mihir Parmar, Omri Azencot

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22327v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22327v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22327v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CL

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Time Series Language Models (TSLMs) enable language-based understanding and reasoning over temporal data, yet a fundamental question remains: what information should their time-series representations preserve, and what variation should they factor out? Existing TSLMs typically expose the language model to a single representation, imposing a fixed inductive bias across downstream tasks. We instead introduce complementary representations as a design principle for time-series reasoning. Our model combines two visual views: line plots preserve absolute measurements and the observed coordinate frame, while normalized delay embeddings factor out positive amplitude rescaling and offsets while exposing temporal structure. Dedicated visual encoders integrate both views into a shared language model through visual tokens and multi-level feature fusion. Evaluation-time interventions verify that the two views exhibit distinct invariance profiles, while single-view ablations show that their combination is particularly beneficial beyond basic perception, with the large gains in prediction and reasoning. Built on Qwen3-VL-8B, our dual-view model improves overall TSRBench accuracy from 44.5% with line plots alone to 53.2%, substantially exceeding the strongest evaluated dedicated TSLM baseline at 36.7%. It further achieves 89.9% accuracy on TSExam and 86.8% numerical accuracy on multivariate ChatTS alignment. Together, these results demonstrate that complementary representations provide an effective design principle for general-purpose TSLMs, enabling models to exploit temporal structure without sacrificing access to the original measurement frame.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Time Series Language Models (TSLMs) enable language-based understanding and reasoning over temporal data, yet a fundamental question remains: what information should their time-series representations preserve, and what variation should they factor out? Existing TSLMs typically expose the language model to a single representation, imposing a fixed inductive bias across downstream tasks. We instead introduce complementary representations as a design principle for time-series reasoning. Our model combines two visual views: line plots preserve absolute measurements and the observed coordinate frame, while normalized delay embeddings factor out positive amplitude rescaling and offsets while exposing temporal structure. Dedicated visual encoders integrate both views into a shared language model through visual tokens and multi-level feature fusion. Evaluation-time interventions verify that the two views exhibit distinct invariance profiles, while single-view ablations show that their combination is particularly beneficial beyond basic perception, with the large gains in prediction and reasoning. Built on Qwen3-VL-8B, our dual-view model improves overall TSRBench accuracy from 44.5% with line plots alone to 53.2%, substantially exceeding the strongest evaluated dedicated TSLM baseline at 36.7%. It further achieves 89.9% accuracy on TSExam and 86.8% numerical accuracy on multivariate ChatTS alignment. Together, these results demonstrate that complementary representations provide an effective design principle for general-purpose TSLMs, enabling models to exploit temporal structure without sacrificing access to the original measurement frame.

</details>

## 23. Realtime-Bench: Can Frontier Models Solve Real-time Interactive Problems?

**中文标题**: Realtime-Bench: Can Frontier Models Solve Real-time Interactive Problems?

**作者**: Jingyu Tang, Xu Yang, Zhehao Zhang, Zheng Wang, Qi Cheng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22155v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22155v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22155v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Real-time interaction requires agents to perceive evolving environments and act under temporal constraints, yet existing evaluations of real-time streaming models focus on conversational quality and streaming perception, leaving effective action under strict temporal constraints untested. We introduce Realtime-Bench, a suite of carefully curated dynamic tasks where delayed actions directly affect success. Under Wall Clock, where environments advance according to actual inference latency, the best success rate reaches only 44% and the best Pass^3 is only 36%, demonstrating that real-time interaction remains challenging. To separate serving latency from models' real-time streaming capability, we compare Wall Clock with Paused Clock, which removes inference delays as an idealized reference, and introduce Token Clock, which advances simulated time according to generated token counts, testing temporal awareness independently of physical serving speed. We find that temporal costs limit success in evolving environments and that higher reasoning effort has mixed effects under time pressure, reflecting a trade-off between decision quality and timely execution. These findings suggest directions for improving real-time multimodal agents, including adaptive reasoning with temporal awareness and continuous perception and action.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Real-time interaction requires agents to perceive evolving environments and act under temporal constraints, yet existing evaluations of real-time streaming models focus on conversational quality and streaming perception, leaving effective action under strict temporal constraints untested. We introduce Realtime-Bench, a suite of carefully curated dynamic tasks where delayed actions directly affect success. Under Wall Clock, where environments advance according to actual inference latency, the best success rate reaches only 44% and the best Pass^3 is only 36%, demonstrating that real-time interaction remains challenging. To separate serving latency from models' real-time streaming capability, we compare Wall Clock with Paused Clock, which removes inference delays as an idealized reference, and introduce Token Clock, which advances simulated time according to generated token counts, testing temporal awareness independently of physical serving speed. We find that temporal costs limit success in evolving environments and that higher reasoning effort has mixed effects under time pressure, reflecting a trade-off between decision quality and timely execution. These findings suggest directions for improving real-time multimodal agents, including adaptive reasoning with temporal awareness and continuous perception and action.

</details>

## 24. Decoding Naturalistic Speech from fMRI with Temporal Modeling and Multimodal Alignment

**中文标题**: Decoding Naturalistic Speech from fMRI with Temporal Modeling and Multimodal Alignment

**作者**: Corentin Caris, Louis Jalouzot, Nona Rajabi, Géraud Ilinca, Ahmed Hassayoune et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22049v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22049v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22049v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reconstructing what a person perceives from their brain activity is a stringent test of whether neural recordings carry meaningful information. Naturalistic speech offers a rich testbed for this challenge: it unfolds over multiple timescales, but fMRI's noisy, temporally smeared measurements make this structure hard to recover. We introduce NOEMA, a temporal convolutional decoder that aligns windows of consecutive fMRI measurements with multimodal stimulus embeddings. These embeddings combine complementary text, audio, and speech representations of the same stimulus segment, enabling its retrieval directly from brain activity. On two naturalistic speech datasets, Cephalonauts One and LeBel, NOEMA retrieves the correct segment among 2,000 candidates within the top 10 for more than 42% of held-out queries. This substantially outperforms baseline decoders, an advantage that persists when the candidate pool is extended with thousands of distractors absent from training. Controlled ablations establish the contributions of temporal modeling and complementary stimulus representations.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Naturalistic speech offers a rich testbed for this challenge: it unfolds over multiple timescales, but fMRI's noisy, temporally smeared measurements make this structure hard to recover. We introduce NOEMA, a temporal convolutional decoder that aligns windows of consecutive fMRI measurements with multimodal stimulus embeddings. These embeddings combine complementary text, audio, and speech representations of the same stimulus segment, enabling its retrieval directly from brain activity. On two naturalistic speech datasets, Cephalonauts One and LeBel, NOEMA retrieves the correct segment among 2,000 candidates within the top 10 for more than 42% of held-out queries. This substantially outperforms baseline decoders, an advantage that persists when the candidate pool is extended with thousands of distractors absent from training. Controlled ablations establish the contributions of temporal modeling and complementary stimulus representations.

</details>
