## 25. GraphBEAT: Graph-based Bucketized Event Aggregation Over Time for Early Risk Stratification of Gastrointestinal Cancers

**中文标题**: GraphBEAT: Graph-based Bucketized Event Aggregation Over Time for Early Risk Stratification of Gastrointestinal Cancers

**作者**: Leo Y. Li-Han, Chris Varghese, Elizabeth B. Habermann, Mark J. Truty, William R. G. Perry et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22014v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22014v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22014v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI, eess.SP

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Longitudinal electronic health records (EHRs) contain complementary diagnostic and laboratory information about patient health. However, irregularity, sparsity, heterogeneity, and incompleteness in documentation complicate EHR modeling. To address the problem, we introduce GraphBEAT (Graph-based Bucketized Event Aggregation over Time), a temporal graph representation learning framework that groups clinical events into time buckets and transforms observed features into patient-specific, temporally directed graphs to enable early risk stratification for gastrointestinal cancers. Each node in an EHR graph encodes feature identity, observed value, and temporal position of clinical events. A context-aware, time-decay message-passing mechanism models transitions between successive buckets, followed by multi-head attention pooling to generate patient-level representations. GraphBEAT was trained and evaluated on multi-site longitudinal diagnosis codes and laboratory tests to predict pancreatic, liver, and intestinal cancer at 12-, 24-, and 36-month diagnostic lead times. In all experimental setups, GraphBEAT consistently outperformed baseline graph neural networks (GraphSAGE, GAT, and GCN) and sequence modeling approaches (Transformer and long short-term memory [LSTM]). GraphBEAT also required over 250x fewer floating-point operations per patient and over 20x fewer parameters than the Transformer and LSTM baselines. These results support GraphBEAT as an accurate and computationally efficient approach for cancer risk stratification from sparse, heterogeneous longitudinal health records.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Longitudinal electronic health records (EHRs) contain complementary diagnostic and laboratory information about patient health. However, irregularity, sparsity, heterogeneity, and incompleteness in documentation complicate EHR modeling. To address the problem, we introduce GraphBEAT (Graph-based Bucketized Event Aggregation over Time), a temporal graph representation learning framework that groups clinical events into time buckets and transforms observed features into patient-specific, temporally directed graphs to enable early risk stratification for gastrointestinal cancers. Each node in an EHR graph encodes feature identity, observed value, and temporal position of clinical events. A context-aware, time-decay message-passing mechanism models transitions between successive buckets, followed by multi-head attention pooling to generate patient-level representations. GraphBEAT was trained and evaluated on multi-site longitudinal diagnosis codes and laboratory tests to predict pancreatic, liver, and intestinal cancer at 12-, 24-, and 36-month diagnostic lead times. In all experimental setups, GraphBEAT consistently outperformed baseline graph neural networks (GraphSAGE, GAT, and GCN) and sequence modeling approaches (Transformer and long short-term memory [LSTM]). GraphBEAT also required over 250x fewer floating-point operations per patient and over 20x fewer parameters than the Transformer and LSTM baselines. These results support GraphBEAT as an accurate and computationally efficient approach for cancer risk stratification from sparse, heterogeneous longitudinal health records.

</details>

## 26. CoWorld Reasoner: Agentic Multimodal Reasoning with the Evolving Video World Model-Driven Harness

**中文标题**: CoWorld Reasoner: Agentic Multimodal Reasoning with the Evolving Video World Model-Driven Harness

**作者**: Dongjie Cheng, Ruifeng Yuan, Ran Wei, Ning Song, Yongqi Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21910v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21910v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21910v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

As Vision-Language Models (VLMs) advance, they are increasingly expected to perform spatiotemporal reasoning over motion, actions, and view changes beyond what the input images show. Video World Models (VWMs) provide a natural unified engine for visual imagination by generating task-relevant visual content as additional evidence. However, existing approaches typically invoke a VWM through fixed pipelines centered on a predefined form of visual imagination, such as novel-view synthesis. To support flexible visual imagination for diverse reasoning tasks, we introduce CoWorld Reasoner, an agentic framework that uses a VWM as a unified visual reasoning engine. Its Video World Model-Driven Harness exposes multiple visual operations through tools and uses skills to guide the VLM in deciding whether to imagine, selecting an operation, constructing a generation request, and interpreting the resulting visual evidence. We further introduce VLM-VWM Harness Co-Evolution, which jointly revises tools, skills, and execution code based on execution feedback while keeping both model weights fixed. Across seven benchmarks spanning spatial intelligence, temporal and causal reasoning, and perceptual reasoning, CoWorld Reasoner outperforms direct reasoning with the same VLM. The strongest variant improves on all seven benchmarks, including relative accuracy gains of 11.8% on MMSI and 13.0% on ERQA, and outperforms all five evolving baselines using the same backbone across five benchmarks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

As Vision-Language Models (VLMs) advance, they are increasingly expected to perform spatiotemporal reasoning over motion, actions, and view changes beyond what the input images show. Video World Models (VWMs) provide a natural unified engine for visual imagination by generating task-relevant visual content as additional evidence. However, existing approaches typically invoke a VWM through fixed pipelines centered on a predefined form of visual imagination, such as novel-view synthesis. To support flexible visual imagination for diverse reasoning tasks, we introduce CoWorld Reasoner, an agentic framework that uses a VWM as a unified visual reasoning engine. Its Video World Model-Driven Harness exposes multiple visual operations through tools and uses skills to guide the VLM in deciding whether to imagine, selecting an operation, constructing a generation request, and interpreting the resulting visual evidence. We further introduce VLM-VWM Harness Co-Evolution, which jointly revises tools, skills, and execution code based on execution feedback while keeping both model weights fixed. Across seven benchmarks spanning spatial intelligence, temporal and causal reasoning, and perceptual reasoning, CoWorld Reasoner outperforms direct reasoning with the same VLM. The strongest variant improves on all seven benchmarks, including relative accuracy gains of 11.8% on MMSI and 13.0% on ERQA, and outperforms all five evolving baselines using the same backbone across five benchmarks.

</details>

## 27. Retrieved but Not Read: Understanding Why Search-Augmented LLMs Fail to Use Available Evidence

**中文标题**: Retrieved but Not Read: Understanding Why Search-Augmented LLMs Fail to Use Available Evidence

**作者**: Owen Walters, Lihu Chen, Francesca Toni

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21731v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21731v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21731v1)

**类别**: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.IR

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Web search allows LLMs to access up-to-date information, but it remains unclear why they sometimes produce outdated answers even when relevant evidence is available. To study this problem, we construct temporal facts whose values changed after the models' training cutoffs. We evaluate one proprietary model under deployed web search and six open-weight model configurations under controlled retrieval. We find that models often answer from search snippets without opening the source pages, which is associated with higher rates of outdated answers and unfaithful citations. Our controlled experiments and layer-wise analysis reveal a gap between the availability of evidence and its effective use. Even when retrieved evidence influences internal predictions, models may still favour outdated information in their final answers. We hope these findings will help advance research on the reliable use of retrieved evidence in search-augmented LLMs.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Web search allows LLMs to access up-to-date information, but it remains unclear why they sometimes produce outdated answers even when relevant evidence is available. To study this problem, we construct temporal facts whose values changed after the models' training cutoffs. We evaluate one proprietary model under deployed web search and six open-weight model configurations under controlled retrieval. We find that models often answer from search snippets without opening the source pages, which is associated with higher rates of outdated answers and unfaithful citations. Our controlled experiments and layer-wise analysis reveal a gap between the availability of evidence and its effective use. Even when retrieved evidence influences internal predictions, models may still favour outdated information in their final answers. We hope these findings will help advance research on the reliable use of retrieved evidence in search-augmented LLMs.

</details>

## 28. LTM: Latent Transition Based In-Context Memory for Vision-Language-Action Models

**中文标题**: LTM: Latent Transition Based In-Context Memory for Vision-Language-Action Models

**作者**: Qixin Sun, WenLong Gao, Xiansheng Chen, Zhongyuan Wang, Si Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21362v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21362v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21362v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robotic manipulation often requires remembering interactions that are no longer visible. We introduce Latent Transition Memory (LTM), which stores history as compact latent transitions between consecutive observations. Instantiated on $π_{0.5}$ as LTM-$π$, our method uses a frozen latent action encoder, a lightweight adapter, and causal KV caching for incremental inference without memory-specific semantic annotations or external reasoning-model calls. With DreamDojo transitions, LTM-$π$ achieves 58.54% success on RoboMME and 80.5% on LIBERO-Mem, performing best among baselines without external reasoning models or memory-specific annotations. Ablations support the contribution of transition content beyond temporal cues and the efficiency of delayed memory admission. Scaling RoboMME demonstrations tenfold improves success from 57.67% to 87.50%, while a Cup Swap diagnostic highlights the role of process supervision in temporal composition. Under a 200-token memory budget, success remains 84.00%, versus 87.50% without compression. Finally, real-robot counting success improves from 11.25% to 82.50%. Together, these results demonstrate the effectiveness, scalability, and efficiency of latent transition memory, highlighting its capacity for complex temporal information, bounded-context operation, and real-world robotic control.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Robotic manipulation often requires remembering interactions that are no longer visible. We introduce Latent Transition Memory (LTM), which stores history as compact latent transitions between consecutive observations. Instantiated on $π_{0.5}$ as LTM-$π$, our method uses a frozen latent action encoder, a lightweight adapter, and causal KV caching for incremental inference without memory-specific semantic annotations or external reasoning-model calls. With DreamDojo transitions, LTM-$π$ achieves 58.54% success on RoboMME and 80.5% on LIBERO-Mem, performing best among baselines without external reasoning models or memory-specific annotations. Ablations support the contribution of transition content beyond temporal cues and the efficiency of delayed memory admission. Scaling RoboMME demonstrations tenfold improves success from 57.67% to 87.50%, while a Cup Swap diagnostic highlights the role of process supervision in temporal composition. Under a 200-token memory budget, success remains 84.00%, versus 87.50% without compression. Finally, real-robot counting success improves from 11.25% to 82.50%. Together, these results demonstrate the effectiveness, scalability, and efficiency of latent transition memory, highlighting its capacity for complex temporal information, bounded-context operation, and real-world robotic control.

</details>

## 29. DST-Net: Decomposed Spatio-Temporal Network for Time Series Forecasting

**中文标题**: DST-Net: Decomposed Spatio-Temporal Network for Time Series Forecasting

**作者**: Yaohua Zha, Hai Wu, Shu-Tao Xia

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21123v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21123v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21123v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Series decomposition has emerged as a widely acknowledged paradigm in deep time series forecasting models. However, existing approaches predominantly employ a homogeneous modeling strategy for all subsequences, thereby overlooking the distinct inductive biases inherent in different components. We identify that the trend component exhibits dominant spatial coupling across channels but carries significant temporal redundancy, whereas the seasonal component is characterized by local temporal evolution but remains susceptible to attention distraction. To address this, we propose a novel disentangled architecture that tailors specific modeling strategies to align with the intrinsic properties of each component. For the trend branch, we design a Latent Graph Block, employing latent graph modeling to capture global topological dependencies and system-wide co-movements. Conversely, for the seasonal branch, we introduce a Gaussian Multi-scale Attention mechanism to mitigate attention distraction. By injecting learnable Gaussian priors, we impose structural constraints that compel the model to focus on multi-granular local contexts, effectively filtering out global noise and accurately capturing local evolutionary phases. Extensive experiments on multiple real-world benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of our proposed model.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Series decomposition has emerged as a widely acknowledged paradigm in deep time series forecasting models. However, existing approaches predominantly employ a homogeneous modeling strategy for all subsequences, thereby overlooking the distinct inductive biases inherent in different components. We identify that the trend component exhibits dominant spatial coupling across channels but carries significant temporal redundancy, whereas the seasonal component is characterized by local temporal evolution but remains susceptible to attention distraction. To address this, we propose a novel disentangled architecture that tailors specific modeling strategies to align with the intrinsic properties of each component. For the trend branch, we design a Latent Graph Block, employing latent graph modeling to capture global topological dependencies and system-wide co-movements. Conversely, for the seasonal branch, we introduce a Gaussian Multi-scale Attention mechanism to mitigate attention distraction. By injecting learnable Gaussian priors, we impose structural constraints that compel the model to focus on multi-granular local contexts, effectively filtering out global noise and accurately capturing local evolutionary phases. Extensive experiments on multiple real-world benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of our proposed model.

</details>

## 30. Tokenized Joint Source-Network Coding for Loss-Resilient Video Streaming

**中文标题**: Tokenized Joint Source-Network Coding for Loss-Resilient Video Streaming

**作者**: Ke Yang, Sixian Wang, Kailin Tan, Guo Lu, Jincheng Dai et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21166v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21166v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21166v1)

**类别**: eess.IV, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

For decades, conventional video streaming systems have relied on packet-level forward error correction (FEC) codes and retransmissions to ensure reliable video delivery. However, in real-time communications, FEC is inherently inefficient under unknown and time-varying packet-loss conditions, while retransmission can incur excessive latency and fail to meet stringent playout deadlines. Emerging deep joint source-channel coding (DeepJSCC) provides an alternative by learning channel-noise-resilient representations, yet its tight coupling with the physical layer makes it difficult to integrate into today's layered network architectures and protocols. In this paper, we propose TokJSNC, a simple-but-effective tokenized joint source-network coding paradigm for packet-loss-resilient video streaming. Rather than first compressing video into a compact bitstream and then protecting it with packet-level FEC, TokJSNC directly encodes video into variable-length discrete token-stream, whose transmission rate is jointly adapted to video content complexity and network conditions. By exploiting the spatiotemporal correlations among learned video tokens, TokJSNC enables robust video reconstruction over a wide range of packet-loss rates without explicit FEC or retransmissions. Consequently, TokJSNC brings the JSCC-like graceful degradation property to the network layer while remaining compatible with existing network architectures and transport protocols. Experimental results show that TokJSNC maintains an average playback frame rate above 22 fps even at a 70% packet-loss rate, whereas the best-performing baseline drops below 12 fps. Overall, TokJSNC establishes a practical pathway for repurposing modern generative video tokenizers for loss-resilient streaming, while providing a fundamental framework for video token communications.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

For decades, conventional video streaming systems have relied on packet-level forward error correction (FEC) codes and retransmissions to ensure reliable video delivery. However, in real-time communications, FEC is inherently inefficient under unknown and time-varying packet-loss conditions, while retransmission can incur excessive latency and fail to meet stringent playout deadlines. Emerging deep joint source-channel coding (DeepJSCC) provides an alternative by learning channel-noise-resilient representations, yet its tight coupling with the physical layer makes it difficult to integrate into today's layered network architectures and protocols. In this paper, we propose TokJSNC, a simple-but-effective tokenized joint source-network coding paradigm for packet-loss-resilient video streaming. Rather than first compressing video into a compact bitstream and then protecting it with packet-level FEC, TokJSNC directly encodes video into variable-length discrete token-stream, whose transmission rate is jointly adapted to video content complexity and network conditions. By exploiting the spatiotemporal correlations among learned video tokens, TokJSNC enables robust video reconstruction over a wide range of packet-loss rates without explicit FEC or retransmissions. Consequently, TokJSNC brings the JSCC-like graceful degradation property to the network layer while remaining compatible with existing network architectures and transport protocols. Experimental results show that TokJSNC maintains an average playback frame rate above 22 fps even at a 70% packet-loss rate, whereas the best-performing baseline drops below 12 fps. Overall, TokJSNC establishes a practical pathway for repurposing modern generative video tokenizers for loss-resilient streaming, while providing a fundamental framework for video token communications.

</details>

---

## 31. Mem4Act: Learning Effective Latent Memory for Long-Horizon Robotic Manipulation

**中文标题**: Mem4Act: Learning Effective Latent Memory for Long-Horizon Robotic Manipulation

**作者**: Yun Zhang, Zhiyu Huang, Tianhui Cai, Chen Tang, Wei Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22323v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22323v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22323v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Long-horizon robotic manipulation fundamentally requires memory: agents must retain task-relevant information from past observations and interactions and use it to guide future actions. Existing approaches typically preserve raw historical observations, compressed visual tokens, or language summaries, but these representations either expose the action policy to increasingly long histories or risk discarding task-relevant temporal, semantic, and perceptual information. We introduce Mem4Act, a framework for learning effective latent memory as a fixed-size representation explicitly optimized for long-horizon robotic control. Mem4Act addresses three coupled challenges: how to efficiently cover long histories, what information memory should retain, and how to ensure the policy actually relies on memory. To this end, it organizes history via multi-granularity temporal aggregation, preserves complementary semantic and perceptual information through question-answering supervision and key-frame DINO feature reconstruction, and employs memory-first two-stage training to encourage greater use of memory by the action policy. The resulting memory captures both high-level task state and fine-grained perceptual evidence while providing a bounded interface to the action policy. Across RoboMME, RMBench, and four real-world manipulation tasks, Mem4Act achieves state-of-the-art performance on memory-dependent control, with success rates of 49.88%, 61.0%, and 81.5%, respectively. Extensive ablations further demonstrate the importance of temporal aggregation, complementary memory supervision, and memory-first training.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Long-horizon robotic manipulation fundamentally requires memory: agents must retain task-relevant information from past observations and interactions and use it to guide future actions. Existing approaches typically preserve raw historical observations, compressed visual tokens, or language summaries, but these representations either expose the action policy to increasingly long histories or risk discarding task-relevant temporal, semantic, and perceptual information. We introduce Mem4Act, a framework for learning effective latent memory as a fixed-size representation explicitly optimized for long-horizon robotic control. Mem4Act addresses three coupled challenges: how to efficiently cover long histories, what information memory should retain, and how to ensure the policy actually relies on memory. To this end, it organizes history via multi-granularity temporal aggregation, preserves complementary semantic and perceptual information through question-answering supervision and key-frame DINO feature reconstruction, and employs memory-first two-stage training to encourage greater use of memory by the action policy. The resulting memory captures both high-level task state and fine-grained perceptual evidence while providing a bounded interface to the action policy. Across RoboMME, RMBench, and four real-world manipulation tasks, Mem4Act achieves state-of-the-art performance on memory-dependent control, with success rates of 49.88%, 61.0%, and 81.5%, respectively. Extensive ablations further demonstrate the importance of temporal aggregation, complementary memory supervision, and memory-first training.

</details>

## 32. FastVLA: Task-Conditioned and Temporally Adaptive Visual Token Compression for Efficient Vision-Language-Action Models

**中文标题**: FastVLA: Task-Conditioned and Temporally Adaptive Visual Token Compression for Efficient Vision-Language-Action Models

**作者**: Jiuyi Xu, Chengyuan Qian, Zhengzhong Tu, Yang Sui, Yangming Shi

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22146v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22146v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22146v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/jiuyixu25/FastVLA.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models present a promising paradigm for general-purpose robot manipulation, but closed-loop deployment is often limited by inference efficiency: at each timestep, high-resolution, often multi-view images are encoded into hundreds of visual tokens that dominate transformer computation and latency. Token compression via attention is a natural solution but is challenging for VLA control because (1) decision-critical tokens are determined by the task, not generic visual saliency, and (2) the token budget varies substantially across steps, so static metrics and budgets waste computation or discard control-critical tokens. We introduce FastVLA, a training-free, task-aware, and temporally adaptive visual token compression framework. At each step, FastVLA scores token importance via task-to-vision attention and adaptively sets the compression rate from the importance-distribution uniformity, measured by an inverse-Simpson metric with online sliding-window calibration. We evaluate FastVLA on OpenVLA, OpenVLA-OFT, and CogACT across LIBERO and SIMPLER, and on a physical Franka Research 3 robot. In simulation, it reduces LLM-backbone FLOPs by 35-59\% and action latency by 9-32\% (a 1.09$\times$-1.46$\times$ speedup) while maintaining task success, achieving a comparable or better efficiency-performance trade-off than existing plug-and-play token compression/pruning/reuse baselines. On the real robot, it cuts end-to-end latency by 20\% (1.25$\times$ speedup) at comparable success. Code is available at https://github.com/jiuyixu25/FastVLA.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models present a promising paradigm for general-purpose robot manipulation, but closed-loop deployment is often limited by inference efficiency: at each timestep, high-resolution, often multi-view images are encoded into hundreds of visual tokens that dominate transformer computation and latency. Token compression via attention is a natural solution but is challenging for VLA control because (1) decision-critical tokens are determined by the task, not generic visual saliency, and (2) the token budget varies substantially across steps, so static metrics and budgets waste computation or discard control-critical tokens. We introduce FastVLA, a training-free, task-aware, and temporally adaptive visual token compression framework. At each step, FastVLA scores token importance via task-to-vision attention and adaptively sets the compression rate from the importance-distribution uniformity, measured by an inverse-Simpson metric with online sliding-window calibration. We evaluate FastVLA on OpenVLA, OpenVLA-OFT, and CogACT across LIBERO and SIMPLER, and on a physical Franka Research 3 robot. In simulation, it reduces LLM-backbone FLOPs by 35-59\% and action latency by 9-32\% (a 1.09$\times$-1.46$\times$ speedup) while maintaining task success, achieving a comparable or better efficiency-performance trade-off than existing plug-and-play token compression/pruning/reuse baselines. On the real robot, it cuts end-to-end latency by 20\% (1.25$\times$ speedup) at comparable success. Code is available at https://github.com/jiuyixu25/FastVLA.

</details>

## 33. VeriVLA: Adaptive Test-Time Verification for Flow-Based VLAs with Latent World Models

**中文标题**: VeriVLA: Adaptive Test-Time Verification for Flow-Based VLAs with Latent World Models

**作者**: Xueli Sun, Yuxiang Lu, Yantong Liu, Xianzhe Fan, Xi Chen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21985v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21985v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21985v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Flow-based Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies are inherently stochastic: different noise seeds can produce action chunks of varying quality from the same observation. At test time, however, the policy typically commits to a single sample without an explicit estimate of its quality. In fine-grained manipulation, executing a suboptimal action can irreversibly disrupt task progress and lead to failure. To exploit policy stochasticity, we introduce a training-free two-axis trigger that uses two initial samples to measure within-chunk temporal variation and cross-sample variance, detecting the critical states. Once triggered, a JEPA-based latent world model ranks candidate action chunks by predicting action-conditioned future latent states and the corresponding changes in task progress. By operating in latent space, the verifier avoids pixel reconstruction and enables efficient and more accurate ranking. Across three real-world tasks, $\textit{i.e.,}$ pen insertion, pipetting, and apple placement), our method raises the average completion score from 50.8% to 65.5%, while adding 326 ms for sampling and verification per triggered decision on a single RTX 4090. Consistent gains are also observed in simulation, where the average success rate rises from 66.4% to 73.6% in the clean setting of RoboTwin 2.0, from 62.8% to 69.6% in its randomized setting, and from 96.9% to 97.6% on LIBERO.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Flow-based Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies are inherently stochastic: different noise seeds can produce action chunks of varying quality from the same observation. At test time, however, the policy typically commits to a single sample without an explicit estimate of its quality. In fine-grained manipulation, executing a suboptimal action can irreversibly disrupt task progress and lead to failure. To exploit policy stochasticity, we introduce a training-free two-axis trigger that uses two initial samples to measure within-chunk temporal variation and cross-sample variance, detecting the critical states. Once triggered, a JEPA-based latent world model ranks candidate action chunks by predicting action-conditioned future latent states and the corresponding changes in task progress. By operating in latent space, the verifier avoids pixel reconstruction and enables efficient and more accurate ranking. Across three real-world tasks, $\textit{i.e.,}$ pen insertion, pipetting, and apple placement), our method raises the average completion score from 50.8% to 65.5%, while adding 326 ms for sampling and verification per triggered decision on a single RTX 4090. Consistent gains are also observed in simulation, where the average success rate rises from 66.4% to 73.6% in the clean setting of RoboTwin 2.0, from 62.8% to 69.6% in its randomized setting, and from 96.9% to 97.6% on LIBERO.

</details>

## 34. TS-WAM: Temporal Latent Actions and World-Action Coupling for Vision-Language Robot Control

**中文标题**: TS-WAM: Temporal Latent Actions and World-Action Coupling for Vision-Language Robot Control

**作者**: Menghao Yin, Jiaju Yin, Xiyue Dong, Lixin Xu, Zhenhui Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21922v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21922v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21922v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Many vision-language-action policies rely on action-labeled robot demonstrations and lack an explicit mechanism for predicting future visual states. These limitations hinder learning from action-free videos and can encourage overfitting to demonstration conditions, weakening generalization and robustness. We present TS-WAM, a lightweight Teacher-Student World-Action Model that addresses these challenges through self-supervised temporal learning and explicit future prediction. Its vlm-jepa framework learns from heterogeneous data, including robot demonstrations and action-free human videos, using an EMA target branch without requiring a separately trained external teacher. By coupling vlm-jepa with delta-lwm, TS-WAM learns to predict latent actions from the current observation and instruction and uses them to predict future visual features that guide action generation at inference. A unified end-to-end training architecture jointly optimizes temporal representation learning, visual dynamics, and robot control without a separate latent-action or world-model pretraining stage. The EMA target branch is removed at inference, keeping the deployed policy lightweight. Experiments on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and SimplerEnv, together with real-world manipulation experiments on Franka Research 3 and RevoTron, demonstrate that TS-WAM achieves strong manipulation performance, generalization, and robustness.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Many vision-language-action policies rely on action-labeled robot demonstrations and lack an explicit mechanism for predicting future visual states. These limitations hinder learning from action-free videos and can encourage overfitting to demonstration conditions, weakening generalization and robustness. By coupling vlm-jepa with delta-lwm, TS-WAM learns to predict latent actions from the current observation and instruction and uses them to predict future visual features that guide action generation at inference. A unified end-to-end training architecture jointly optimizes temporal representation learning, visual dynamics, and robot control without a separate latent-action or world-model pretraining stage. The EMA target branch is removed at inference, keeping the deployed policy lightweight. Experiments on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and SimplerEnv, together with real-world manipulation experiments on Franka Research 3 and RevoTron, demonstrate that TS-WAM achieves strong manipulation performance, generalization, and robustness.

</details>

## 35. TRACE: Typed Reference-Aware Conversational Editing for Humanoid Motion Diffusion

**中文标题**: TRACE: Typed Reference-Aware Conversational Editing for Humanoid Motion Diffusion

**作者**: Zejian Kang, Yifu Luo, Boyang Shen, Jingxuan Li, Zekai Chen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21515v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21515v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21515v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Language-conditioned humanoid motion generation enables users to create and refine robot movements through natural language. The central challenge is to connect an underspecified request to concrete motion structure that can subsequently be revised relative to an accepted reference. We present TRACE, a generation and editing framework for the Unitree G1 humanoid robot built around typed, measurable kinematic quantities. Our insight is to ground generation conditions in paired training motions and editing goals in the current motion. A language model learns to predict support, root, and hand events from motion-derived supervision; their time-aligned encodings complement the original text in a diffusion generator. Gravity-referenced states and trajectory--contact objectives improve kinematic consistency. For editing, typed requests define body--time scopes and source-relative goals. A separate reference-conditioned diffusion editor uses these goals and revised events to realize changes, while sampling-time projection preserves protected state coordinates. Quantitative evaluation and human assessment show improved semantic alignment and motion quality over the compared methods. Controlled ablations support the value of motion-grounded conditioning and kinematic consistency, while multi-turn editing evaluations demonstrate more reliable execution of successive relative requests under shared preservation constraints. Together, these results support typed kinematic structure as a common basis for motion generation and reference-aware revision.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Language-conditioned humanoid motion generation enables users to create and refine robot movements through natural language. The central challenge is to connect an underspecified request to concrete motion structure that can subsequently be revised relative to an accepted reference. We present TRACE, a generation and editing framework for the Unitree G1 humanoid robot built around typed, measurable kinematic quantities. Our insight is to ground generation conditions in paired training motions and editing goals in the current motion. A language model learns to predict support, root, and hand events from motion-derived supervision; their time-aligned encodings complement the original text in a diffusion generator. Gravity-referenced states and trajectory--contact objectives improve kinematic consistency. For editing, typed requests define body--time scopes and source-relative goals. A separate reference-conditioned diffusion editor uses these goals and revised events to realize changes, while sampling-time projection preserves protected state coordinates. Quantitative evaluation and human assessment show improved semantic alignment and motion quality over the compared methods. Controlled ablations support the value of motion-grounded conditioning and kinematic consistency, while multi-turn editing evaluations demonstrate more reliable execution of successive relative requests under shared preservation constraints. Together, these results support typed kinematic structure as a common basis for motion generation and reference-aware revision.

</details>

## 36. MR-MPCC: Model Predictive Contouring Control for Robotic Manipulator Tracking under Moving Reference Frames

**中文标题**: MR-MPCC: Model Predictive Contouring Control for Robotic Manipulator Tracking under Moving Reference Frames

**作者**: Chonghao Cheng, Tiancheng Li, Jonathan Woolfrey, Liang Zhao, Shoudong Huang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21292v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21292v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21292v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Maintaining small path-tracking errors under moving reference frames is essential for robotic manipulation tasks with tight execution tolerances, such as machining and surgical automation. Conventional time-indexed controllers, including tracking Model Predictive Control (MPC), rely on a fixed temporal correspondence with the reference trajectory and may therefore accumulate path-relative error under abrupt reference-frame motion or timing mismatch. This paper presents MR-MPCC, a Model Predictive Contouring Control (MPCC) framework for velocity-controlled robotic manipulators with 6-DoF end-effector pose regulation under Moving-Reference disturbances. A hierarchical control architecture is adopted, in which an upper-level MPCC optimizes path progress while penalizing contouring and orientation errors, and a constrained resolved motion rate control (RMRC) layer maps the commanded Cartesian twist to feasible joint velocities under joint-level and safety constraints. MR-MPCC is implemented in a ROS2--MuJoCo pipeline and validated on a UR5e platform under moving-reference disturbances, where the path frame is perturbed and tracked by electromagnetic sensors. Compared with PI control and time-indexed tracking MPC under matched disturbance, MR-MPCC achieves lower geometric path-following error and smaller deviations in the moving path frame, demonstrating improved path-following performance under reference-frame motion.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Maintaining small path-tracking errors under moving reference frames is essential for robotic manipulation tasks with tight execution tolerances, such as machining and surgical automation. Conventional time-indexed controllers, including tracking Model Predictive Control (MPC), rely on a fixed temporal correspondence with the reference trajectory and may therefore accumulate path-relative error under abrupt reference-frame motion or timing mismatch. This paper presents MR-MPCC, a Model Predictive Contouring Control (MPCC) framework for velocity-controlled robotic manipulators with 6-DoF end-effector pose regulation under Moving-Reference disturbances. A hierarchical control architecture is adopted, in which an upper-level MPCC optimizes path progress while penalizing contouring and orientation errors, and a constrained resolved motion rate control (RMRC) layer maps the commanded Cartesian twist to feasible joint velocities under joint-level and safety constraints. MR-MPCC is implemented in a ROS2--MuJoCo pipeline and validated on a UR5e platform under moving-reference disturbances, where the path frame is perturbed and tracked by electromagnetic sensors. Compared with PI control and time-indexed tracking MPC under matched disturbance, MR-MPCC achieves lower geometric path-following error and smaller deviations in the moving path frame, demonstrating improved path-following performance under reference-frame motion.

</details>

## 37. Learning Rational Strategies for Temporal Objectives in Multi-Agent Systems

**中文标题**: Learning Rational Strategies for Temporal Objectives in Multi-Agent Systems

**作者**: Omar Adalat, Edwin Hamel-De le Court, Francesco Belardinelli

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22287v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22287v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22287v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.LO, cs.MA

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We study rational synthesis for stochastic multi-agent systems with unknown dynamics, where each agent is equipped with its own probabilistic temporal objective specified using Probabilistic Linear Temporal Logic on Finite Traces ($\mathsf{PLTL}_f}$). We impose solution concepts (e.g., Nash equilibrium, subgame-perfect equilibrium) as constraints on the joint strategy profile, using payoffs derived from each agent's probability of satisfying its temporal objective. In particular, we formulate per-agent rational synthesis as finding a strategy profile -- if one exists -- such that (i) the designated agent's probabilistic temporal constraint is satisfied, and (ii) the profile represents a stable solution under a chosen solution concept. We give conditional convergence guarantees for tabular Nash-Q learning, certify satisfaction with simultaneous finite-sample confidence bounds, and assess strategic stability with approximate best-response checking. Our approach operates in a stochastic \emph{Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning} (MARL) environment, where model-free learning algorithms attempt to attain extremal satisfaction probabilities for temporal objectives automatically compiled into reward automata. Our approach is empirically validated using 8 general-sum concurrent stochastic games.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We study rational synthesis for stochastic multi-agent systems with unknown dynamics, where each agent is equipped with its own probabilistic temporal objective specified using Probabilistic Linear Temporal Logic on Finite Traces ($\mathsf{PLTL}_f}$). We impose solution concepts (e.g., Nash equilibrium, subgame-perfect equilibrium) as constraints on the joint strategy profile, using payoffs derived from each agent's probability of satisfying its temporal objective. In particular, we formulate per-agent rational synthesis as finding a strategy profile -- if one exists -- such that (i) the designated agent's probabilistic temporal constraint is satisfied, and (ii) the profile represents a stable solution under a chosen solution concept. We give conditional convergence guarantees for tabular Nash-Q learning, certify satisfaction with simultaneous finite-sample confidence bounds, and assess strategic stability with approximate best-response checking. Our approach operates in a stochastic \emph{Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning} (MARL) environment, where model-free learning algorithms attempt to attain extremal satisfaction probabilities for temporal objectives automatically compiled into reward automata. Our approach is empirically validated using 8 general-sum concurrent stochastic games.

</details>

## 38. Beyond Fixed Acquisition Functions: Temporal Difference Active Learning for Perturbation Discovery

**中文标题**: Beyond Fixed Acquisition Functions: Temporal Difference Active Learning for Perturbation Discovery

**作者**: Xudong Zhang, Jianzhe Li, Yitong Tang, Peijie Zhou

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.22122v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22122v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22122v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Genetic and drug perturbation screens are important tools for elucidating disease mechanisms and validating therapeutic targets, but experimental costs limit the number of candidates that can be tested. Existing perturbation screening methods typically update the surrogate model with feedback while keeping the acquisition function fixed, limiting their ability to adapt selection to different response landscapes. Experimental feedback reveals current hits and informs subsequent selection by updating candidate predictions, providing a training signal for learning how to select experiments. We formulate budgeted perturbation discovery as a sequential decision problem and propose Temporal Difference Active Learning (TDAL), which learns an acquisition function online through temporal difference learning from a single screening trajectory, accounting for current hits and future discoveries. TDAL uses the Hellinger distance between hit probabilities for unmeasured candidates before and after an experiment to weight successor value, and reuses measured outcomes through leave-batch-out (LBO) to construct virtual transitions and alleviate training data scarcity. Across eight public screens spanning single, double, and triple perturbations under varied experimental configurations, TDAL achieves an average rank of 1.63 among eleven methods and ranks first on four tasks, demonstrating its adaptability across response landscapes.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Genetic and drug perturbation screens are important tools for elucidating disease mechanisms and validating therapeutic targets, but experimental costs limit the number of candidates that can be tested. Existing perturbation screening methods typically update the surrogate model with feedback while keeping the acquisition function fixed, limiting their ability to adapt selection to different response landscapes. Experimental feedback reveals current hits and informs subsequent selection by updating candidate predictions, providing a training signal for learning how to select experiments. We formulate budgeted perturbation discovery as a sequential decision problem and propose Temporal Difference Active Learning (TDAL), which learns an acquisition function online through temporal difference learning from a single screening trajectory, accounting for current hits and future discoveries. TDAL uses the Hellinger distance between hit probabilities for unmeasured candidates before and after an experiment to weight successor value, and reuses measured outcomes through leave-batch-out (LBO) to construct virtual transitions and alleviate training data scarcity. </details>

## 39. ChronoSheaf: Joint Spatio-Temporal Spectral Filtering on Cellular Sheaves

**中文标题**: ChronoSheaf: Joint Spatio-Temporal Spectral Filtering on Cellular Sheaves

**作者**: Abeer Mostafa, Zahra Ahmadi

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21958v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21958v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21958v1)

**项目主页**: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/ChronoSheaf-1D7D/.

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Sheaf neural networks generalize graph neural networks by assigning each edge a learned linear restriction map that specifies how the local representations of adjacent nodes relate to one another, thereby mitigating oversmoothing. Existing sheaf architectures, however, define the sheaf Laplacian as an instantaneous spatial operator and therefore cannot explicitly model how signals evolve over time on a graph. We first introduce a spectral sheaf Laplacian, defined jointly over space and time, such that a single operator regulates signal propagation across both the spatial graph and the temporal domain. Directly extending this operator's receptive field in space or time would require increasingly complex sheaf structures. Instead, we propose a spectral filter that expands the effective receptive field along both dimensions without enlarging the underlying sheaf. Building on this operator, we introduce ChronoSheaf, a spatio-temporal sheaf network with two key components. First, Transitional Restriction Maps (TRM) decompose each node's restriction map into a spatial term and two temporal frames that independently control its relation to neighboring nodes at the preceding and following timestep, enabling asymmetric temporal influence. Second, the Dual Spectral Filter (DSF) applies a single bounded-degree polynomial filter with an adaptively estimated spectral radius to the joint spatio-temporal sheaf operator. We validate ChronoSheaf on a diverse set of spatio-temporal forecasting benchmarks, where it outperforms state-of-the-art baselines. The code is available at: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/ChronoSheaf-1D7D/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Sheaf neural networks generalize graph neural networks by assigning each edge a learned linear restriction map that specifies how the local representations of adjacent nodes relate to one another, thereby mitigating oversmoothing. Existing sheaf architectures, however, define the sheaf Laplacian as an instantaneous spatial operator and therefore cannot explicitly model how signals evolve over time on a graph. We first introduce a spectral sheaf Laplacian, defined jointly over space and time, such that a single operator regulates signal propagation across both the spatial graph and the temporal domain. Directly extending this operator's receptive field in space or time would require increasingly complex sheaf structures. Instead, we propose a spectral filter that expands the effective receptive field along both dimensions without enlarging the underlying sheaf. Building on this operator, we introduce ChronoSheaf, a spatio-temporal sheaf network with two key components. First, Transitional Restriction Maps (TRM) decompose each node's restriction map into a spatial term and two temporal frames that independently control its relation to neighboring nodes at the preceding and following timestep, enabling asymmetric temporal influence. Second, the Dual Spectral Filter (DSF) applies a single bounded-degree polynomial filter with an adaptively estimated spectral radius to the joint spatio-temporal sheaf operator. We validate ChronoSheaf on a diverse set of spatio-temporal forecasting benchmarks, where it outperforms state-of-the-art baselines. The code is available at: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/ChronoSheaf-1D7D/.

</details>

## 40. Sparse Federated Echo State Networks for Time-Series Classification via Smooth-L1 Regularization

**中文标题**: Sparse Federated Echo State Networks for Time-Series Classification via Smooth-L1 Regularization

**作者**: Gengcheng Lyu, Manuel Röder, Frank-Michael Schleif

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21742v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21742v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21742v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Echo State Networks (ESNs), as lightweight implementations of reservoir computing, provide efficient solutions for temporal data processing in resource-constrained environments. While standard ESNs offer computational efficiency, their dense readout layers inflate the stored model and the per-sample inference cost. This paper introduces a Smooth-L1 regularized ESN (SL1ESN) that promotes sparsity in the readout layer while preserving differentiability for gradient-based optimization. The method employs a twice-differentiable Smooth-L1 approximation compatible with Newton's method, which we exploit both for centralized training and for a second-order (Newton-step) federated aggregation scheme. Under a single fixed shrinkage threshold shared by every dataset and both settings, experiments on seven time-series classification benchmarks show that SL1ESN learns readouts that are $77$--$95\%$ sparse in the centralized setting and $75$--$95\%$ sparse in the federated one, while staying within $0.7$ accuracy points of the strongest dense baseline on five of the seven benchmarks in the centralized setting and within $1.6$ points on the same five in the federated one, and the second-order federated update converges in only a few rounds. The resulting sparsity compresses the stored model, the per-sample inference cost, and the server-to-client broadcast by a comparable factor. These results demonstrate the practical value of sparse readouts for edge computing and IoT deployments.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The resulting sparsity compresses the stored model, the per-sample inference cost, and the server-to-client broadcast by a comparable factor. These results demonstrate the practical value of sparse readouts for edge computing and IoT deployments.

</details>

## 41. Local Temporal Contrasts on Human Motion Data Induce a Latent Geometry

**中文标题**: Local Temporal Contrasts on Human Motion Data Induce a Latent Geometry

**作者**: Lourenço A. Rodrigues, Felix Putze

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21630v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21630v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21630v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Time series such as recordings of human motion data often encode structure across multiple temporal scales, and when a latent state determining this sequence evolves, this structure needs to survive in the representation space for downstream use. Yet the currently prevalent attention-based encoders paired with sequence-level pooling have made single-vector, pointwise comparison the default mode of inference, discarding the trajectory a sequence traces through the representation space. We propose recovering this geometric structure directly, without relying on forecasting-based objectives, by training an encoder model (SemTAC) with a temporal-neighborhood contrastive loss: representations that satisfy local consistency everywhere must give rise to a coherent global structure, letting inference happen at temporal scales beyond those seen during training. We evaluate SemTAC against four baselines on a five-step motion sequence discrimination task: the same architecture trained with a classification or a standard contrastive objective, and two established self-supervised time-series methods, TS-TCC and TS2Vec. At the full sequence length all five are broadly competitive, but on incomplete, truncated sequences SemTAC holds a large advantage: a median F1 of 0.77 one step before the sequence completes, against 0.27-0.70 for the four baselines. A hyperparameter-matched ablation attributes this effect specifically to the temporal-contrastive objective. SemTAC's representation trajectories are also the only ones among these five to exhibit interpretable geometric structures, exceeding a no-structure chance baseline on a displacement-smoothness metric which we introduce. This is evidence that its objective, not classification accuracy alone, produces genuinely temporally-ordered trajectories.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We evaluate SemTAC against four baselines on a five-step motion sequence discrimination task: the same architecture trained with a classification or a standard contrastive objective, and two established self-supervised time-series methods, TS-TCC and TS2Vec. At the full sequence length all five are broadly competitive, but on incomplete, truncated sequences SemTAC holds a large advantage: a median F1 of 0.77 one step before the sequence completes, against 0.27-0.70 for the four baselines. A hyperparameter-matched ablation attributes this effect specifically to the temporal-contrastive objective. SemTAC's representation trajectories are also the only ones among these five to exhibit interpretable geometric structures, exceeding a no-structure chance baseline on a displacement-smoothness metric which we introduce. This is evidence that its objective, not classification accuracy alone, produces genuinely temporally-ordered trajectories.

</details>

## 42. From Perception to Prediction and Intervention: Benchmarking Proactive Video Assistants in Real-Life Scenarios

**中文标题**: From Perception to Prediction and Intervention: Benchmarking Proactive Video Assistants in Real-Life Scenarios

**作者**: Boyu Cui, Junhao Luo, Zhihao Wang, Linghan Chen, Zejun Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21568v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21568v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21568v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent multimodal large language models have advanced video understanding, but existing benchmarks mainly evaluate whether models can interpret observed visual content. In real-world scenarios, assistants must additionally anticipate what assistance is needed and decide when to provide it. To address this gap, we introduce \textsc{P$^3$IBench}, a benchmark for proactive video understanding in real-life streaming scenarios. It evaluates three complementary capabilities: daily perception, risk prediction and intervention. We benchmark 12 representative open-source and proprietary video-language models, including streaming and adapted non-streaming systems. Our evaluation shows that current models struggle to make well-calibrated response decisions. Finally, we construct additional proactive training data and train \textsc{P$^3$I-Assistant-4B}, achieving improvements on several tasks, though gains remain uneven across capabilities. Together, these findings show that effective proactive assistance requires not only visual understanding, but also precise response timing and appropriate restraint.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recent multimodal large language models have advanced video understanding, but existing benchmarks mainly evaluate whether models can interpret observed visual content. In real-world scenarios, assistants must additionally anticipate what assistance is needed and decide when to provide it. To address this gap, we introduce \textsc{P$^3$IBench}, a benchmark for proactive video understanding in real-life streaming scenarios. It evaluates three complementary capabilities: daily perception, risk prediction and intervention. We benchmark 12 representative open-source and proprietary video-language models, including streaming and adapted non-streaming systems. Our evaluation shows that current models struggle to make well-calibrated response decisions. Finally, we construct additional proactive training data and train \textsc{P$^3$I-Assistant-4B}, achieving improvements on several tasks, though gains remain uneven across capabilities. Together, these findings show that effective proactive assistance requires not only visual understanding, but also precise response timing and appropriate restraint.

</details>

## 43. DuET: Note-Frame Dual Encoding for Piano Performance-to-Score Transcription

**中文标题**: DuET: Note-Frame Dual Encoding for Piano Performance-to-Score Transcription

**作者**: Wei Zeng, Ye Wang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21467v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21467v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21467v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/wei-zeng98/DuET.

**类别**: cs.SD, cs.LG, eess.AS

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Transcribing expressive piano performances into scores requires interpreting individual note timings within an underlying metrical structure. However, score attributes are associated with notes, whereas beats may occur without coincident note onsets. We introduce DuET, a Dual-Encoder Transformer that jointly learns note-level performance MIDI-to-score (PM2S) transcription and frame-level beat and downbeat tracking. Bidirectional cross-attention connects note and frame encoders, while an autoregressive decoder alternates between their representations to predict structured score attributes. We further introduce TimeRoPE and Time-Pitch Rotary Positional Encoding (TPRoPE) to establish a shared performance-time reference and explicitly encode relative temporal and pitch relationships. Evaluations on ASAP, a classical piano dataset, and our curated Gangqinpu dataset, which spans multiple genres with a focus on popular piano music, demonstrate improvements in score timing and notated duration. Compared with the strongest baseline on each dataset, DuET reduces MUSTER mean error from 6.56% to 3.64% on Gangqinpu and from 11.30% to 11.22% on ASAP. Ablations support the benefits of frame-level context, time-based positional encoding, and decoder-history masking. Code, weights, and demos are available at https://github.com/wei-zeng98/DuET.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Transcribing expressive piano performances into scores requires interpreting individual note timings within an underlying metrical structure. However, score attributes are associated with notes, whereas beats may occur without coincident note onsets. We introduce DuET, a Dual-Encoder Transformer that jointly learns note-level performance MIDI-to-score (PM2S) transcription and frame-level beat and downbeat tracking. Bidirectional cross-attention connects note and frame encoders, while an autoregressive decoder alternates between their representations to predict structured score attributes. We further introduce TimeRoPE and Time-Pitch Rotary Positional Encoding (TPRoPE) to establish a shared performance-time reference and explicitly encode relative temporal and pitch relationships. Evaluations on ASAP, a classical piano dataset, and our curated Gangqinpu dataset, which spans multiple genres with a focus on popular piano music, demonstrate improvements in score timing and notated duration. Compared with the strongest baseline on each dataset, DuET reduces MUSTER mean error from 6.56% to 3.64% on Gangqinpu and from 11.30% to 11.22% on ASAP. Ablations support the benefits of frame-level context, time-based positional encoding, and decoder-history masking. Code, weights, and demos are available at https://github.com/wei-zeng98/DuET.

</details>

## 44. What Does History Add? Calendar Controls and Information-Timing Audits for University Trajectory Forecasting

**中文标题**: What Does History Add? Calendar Controls and Information-Timing Audits for University Trajectory Forecasting

**作者**: Zepei Cao

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21419v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21419v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21419v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We audit the predictive value of historical summaries in a rule-based university trajectory simulator. Seventeen ridge specifications and persistence separate current plans, past-state and past-plan summaries, and calendar-only history-length indicators. Across training, validation and two family-held-out evaluations, we reconstruct 16,750 routes and 804,000 monthly records. On the original 40-family set, six-month history lowers six-step normalized RMSE from 0.159234 to 0.151522, but its difference from a length-only control is -0.000895 (descriptive paired 95% interval [-0.001957,0.000248]). Fixed weights on 30 families unused in identified prior tasks reproduce this pattern: 0.156480 to 0.150074 versus current-state prediction, but -0.000558 [-0.001783,0.000728] versus the length control. These uncertain comparisons do not generalize to all-history summaries, which improve on their own calendar control. Exploratory decomposition finds clearer preparation-index and funds benefits, whereas fatigue dominates the six-month model's loss and has an uncertain length-controlled benefit. Moreover, 28.20% of its six-step fatigue predictions violate the simulator's [5,90] support. We document family dependence, prior exposure, strict temporal indexing, matched recursive references and independent numerical replay. The task conditions on recorded future plans; it does not evaluate real-student forecasts, causal action effects, autonomous planning or language-model memory. This is an evaluation audit, not a new memory architecture.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Fixed weights on 30 families unused in identified prior tasks reproduce this pattern: 0.156480 to 0.150074 versus current-state prediction, but -0.000558 [-0.001783,0.000728] versus the length control. These uncertain comparisons do not generalize to all-history summaries, which improve on their own calendar control. Exploratory decomposition finds clearer preparation-index and funds benefits, whereas fatigue dominates the six-month model's loss and has an uncertain length-controlled benefit. We document family dependence, prior exposure, strict temporal indexing, matched recursive references and independent numerical replay. The task conditions on recorded future plans; it does not evaluate real-student forecasts, causal action effects, autonomous planning or language-model memory. This is an evaluation audit, not a new memory architecture.

</details>

## 45. Learning to Forecast on Multiple Spatiotemporal Hierarchies

**中文标题**: Learning to Forecast on Multiple Spatiotemporal Hierarchies

**作者**: Valentina Moretti, Cesare Alippi, Andrea Cini

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21414v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21414v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21414v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Hierarchical forecasting exploits aggregation structures that expose multiple views of the same underlying spatiotemporal forecasting problem, while ensuring coherence among forecasts. Individual series provide fine-grained information, while progressively coarser aggregates reveal temporal and spatial patterns at different scales. In the associated deep learning architectures, the hierarchy determines which aggregate series are available and how they are processed together with individual series (e.g., through reconciliation or message-passing operators). Different hierarchies thus provide distinct learning signals and inductive biases for forecasting the same collection of time series. Nonetheless, existing architectures are typically trained on a single, often fixed, structure. In this work, we propose a graph deep learning framework that leverages variations across hierarchies as multi-task regularization. We show that jointly learning across these structures can reduce over-specialization to any single hierarchy and improve forecasting performance. Indeed, training across hierarchies provides an inductive bias toward representations that are useful across different structures, and as such, generalize better. Making forecasts coherent with the aggregation constraints for each sampled hierarchy is computationally expensive: to complement the framework, we propose a novel operator-based on a top-down approach-that produces coherent forecasts with linear time and space complexity w.r.t. the hierarchy size. Experiments show that our approach improves forecasting accuracy over training on a single structure and is competitive with current state-of-the-art methods.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Individual series provide fine-grained information, while progressively coarser aggregates reveal temporal and spatial patterns at different scales. In the associated deep learning architectures, the hierarchy determines which aggregate series are available and how they are processed together with individual series (e.g., through reconciliation or message-passing operators). Different hierarchies thus provide distinct learning signals and inductive biases for forecasting the same collection of time series. In this work, we propose a graph deep learning framework that leverages variations across hierarchies as multi-task regularization. We show that jointly learning across these structures can reduce over-specialization to any single hierarchy and improve forecasting performance. Indeed, training across hierarchies provides an inductive bias toward representations that are useful across different structures, and as such, generalize better. Making forecasts coherent with the aggregation constraints for each sampled hierarchy is computationally expensive: to complement the framework, we propose a novel operator-based on a top-down approach-that produces coherent forecasts with linear time and space complexity w.r.t. the hierarchy size. Experiments show that our approach improves forecasting accuracy over training on a single structure and is competitive with current state-of-the-art methods.

</details>

## 46. RDL-Bench: A Benchmark for Task-Aware Question Answering over Relational Databases

**中文标题**: RDL-Bench: A Benchmark for Task-Aware Question Answering over Relational Databases

**作者**: Yao Cheng, Siqiang Luo

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21382v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21382v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21382v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Relational databases (RDBs) remain the primary infrastructure for storing and managing structured data across enterprise and scientific domains. Natural-language (NL) interfaces to RDBs aim to let users ask questions without writing SQL, but real user needs go beyond SQL-style lookup: some questions require reasoning over the observable database state, while others require predictive inference from historical relational records under temporal constraints. Existing benchmarks address these goals in isolation. To address this gap, we introduce RDL-Bench, a benchmark for task-aware question answering (QA) over real RDBs. RDL-Bench organizes diverse user questions into reasoning-oriented questions, whose answers are determined by the current database state, and prediction-oriented questions, whose answers depend on future outcomes under temporal constraints. Each instance contains an NL question, task metadata, schema information, temporal context, either executable evidence or target-construction logic, and denotation-level ground truth. RDL-Bench supports two evaluation settings: native solvers under their intended capabilities, and task-aware modular systems where a router dispatches each question to an appropriate execution module.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Relational databases (RDBs) remain the primary infrastructure for storing and managing structured data across enterprise and scientific domains. Existing benchmarks address these goals in isolation. To address this gap, we introduce RDL-Bench, a benchmark for task-aware question answering (QA) over real RDBs. RDL-Bench organizes diverse user questions into reasoning-oriented questions, whose answers are determined by the current database state, and prediction-oriented questions, whose answers depend on future outcomes under temporal constraints. Each instance contains an NL question, task metadata, schema information, temporal context, either executable evidence or target-construction logic, and denotation-level ground truth. RDL-Bench supports two evaluation settings: native solvers under their intended capabilities, and task-aware modular systems where a router dispatches each question to an appropriate execution module.

</details>

## 47. Same Target, Different Origins: Aligning Latent Rollout for Consistent PDE Emulation

**中文标题**: Same Target, Different Origins: Aligning Latent Rollout for Consistent PDE Emulation

**作者**: Xinying Li, Jing Xu, Wenjun Wang, Limei Han, Fenglei Cao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21370v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21370v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21370v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Neural PDE solvers commonly generate multi-step predictions through autoregressive rollout, recursively applying a learned evolution operator and feeding each prediction back as input for the next step. Approximation errors can therefore propagate through the rollout, causing predictions at a shared target time to depend on their temporal origins and rollout depths. For a well-posed deterministic PDE flow, however, states at different times along the same physical trajectory should, under exact evolution, reach the same target state. To this end, we propose Align Latent Rollout (ALR), a regularization approach that promotes consistency across rollout origins through the neural PDE solver's internal representations. A second-order Taylor analysis of latent discrepancy propagation motivates two objectives: zeroth-order latent state consistency, which aligns representations at a shared target time, and first-order latent dynamics consistency, which aligns local evolution responses to shared perturbations. ALR can be integrated into different neural PDE solvers by regularizing suitable internal representations, without modifying their backbone architectures. Experiments across multiple PDE benchmarks and neural-operator backbones show that ALR improves prediction accuracy while reducing shared-target discrepancies across rollout origins.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Neural PDE solvers commonly generate multi-step predictions through autoregressive rollout, recursively applying a learned evolution operator and feeding each prediction back as input for the next step. Approximation errors can therefore propagate through the rollout, causing predictions at a shared target time to depend on their temporal origins and rollout depths. For a well-posed deterministic PDE flow, however, states at different times along the same physical trajectory should, under exact evolution, reach the same target state. To this end, we propose Align Latent Rollout (ALR), a regularization approach that promotes consistency across rollout origins through the neural PDE solver's internal representations. A second-order Taylor analysis of latent discrepancy propagation motivates two objectives: zeroth-order latent state consistency, which aligns representations at a shared target time, and first-order latent dynamics consistency, which aligns local evolution responses to shared perturbations. ALR can be integrated into different neural PDE solvers by regularizing suitable internal representations, without modifying their backbone architectures. Experiments across multiple PDE benchmarks and neural-operator backbones show that ALR improves prediction accuracy while reducing shared-target discrepancies across rollout origins.

</details>

## 48. PocketTSLM: Deploying an On-Device Soft-Prompt Time-Series Language Model for Privacy-Preserving Smartphone Reasoning

**中文标题**: PocketTSLM: Deploying an On-Device Soft-Prompt Time-Series Language Model for Privacy-Preserving Smartphone Reasoning

**作者**: Max Rosenblattl, Nikhil Krishnaswamy, Leon Nissen, Manuel Milling, Nicolas Zumarraga et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21308v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21308v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21308v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.CL

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Consumer phones and wearables increasingly collect physiological time-series data, yet most mobile systems treat these streams as summaries or text rather than as temporal evidence. This representation is poorly matched to personal health systems that need to retrieve, route, or summarize sensor evidence while preserving temporal structure. Cloud-hosted time-series language models avoid smartphone limits, but they repeatedly move sensitive physiology off the device and add latency and per-query compute cost. Mobile model alternatives that aggregate time-series data into statistical features or serialize raw samples as text either compress temporal structure or exhaust the context and memory budgets of small on-device models. We present PocketTSLM, a mobile-native deployment of OpenTSLM SoftPrompt, the soft-prompt variant of the OpenTSLM time-series language model. PocketTSLM runs fully on a consumer smartphone by injecting encoded time-series windows as embeddings instead of tokenizing time series as text. A numerical parity protocol verifies identical greedy-decoded token sequences between the Swift port and a Python reference using Apple's MLX machine-learning framework. Answers also match the PyTorch reference, although rationale wording can differ. An on-device context-economics benchmark compares the soft-prompt path with a text-serialized baseline on the same backbone. In our benchmark format, soft-prompting uses approximately 12 times fewer time-series tokens and reduces prompt-prefill cost. PocketTSLM processes 30-second Sleep-EDF electroencephalography clips for sleep staging and a 1,000-sample (10 s) 12-lead electrocardiogram clip from ECG-QA. In comparison, text-serialized baselines exceed the phone's context or memory budget at longer time-series windows. All inference runs entirely on the device, with no health data transmitted during inference.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

This representation is poorly matched to personal health systems that need to retrieve, route, or summarize sensor evidence while preserving temporal structure. In comparison, text-serialized baselines exceed the phone's context or memory budget at longer time-series windows. All inference runs entirely on the device, with no health data transmitted during inference.

</details>
