## 49. Learning and Forecasting Multiscale Spatiotemporal Epidemic Dynamics with Lagrangian-Eulerian Graph Neural ODEs

**中文标题**: Learning and Forecasting Multiscale Spatiotemporal Epidemic Dynamics with Lagrangian-Eulerian Graph Neural ODEs

**作者**: Jinyi Duan, Qian Li, Biao Tang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-15

**arXiv ID**: [2610.21223v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21223v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21223v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Accurate medium- to long-range epidemic forecasting requires extrapolating high-dimensional, heterogeneous and nonstationary dynamics from partial observations. Yet hybrid models often leave implicit which observations inform which epidemic processes and how learned graph interactions modify system state. We introduce LE-EpiGNODE, which replaces generic message passing with process-specific, mechanism-guided neural operators: age-contact information drives Lagrangian infection generation, whereas mobility drives conservative Eulerian population transport within a structure-preserving universal differential equation. We evaluated LE-EpiGNODE across 14 controlled synthetic mechanisms spanning time-modulated, state-dependent, nonlinear and coupled dynamics, three complementary COVID-19 settings, 21 comparator models and five targeted ablations. Forecasting gains tended to become more pronounced at longer horizons; at the longest horizon, LE-EpiGNODE achieved the lowest WMAPE in all three real-world datasets, reducing error by 17.7-40.0% relative to the best baseline. Controlled ablations linked these gains to the Lagrangian-Eulerian decomposition and process-aligned priors, while synthetic ground truth showed that learned operators and induced state fluxes track known generating dynamics. These results support mechanism-organized graph neural dynamics as a route to reliable long-horizon extrapolation with directly testable, process-level mechanistic interpretation beyond trajectory accuracy.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Yet hybrid models often leave implicit which observations inform which epidemic processes and how learned graph interactions modify system state. We evaluated LE-EpiGNODE across 14 controlled synthetic mechanisms spanning time-modulated, state-dependent, nonlinear and coupled dynamics, three complementary COVID-19 settings, 21 comparator models and five targeted ablations. Forecasting gains tended to become more pronounced at longer horizons; at the longest horizon, LE-EpiGNODE achieved the lowest WMAPE in all three real-world datasets, reducing error by 17.7-40.0% relative to the best baseline. Controlled ablations linked these gains to the Lagrangian-Eulerian decomposition and process-aligned priors, while synthetic ground truth showed that learned operators and induced state fluxes track known generating dynamics. These results support mechanism-organized graph neural dynamics as a route to reliable long-horizon extrapolation with directly testable, process-level mechanistic interpretation beyond trajectory accuracy.

</details>

---

## 50. Temporal Specification Guidance for Text-to-Video Generation

**中文标题**: Temporal Specification Guidance for Text-to-Video Generation

**作者**: Avishree Khare, Hideki Okamoto, Bardh Hoxha, Georgios Fainekos, Rajeev Alur

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20822v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20822v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20822v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Text-to-video models often generate the visual concepts requested in natural language prompts but fail to satisfy their intended ordering, timing, and persistence requirements. We propose representing these requirements as specifications in a formal Temporal Logic, and introduce Temporal Specification Guidance (TSG), a training-free inference-time framework that turns temporal specifications into executable objectives for guiding video generation during sampling. TSG scores traces of visual concept evidence using differentiable quantitative semantics and uses the score's gradients to construct a specification-guided denoising direction. We instantiate this framework with video-text attention traces, efficient differentiable scores for Metric Temporal Logic, and gradient-derived attention biases. This instantiation requires neither an external concept classifier nor backpropagation through the video transformer. Its attention-steered forward pass yields a denoising direction that augments classifier-free guidance (CFG). On a benchmark of 220 prompts spanning 11 categories of temporal constraints, including ordered object entry and state transitions, this instantiation improves specification satisfaction accuracy over CFG by 9.5-20 percentage points across five models, with minimal memory overhead. These results demonstrate the potential of temporal specifications and differentiable satisfaction objectives as a flexible interface for controlling video generation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Text-to-video models often generate the visual concepts requested in natural language prompts but fail to satisfy their intended ordering, timing, and persistence requirements. We propose representing these requirements as specifications in a formal Temporal Logic, and introduce Temporal Specification Guidance (TSG), a training-free inference-time framework that turns temporal specifications into executable objectives for guiding video generation during sampling. TSG scores traces of visual concept evidence using differentiable quantitative semantics and uses the score's gradients to construct a specification-guided denoising direction. We instantiate this framework with video-text attention traces, efficient differentiable scores for Metric Temporal Logic, and gradient-derived attention biases. This instantiation requires neither an external concept classifier nor backpropagation through the video transformer. Its attention-steered forward pass yields a denoising direction that augments classifier-free guidance (CFG). On a benchmark of 220 prompts spanning 11 categories of temporal constraints, including ordered object entry and state transitions, this instantiation improves specification satisfaction accuracy over CFG by 9.5-20 percentage points across five models, with minimal memory overhead. These results demonstrate the potential of temporal specifications and differentiable satisfaction objectives as a flexible interface for controlling video generation.

</details>

## 51. RIGen: Beyond Action Following with Reciprocal Interaction in Generative Gameplay

**中文标题**: RIGen: Beyond Action Following with Reciprocal Interaction in Generative Gameplay

**作者**: Mengfei Li, Yi Zhang, Xingtong Ge, Lunjie Zhu, Fangyu Lin et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20811v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20811v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20811v1)

**项目主页**: https://murphylmf.github.io/RIGen/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Generative gameplay requires more than immediate responses to player actions.Actions should have persistent consequences, while the generated world should act according to its own state and rules, shaping the player's situation and subsequent choices. Video generation captures local dynamics, but limited history, partially observed game state, and the need for contextual behavior decisions make such interactions difficult to sustain through audiovisual prediction alone. We introduce \rig (Reciprocally Interactive Generation), a framework for reciprocal interaction in generative gameplay. \rig combines persistent state and configurable rules, contextual interaction reasoning, and action-conditioned audiovisual generation. Together, these components make interaction consequences and actor behavior jointly determine generated content while maintaining consistency between game state and visible outcomes. We construct a Xonotic gameplay dataset to train an audiovisual generator for learning dynamics and interactions. Further post-training enables \rigarena, an interactive generative first-person shooter (FPS) prototype. Experiments demonstrate more reliable combat outcomes and reciprocal interaction between the player and the generated world. Project page: https://murphylmf.github.io/RIGen/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Generative gameplay requires more than immediate responses to player actions.Actions should have persistent consequences, while the generated world should act according to its own state and rules, shaping the player's situation and subsequent choices. Video generation captures local dynamics, but limited history, partially observed game state, and the need for contextual behavior decisions make such interactions difficult to sustain through audiovisual prediction alone. We introduce \rig (Reciprocally Interactive Generation), a framework for reciprocal interaction in generative gameplay. \rig combines persistent state and configurable rules, contextual interaction reasoning, and action-conditioned audiovisual generation. Together, these components make interaction consequences and actor behavior jointly determine generated content while maintaining consistency between game state and visible outcomes. We construct a Xonotic gameplay dataset to train an audiovisual generator for learning dynamics and interactions. Further post-training enables \rigarena, an interactive generative first-person shooter (FPS) prototype. Experiments demonstrate more reliable combat outcomes and reciprocal interaction between the player and the generated world. Project page: https://murphylmf.github.io/RIGen/.

</details>

## 52. DynThinker: Unifying Static 3D and Dynamic 4D Spatial Reasoning

**中文标题**: DynThinker: Unifying Static 3D and Dynamic 4D Spatial Reasoning

**作者**: Qihang Cao, Haoyuan Li, Zihan Guo, Sensen Gao, Zihui Gao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20598v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20598v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20598v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Achieving general spatial intelligence requires a unified understanding of both static 3D and dynamic 4D. However, we find that existing models struggle to excel at both: 3D post-training can weaken dynamic understanding, while 4D-specialized models often lack strong static 3D reasoning. Our analysis reveals that this spatial--dynamic interference is highly non-uniform across parameter groups, with the balance between static and dynamic capabilities particularly sensitive to changes in late-layer MLPs. To address this interference, we propose DynThinker, a unified framework for jointly strengthening static and dynamic spatial reasoning. Decoupled Exponential Moving Average (D-EMA) applies configurable group-wise averaging to constrain drift in interference-sensitive pretrained parameters while preserving flexibility for capability enhancement. Spatial-Dynamic Fusion (SDF) complements D-EMA by selectively integrating spatial and temporal priors from a dynamic geometry encoder into intermediate VLM representations under semantic guidance. Across ten metrics spanning nine static 3D and dynamic 4D benchmarks, DynThinker achieves the highest average score among all evaluated models. Notably, DynThinker achieves 56.0 on ReVSI, 67.2 on DSR-Bench, and 51.2 and 40.1 under the sample-wise and group-wise evaluation protocols of DSI-Bench, respectively, setting new state-of-the-art results among the compared open-source models across all four evaluations within a unified model.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Achieving general spatial intelligence requires a unified understanding of both static 3D and dynamic 4D. However, we find that existing models struggle to excel at both: 3D post-training can weaken dynamic understanding, while 4D-specialized models often lack strong static 3D reasoning. Our analysis reveals that this spatial--dynamic interference is highly non-uniform across parameter groups, with the balance between static and dynamic capabilities particularly sensitive to changes in late-layer MLPs. To address this interference, we propose DynThinker, a unified framework for jointly strengthening static and dynamic spatial reasoning. Decoupled Exponential Moving Average (D-EMA) applies configurable group-wise averaging to constrain drift in interference-sensitive pretrained parameters while preserving flexibility for capability enhancement. Spatial-Dynamic Fusion (SDF) complements D-EMA by selectively integrating spatial and temporal priors from a dynamic geometry encoder into intermediate VLM representations under semantic guidance. Across ten metrics spanning nine static 3D and dynamic 4D benchmarks, DynThinker achieves the highest average score among all evaluated models. Notably, DynThinker achieves 56.0 on ReVSI, 67.2 on DSR-Bench, and 51.2 and 40.1 under the sample-wise and group-wise evaluation protocols of DSI-Bench, respectively, setting new state-of-the-art results among the compared open-source models across all four evaluations within a unified model.

</details>

## 53. GeoThinker-$F$: Endogenous Predictive World Representations for Fast Spatial Intelligence

**中文标题**: GeoThinker-$F$: Endogenous Predictive World Representations for Fast Spatial Intelligence

**作者**: Haoyuan Li, Qihang Cao, Huanyuan Zhou, Xin Hu, Sijia Chen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20592v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20592v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20592v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Existing geometry-aware Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) increasingly rely on external 3D encoders to provide structural priors, but executing these additional encoder paths at inference introduces substantial latency and computational overhead. We propose GeoThinker-$F$, an efficient framework that internalizes spatiotemporal perception by transforming causal MLLM hidden states into predictive latent world representations. Our key insight is that intermediate semantic representations contain sufficient information to predict useful representations of current geometry and future semantics, without requiring an exact reproduction of features from a dedicated 3D encoder. To realize this idea, GeoThinker-$F$ adopts a decoupled two-stage training strategy. In the first stage, the MLLM backbone is frozen while lightweight Latent Frame Prediction (LFP) heads learn from an offline 3D geometry expert and next-frame semantic targets. In the second stage, the external expert is removed, and the model is jointly optimized to generate and selectively integrate its self-predicted priors through a specialized Spatial-Grounded Temporal (SGT) Fusion module. Extensive evaluations show that GeoThinker-$F$ eliminates runtime dependence on external 3D geometry encoders while maintaining competitive performance across diverse spatial intelligence benchmarks. It achieves up to a $3.6\times$ inference speedup, reducing latency from $1.45\,\mathrm{s}$ to $0.40\,\mathrm{s}$ while retaining strong spatial reasoning performance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing geometry-aware Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) increasingly rely on external 3D encoders to provide structural priors, but executing these additional encoder paths at inference introduces substantial latency and computational overhead. We propose GeoThinker-$F$, an efficient framework that internalizes spatiotemporal perception by transforming causal MLLM hidden states into predictive latent world representations. Our key insight is that intermediate semantic representations contain sufficient information to predict useful representations of current geometry and future semantics, without requiring an exact reproduction of features from a dedicated 3D encoder. To realize this idea, GeoThinker-$F$ adopts a decoupled two-stage training strategy. In the first stage, the MLLM backbone is frozen while lightweight Latent Frame Prediction (LFP) heads learn from an offline 3D geometry expert and next-frame semantic targets. In the second stage, the external expert is removed, and the model is jointly optimized to generate and selectively integrate its self-predicted priors through a specialized Spatial-Grounded Temporal (SGT) Fusion module. Extensive evaluations show that GeoThinker-$F$ eliminates runtime dependence on external 3D geometry encoders while maintaining competitive performance across diverse spatial intelligence benchmarks. It achieves up to a $3.6\times$ inference speedup, reducing latency from $1.45\,\mathrm{s}$ to $0.40\,\mathrm{s}$ while retaining strong spatial reasoning performance.

</details>

## 54. Any Time, Any Space: A World-State-Centric Framework for Agentic Video Generation

**中文标题**: Any Time, Any Space: A World-State-Centric Framework for Agentic Video Generation

**作者**: Weijie Wang, Haomin Zhang, Ye Li, Akide Liu, Zeyu Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20571v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20571v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20571v1)

**项目主页**: https://lhmd.top/atas/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Long-form agentic video generation must turn a screenplay into shots that remain spatially and temporally consistent across nonconsecutive requests. Passing prompts or recent frames between shots does not provide a shared spatial frame for revisiting a scene or a time-indexed record for replaying an earlier event. We present Any Time, Any Space (ATAS), which compiles a screenplay into shot requests with explicit location, camera, and event-time references. Before generation, it retrieves relevant evidence from a scene memory of registered static geometry and an event memory of dynamic observations indexed by scene, event, and time. After generation, ATAS reconstructs and registers the clip. It updates memory only when review and state checks accept the observation, while film delivery remains independent of memory admission. Across four video generators, ATAS improves all reported cross-shot consistency measures and achieves the highest aggregate benchmark score for each backbone. It also outperforms the compared agents and 3D-guided methods on target-image spatial revisit, leads three event-recapture criteria, and receives the highest human ratings on all six criteria. The video results and code are available on our project page: https://lhmd.top/atas/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Long-form agentic video generation must turn a screenplay into shots that remain spatially and temporally consistent across nonconsecutive requests. Passing prompts or recent frames between shots does not provide a shared spatial frame for revisiting a scene or a time-indexed record for replaying an earlier event. We present Any Time, Any Space (ATAS), which compiles a screenplay into shot requests with explicit location, camera, and event-time references. Before generation, it retrieves relevant evidence from a scene memory of registered static geometry and an event memory of dynamic observations indexed by scene, event, and time. After generation, ATAS reconstructs and registers the clip. It updates memory only when review and state checks accept the observation, while film delivery remains independent of memory admission. Across four video generators, ATAS improves all reported cross-shot consistency measures and achieves the highest aggregate benchmark score for each backbone. It also outperforms the compared agents and 3D-guided methods on target-image spatial revisit, leads three event-recapture criteria, and receives the highest human ratings on all six criteria. The video results and code are available on our project page: https://lhmd.top/atas/.

## 55. Speculative Sampling in Interactive Video Generation

**中文标题**: Speculative Sampling in Interactive Video Generation

**作者**: Yubo Huang, Yuxuan Zhang, Jiaming Liu, Sirui Zhao, Enhong Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20556v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20556v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20556v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.HC

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Interactive video generation commonly relies on small bidirectional chunks to reduce response latency. Yet our preliminary experiments show that larger chunks improve video quality and better utilize GPU parallelism, allowing more latents to be sampled with only a modest increase in sampling time. Motivated by these observations, we propose Speculative Sampling, a strategy that combines the modeling power of large bidirectional windows with the low-latency interaction of small ones. It expands the modeling window by appending noise latents as padding and caches the resulting predictions for reuse when the condition remains unchanged. Predictions are delivered incrementally, while the uncommitted future remains replaceable. Our latency analysis shows that this removes the waiting-time lower bound imposed by chunk boundaries, shifting the optimization focus from reducing chunk size to accelerating per-chunk sampling. Bidirectional window size thus becomes a decision variable for jointly optimizing visual quality and sampling time, rather than a necessary constraint on low-latency interaction. This opens a path to serving large bidirectional models in interactive streaming systems without complex streaming post-training.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Interactive video generation commonly relies on small bidirectional chunks to reduce response latency. Yet our preliminary experiments show that larger chunks improve video quality and better utilize GPU parallelism, allowing more latents to be sampled with only a modest increase in sampling time. Motivated by these observations, we propose Speculative Sampling, a strategy that combines the modeling power of large bidirectional windows with the low-latency interaction of small ones. It expands the modeling window by appending noise latents as padding and caches the resulting predictions for reuse when the condition remains unchanged. Predictions are delivered incrementally, while the uncommitted future remains replaceable. Our latency analysis shows that this removes the waiting-time lower bound imposed by chunk boundaries, shifting the optimization focus from reducing chunk size to accelerating per-chunk sampling. Bidirectional window size thus becomes a decision variable for jointly optimizing visual quality and sampling time, rather than a necessary constraint on low-latency interaction. This opens a path to serving large bidirectional models in interactive streaming systems without complex streaming post-training.

</details>

## 56. Reliability-Aware Multimodal Sentiment Prediction Under Local Modality Missingness

**中文标题**: Reliability-Aware Multimodal Sentiment Prediction Under Local Modality Missingness

**作者**: Minghao Kong, Deng Yao, Xi Zhang, Ying Gao, Jiurun Chen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20543v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20543v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20543v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multimodal sentiment models are usually evaluated on complete utterances, yet streams often lose a contiguous span of text, audio, or video. We study local missingness on aligned CMU-MOSEI features. Our reliability-aware network (RM-Net) keeps the original temporal positions, represents unobserved positions with a learned token, outputs bounded per-modality intensity contributions gated by observation quality, and is trained with dual-view masking of contiguous segments. We fuse five RM-Net seeds with a LoRA-adapted BERT text expert, using a fusion weight that depends only on the visible fraction of content tokens. Architecture, checkpoint, and fusion choices were made on the validation split. On the held-out split, the fused model reaches a macro-F1 of 0.655 on clean inputs and 0.634 under 40\% mixed missingness; its two-view average exceeds the auxiliary-head baseline by 0.024 (95\% video-cluster bootstrap interval [0.003, 0.045]). Because this split was inspected during development, these numbers are not a blind test. For explanations, exact Shapley attributions over the eight modality subsets and position-level occlusion show that top-ranked positions reduce the predicted-class probability faster than random positions (AUC gap 0.113 on validation). We also describe an auditable word-level alignment pipeline for raw clips; its outputs do not feed the predictive models.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Multimodal sentiment models are usually evaluated on complete utterances, yet streams often lose a contiguous span of text, audio, or video. We study local missingness on aligned CMU-MOSEI features. Our reliability-aware network (RM-Net) keeps the original temporal positions, represents unobserved positions with a learned token, outputs bounded per-modality intensity contributions gated by observation quality, and is trained with dual-view masking of contiguous segments. We fuse five RM-Net seeds with a LoRA-adapted BERT text expert, using a fusion weight that depends only on the visible fraction of content tokens. Architecture, checkpoint, and fusion choices were made on the validation split. On the held-out split, the fused model reaches a macro-F1 of 0.655 on clean inputs and 0.634 under 40\% mixed missingness; its two-view average exceeds the auxiliary-head baseline by 0.024 (95\% video-cluster bootstrap interval [0.003, 0.045]). Because this split was inspected during development, these numbers are not a blind test. For explanations, exact Shapley attributions over the eight modality subsets and position-level occlusion show that top-ranked positions reduce the predicted-class probability faster than random positions (AUC gap 0.113 on validation). We also describe an auditable word-level alignment pipeline for raw clips; its outputs do not feed the predictive models.

</details>

## 57. OLiVES: An Outdoor Low-Light Video Benchmark for Enhancement and Segmentation

**中文标题**: OLiVES: An Outdoor Low-Light Video Benchmark for Enhancement and Segmentation

**作者**: Yini Li, Joanne Lin, David Blackham, David Bull, Nantheera Anantrasirichai

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20501v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20501v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20501v1)

**项目主页**: https://anonymous-OLiVES.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Low-light video processing remains an underexplored problem in computer vision, primarily due to the scarcity of high-quality ground-truth data. Existing public datasets exhibit several limitations, including restricted motion diversity, limited scene coverage, weak degradations, reliance on auxiliary modalities such as event data, and misalignment between low-light inputs and ground truth. Furthermore, current datasets typically address either low-light enhancement or scene understanding, whereas these tasks should be considered jointly. We introduce \textbf{OLiVES}, a benchmark dataset for outdoor low-light videos with dynamic scenes, designed to support both low-light video enhancement (LLVE) and video object segmentation (VOS). The dataset addresses key content gaps by incorporating a diverse range of natural textures, animals, occlusions, dynamic activities, and close-up scenes. To the best of our knowledge, OLiVES is the first outdoor low-light video dataset captured using professional cinema-grade cameras. It provides over 25,000 aligned normal- and low-light frame pairs, along with over 46,000 VOS annotations. We benchmark state-of-the-art LLVE and VOS methods on OLiVES and show that training on it improves performance on both tasks. The OLiVES project page is available at: https://anonymous-OLiVES.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Low-light video processing remains an underexplored problem in computer vision, primarily due to the scarcity of high-quality ground-truth data. Existing public datasets exhibit several limitations, including restricted motion diversity, limited scene coverage, weak degradations, reliance on auxiliary modalities such as event data, and misalignment between low-light inputs and ground truth. Furthermore, current datasets typically address either low-light enhancement or scene understanding, whereas these tasks should be considered jointly. We introduce \textbf{OLiVES}, a benchmark dataset for outdoor low-light videos with dynamic scenes, designed to support both low-light video enhancement (LLVE) and video object segmentation (VOS). The dataset addresses key content gaps by incorporating a diverse range of natural textures, animals, occlusions, dynamic activities, and close-up scenes. To the best of our knowledge, OLiVES is the first outdoor low-light video dataset captured using professional cinema-grade cameras. It provides over 25,000 aligned normal- and low-light frame pairs, along with over 46,000 VOS annotations. We benchmark state-of-the-art LLVE and VOS methods on OLiVES and show that training on it improves performance on both tasks. The OLiVES project page is available at: https://anonymous-OLiVES.github.io/.

</details>

## 58. NeuroDETR: Multiscale Discrete Spatiotemporal Tokenization for Asynchronous EEG Event Detection

**中文标题**: NeuroDETR: Multiscale Discrete Spatiotemporal Tokenization for Asynchronous EEG Event Detection

**作者**: Yaozhi Wen, Kun Zhao, Wei Wei, Haoyang Chen, Kangning Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20349v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20349v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20349v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Asynchronous EEG event detection supports practical brain-computer interfaces by identifying event classes and temporal boundaries directly from continuous recordings without predefined event boundaries at inference time. The primary challenge lies in extracting event-related patterns embedded in ongoing background activity. To address this, we propose \textbf{NeuroDETR}, a discrete spatiotemporal multiscale framework that combines structured tokenization with event-aware feature refinement. Multiscale temporal codebooks capture brief responses and sustained dynamics, while Spatial-Pyramid VQ hierarchically encodes spatial structure across EEG channels. These complementary representations form a compact event memory for jointly classifying events and estimating their onsets and offsets. Before tokenization, we apply FOCAL, an event-aware sparse attention module that uses predicted event presence to guide temporal feature refinement and spatial aggregation, emphasizing likely event periods while preserving context across the recording. Experiments on eight EEG datasets that span diverse paradigms demonstrate state-of-the-art performance. These findings provide a basis for the development of BCI systems that recognize meaningful neural activity during continuous interaction and monitoring.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Asynchronous EEG event detection supports practical brain-computer interfaces by identifying event classes and temporal boundaries directly from continuous recordings without predefined event boundaries at inference time. The primary challenge lies in extracting event-related patterns embedded in ongoing background activity. To address this, we propose \textbf{NeuroDETR}, a discrete spatiotemporal multiscale framework that combines structured tokenization with event-aware feature refinement. Multiscale temporal codebooks capture brief responses and sustained dynamics, while Spatial-Pyramid VQ hierarchically encodes spatial structure across EEG channels. These complementary representations form a compact event memory for jointly classifying events and estimating their onsets and offsets. Before tokenization, we apply FOCAL, an event-aware sparse attention module that uses predicted event presence to guide temporal feature refinement and spatial aggregation, emphasizing likely event periods while preserving context across the recording. Experiments on eight EEG datasets that span diverse paradigms demonstrate state-of-the-art performance. These findings provide a basis for the development of BCI systems that recognize meaningful neural activity during continuous interaction and monitoring.

</details>

## 59. Glance Forcing: Making Bidirectional Diffusion Autoregressive at a Glance

**中文标题**: Glance Forcing: Making Bidirectional Diffusion Autoregressive at a Glance

**作者**: Zhuobai Dong, Junchao Yi, Hu Jian Guo, Linjie Li, Rui Zhao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20220v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20220v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20220v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autoregressive generation underlies real-time, causal video synthesis and is the backbone of video world models. Today's dominant approach distills a bidirectional diffusion model into a causal few-step student, but existing pipelines make this conversion prohibitively expensive, requiring offline Probability Flow ODE} (PF-ODE) trajectories that cost tens of thousands of GPU-hours and terabytes of storage. We show it can be made dramatically more efficient. Pushing this efficiency to its extreme, we reduce trajectory supervision to a single sample, the strictest setting possible, and find that it causes severe quality collapse. We trace this to the teacher's trajectory geometry: the high-noise regime, governing semantic layout and motion, and the low-noise regime, governing fine texture exhibit fundamentally different curvature and optimization behavior, so a single monolithic student cannot jointly fit both, causing severe representation interference. Motivated by this, we propose Glance Forcing, a lightweight distillation framework that decouples the denoising trajectory into two complementary slow and fast experts, each guided by a tailored, stage-specific objective, while keeping the pretrained teacher entirely frozen. This decoupling further reveals a general mechanism underlying few-step distillation: PF-ODE distillation mainly straightens the few-step trajectory geometry, while the subsequent distribution-matching stage independently governs distributional quality. Using only one PF-ODE trajectory, Glance Forcing already surpasses the fully-trained state-of-the-art baseline, raising the VBench Total score from 84.04 to 84.22 while cutting trajectory-collection cost from thousands of GPU-hours to about one A100-GPU-hour, with markedly larger gains in dynamic degree, VisionReward, and instruction-following, and consistent preference from human raters over strong baselines.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Autoregressive generation underlies real-time, causal video synthesis and is the backbone of video world models. Today's dominant approach distills a bidirectional diffusion model into a causal few-step student, but existing pipelines make this conversion prohibitively expensive, requiring offline Probability Flow ODE} (PF-ODE) trajectories that cost tens of thousands of GPU-hours and terabytes of storage. We show it can be made dramatically more efficient. Pushing this efficiency to its extreme, we reduce trajectory supervision to a single sample, the strictest setting possible, and find that it causes severe quality collapse. We trace this to the teacher's trajectory geometry: the high-noise regime, governing semantic layout and motion, and the low-noise regime, governing fine texture exhibit fundamentally different curvature and optimization behavior, so a single monolithic student cannot jointly fit both, causing severe representation interference. Motivated by this, we propose Glance Forcing, a lightweight distillation framework that decouples the denoising trajectory into two complementary slow and fast experts, each guided by a tailored, stage-specific objective, while keeping the pretrained teacher entirely frozen. This decoupling further reveals a general mechanism underlying few-step distillation: PF-ODE distillation mainly straightens the few-step trajectory geometry, while the subsequent distribution-matching stage independently governs distributional quality. Using only one PF-ODE trajectory, Glance Forcing already surpasses the fully-trained state-of-the-art baseline, raising the VBench Total score from 84.04 to 84.22 while cutting trajectory-collection cost from thousands of GPU-hours to about one A100-GPU-hour, with markedly larger gains in dynamic degree, VisionReward, and instruction-following, and consistent preference from human raters over strong baselines.

</details>

---

## 60. Attention-Based Spatio-Temporal Approach for Fire and Smoke Detection

**中文标题**: Attention-Based Spatio-Temporal Approach for Fire and Smoke Detection

**作者**: Alireza Mirrashid, Mohammad Khoshbin, Ali Atghaei, Hassan Shahbazi

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20141v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20141v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20141v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In various industries, smoke and fire are two of the most important threats in the workplace. One of the common methods for detecting smoke and fire is the use of infrared thermal and smoke sensors, which cannot be used in outdoor applications. Therefore, the use of vision-based methods seems necessary. The problem of smoke and fire detection is spatiotemporal and requires spatiotemporal solutions. This paper presents a method that uses spatial features along with temporal-based features to detect smoke and fire in the scene. It consists of three main parts; the task of each part is to reduce the error of the previous part so that the final model has a robust performance. This method also uses transformer modules to increase the accuracy of the model. The results of our model show the proper performance of the proposed approach in solving the problem of smoke and fire detection and can be used to increase workplace safety.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

One of the common methods for detecting smoke and fire is the use of infrared thermal and smoke sensors, which cannot be used in outdoor applications. Therefore, the use of vision-based methods seems necessary. The problem of smoke and fire detection is spatiotemporal and requires spatiotemporal solutions. This paper presents a method that uses spatial features along with temporal-based features to detect smoke and fire in the scene. It consists of three main parts; the task of each part is to reduce the error of the previous part so that the final model has a robust performance. This method also uses transformer modules to increase the accuracy of the model. The results of our model show the proper performance of the proposed approach in solving the problem of smoke and fire detection and can be used to increase workplace safety.

</details>

## 61. StreamErase: Towards Ultra-Fast Streaming Video Erasing

**中文标题**: StreamErase: Towards Ultra-Fast Streaming Video Erasing

**作者**: Zhuobai Dong, Xiaobing Tu, Xiantao Zhang, Jinkui Ren, Yinggui Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20137v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20137v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20137v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Current video object removal methods suffer from prohibitive latency (minutes per video) and non-causal processing that prevents real-time interaction. We present StreamErase, enabling sub-second latency with up to 26 FPS streaming generation on a single GPU. Our approach first augments a text-to-video model with mask input to generate high-quality videos that erase target objects and synthesize coherent backgrounds, but does not perform inference on the fly. As such, we progressively transfer the erasing capability from a powerful bidirectional foundation model to an efficient unidirectional streaming eraser, enabling long-horizon erasing without sacrificing visual fidelity. Several key challenges arise when generating videos over long, potentially infinite horizons: (1) bridging the domain gap from training on finite length and extrapolating to long horizons, (2) sustaining high quality by preventing error accumulation, and (3) maintaining fast inference, without increasing computational cost as the context grows. To address these challenges, we carefully designed sliding-window causal attention with attention sinks. By incorporating self-rollout with attention sinks and KV cache rolling during training, we simulate inference-time extrapolations with a fixed context window, enabling constant-speed generation of arbitrarily long videos. Finally, we establish a dedicated benchmark, LongErase, for streaming long-video object removal. Our model achieves strong performance compared to existing video object erasing models and generalizes well to real-world video scenarios, uniquely enabling infinite-length streaming. With StreamErase, users can input a mask and see the erasing results unfold in real-time, delivering a truly interactive experience.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

As such, we progressively transfer the erasing capability from a powerful bidirectional foundation model to an efficient unidirectional streaming eraser, enabling long-horizon erasing without sacrificing visual fidelity. To address these challenges, we carefully designed sliding-window causal attention with attention sinks. By incorporating self-rollout with attention sinks and KV cache rolling during training, we simulate inference-time extrapolations with a fixed context window, enabling constant-speed generation of arbitrarily long videos. Finally, we establish a dedicated benchmark, LongErase, for streaming long-video object removal. Our model achieves strong performance compared to existing video object erasing models and generalizes well to real-world video scenarios, uniquely enabling infinite-length streaming. With StreamErase, users can input a mask and see the erasing results unfold in real-time, delivering a truly interactive experience.

</details>

## 62. DreamHMR: From Video Generation to Human Motion Modeling via Structural Focusing

**中文标题**: DreamHMR: From Video Generation to Human Motion Modeling via Structural Focusing

**作者**: Zhengjie Xu, Ruihao Gong, Xianglong Liu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20124v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20124v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20124v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Human mesh recovery (HMR) provides a geometric foundation for understanding human motion, but accurately reconstructing both body motion and fine hand articulation from videos remains challenging, especially in real-world scenes where occlusion and partial visibility are common. Video generation pretraining offers rich spatial and temporal priors for this task, but its focus on visual synthesis leaves a gap between these representations and explicit human geometry. We present DreamHMR, a general framework that adapts video generation priors for complete body-and-hand reconstruction. It follows a global-to-local strategy, first grounding video representations in human geometry and then refining body and hand detail with reliable local evidence. Global Body Representation Transfer trains the video backbone to predict complete human renderings, turning its native prediction task into a means of learning surface correspondence and posed geometry. Local Joint Feature Enhancement combines contextual estimates with reliable local evidence to recover fine body and hand detail, while limiting interference from occluders and background. Experiments on five datasets with four video backbones demonstrate strong reconstruction performance across backbone families. Under a unified evaluation protocol, our best model outperforms the best existing methods on all reported metrics of 3DPW, EMDB-1, and RICH, reducing body MPJPE by 4.2%, 4.7%, and 6.1%, respectively. In more challenging occlusion settings on held-out BEDLAM and EgoBody sequences, DreamHMR reduces overall body and hand MPJPE by 7.0% and 10.0%, respectively. Our code will be made publicly available.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Human mesh recovery (HMR) provides a geometric foundation for understanding human motion, but accurately reconstructing both body motion and fine hand articulation from videos remains challenging, especially in real-world scenes where occlusion and partial visibility are common. Video generation pretraining offers rich spatial and temporal priors for this task, but its focus on visual synthesis leaves a gap between these representations and explicit human geometry. We present DreamHMR, a general framework that adapts video generation priors for complete body-and-hand reconstruction. It follows a global-to-local strategy, first grounding video representations in human geometry and then refining body and hand detail with reliable local evidence. Global Body Representation Transfer trains the video backbone to predict complete human renderings, turning its native prediction task into a means of learning surface correspondence and posed geometry. Local Joint Feature Enhancement combines contextual estimates with reliable local evidence to recover fine body and hand detail, while limiting interference from occluders and background. Experiments on five datasets with four video backbones demonstrate strong reconstruction performance across backbone families. Under a unified evaluation protocol, our best model outperforms the best existing methods on all reported metrics of 3DPW, EMDB-1, and RICH, reducing body MPJPE by 4.2%, 4.7%, and 6.1%, respectively. In more challenging occlusion settings on held-out BEDLAM and EgoBody sequences, DreamHMR reduces overall body and hand MPJPE by 7.0% and 10.0%, respectively. Our code will be made publicly available.

</details>

## 63. RepForcing: Representation-Space Initialization for Autoregressive Video Distillation

**中文标题**: RepForcing: Representation-Space Initialization for Autoregressive Video Distillation

**作者**: Siming Fu, Zheming Fu, Lianrui Mu, Haoyang Huang, Chaojie Gu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20111v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20111v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20111v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Distilling pretrained video diffusion models into few-step autoregressive (AR) generators typically combines student initialization with distribution matching distillation (DMD). An intermediate AR teacher can provide causally compatible targets for ODE initialization. However, causally compatible targets alone do not ensure high-quality generation under a short sampling schedule. We analyze the posterior-mean predictions favored by direct latent regression and empirically find that causal ODE initialization alleviates blur but still leaves fine detail unresolved. To address this limitation, we propose Representation Forcing, which learns few-step causal initialization through representation-space supervision rather than intermediate-teacher trajectory regression. The student is trained with teacher forcing by comparing clean predictions and training targets through normalized features of a frozen video diffusion transformer. This phase reuses the synthetic videos generated by a bidirectional model for AR teacher training and replaces both teacher training and causal ODE distillation, without generating additional causal ODE trajectories. The subsequent asymmetric DMD procedure remains unchanged. Empirical results show that, at four sampling steps, RepForcing better preserves generation quality than intermediate-teacher-based initialization before DMD. After DMD, our four-step chunk-wise model improves VBench Total from 84.04 to 84.66 (+0.62 points) and VisionReward from 6.326 to 10.074 (+59.25%) over the reported causal-ODE-based baseline.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Distilling pretrained video diffusion models into few-step autoregressive (AR) generators typically combines student initialization with distribution matching distillation (DMD). An intermediate AR teacher can provide causally compatible targets for ODE initialization. However, causally compatible targets alone do not ensure high-quality generation under a short sampling schedule. We analyze the posterior-mean predictions favored by direct latent regression and empirically find that causal ODE initialization alleviates blur but still leaves fine detail unresolved. To address this limitation, we propose Representation Forcing, which learns few-step causal initialization through representation-space supervision rather than intermediate-teacher trajectory regression. The student is trained with teacher forcing by comparing clean predictions and training targets through normalized features of a frozen video diffusion transformer. This phase reuses the synthetic videos generated by a bidirectional model for AR teacher training and replaces both teacher training and causal ODE distillation, without generating additional causal ODE trajectories. The subsequent asymmetric DMD procedure remains unchanged. Empirical results show that, at four sampling steps, RepForcing better preserves generation quality than intermediate-teacher-based initialization before DMD. After DMD, our four-step chunk-wise model improves VBench Total from 84.04 to 84.66 (+0.62 points) and VisionReward from 6.326 to 10.074 (+59.25%) over the reported causal-ODE-based baseline.

</details>

## 64. RTAformer: Relation-Transition-Aware Transformer for Video Scene Graph Generation

**中文标题**: RTAformer: Relation-Transition-Aware Transformer for Video Scene Graph Generation

**作者**: Jaeyeon Heo, Geuntaek Lim, Yukyung Choi

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20091v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20091v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20091v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video scene graph generation (VidSGG) aims to capture objects and their evolving relationships in videos. This requires accounting for both the persistence of object identities and abrupt changes in relationships. Existing methods aggregate information across frames to exploit temporal context, but an emphasis on consistency can obscure these changes. As a result, a model may retain its previous prediction even after the underlying relation has changed. We refer to this failure mode as relational semantic inertia. To address this issue, we propose Relation-Transition-Aware Transformer (RTAformer), a one-stage framework that separately models entity persistence and relation changes. It injects calibrated differences between adjacent-frame relation queries to guide predicate updates, while cross-frame instance alignment during training helps reduce cross-frame representational drift. Experiments on Action Genome show improvements in recall and mean recall over existing methods, with ablation studies supporting the contributions of the proposed components and comparisons using matched backbones showing larger gains on relation-transition frames than on non-transition frames.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video scene graph generation (VidSGG) aims to capture objects and their evolving relationships in videos. This requires accounting for both the persistence of object identities and abrupt changes in relationships. Existing methods aggregate information across frames to exploit temporal context, but an emphasis on consistency can obscure these changes. As a result, a model may retain its previous prediction even after the underlying relation has changed. We refer to this failure mode as relational semantic inertia. To address this issue, we propose Relation-Transition-Aware Transformer (RTAformer), a one-stage framework that separately models entity persistence and relation changes. It injects calibrated differences between adjacent-frame relation queries to guide predicate updates, while cross-frame instance alignment during training helps reduce cross-frame representational drift. Experiments on Action Genome show improvements in recall and mean recall over existing methods, with ablation studies supporting the contributions of the proposed components and comparisons using matched backbones showing larger gains on relation-transition frames than on non-transition frames.

</details>

## 65. When Pairwise Preferences Cycle: A Reliability-Anchored Preference Walk for Video GRPO

**中文标题**: When Pairwise Preferences Cycle: A Reliability-Anchored Preference Walk for Video GRPO

**作者**: Xinrui Lin, Fan Li, Shumin Wang, Dehong Kong, Yanyong Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20089v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20089v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20089v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Pairwise preference feedback has become a widely adopted supervision signal for reinforcement learning in video generation models, as pairwise comparisons provide a more reliable alternative than pointwise scalar ratings for evaluating the quality of generated videos. Specifically, we define a preference graph for each GRPO group under a pairwise evaluator, where nodes represent sampled rollouts and edges encode pairwise preferences. Auditing 6,976 on-policy comparison graphs, we find that cyclic preferences are prevalent and persist throughout training. Naive pairwise reward aggregation based on average win rates discards the underlying comparison structures and treats each rollout independently, which may introduce unreliable advantage estimates for certain rollouts and consequently affect reinforcement learning optimization. Therefore, we propose the \emph{Reliability-Anchored Preference Walk} (\ours{}), a graph-aware advantage aggregation method that strengthens the global preference signal from win rates with reliable relational evidence from cyclic preference graphs through a reliability-guided preference walk. Under matched evaluator, comparison protocol, group size, prompt schedule, and query budget, \ours{} requires no additional evaluator queries beyond the observed comparisons within each rollout group and improves over Pairwise-GRPO across controlled comparisons, transfer settings, and blind human evaluations.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Pairwise preference feedback has become a widely adopted supervision signal for reinforcement learning in video generation models, as pairwise comparisons provide a more reliable alternative than pointwise scalar ratings for evaluating the quality of generated videos. Specifically, we define a preference graph for each GRPO group under a pairwise evaluator, where nodes represent sampled rollouts and edges encode pairwise preferences. Auditing 6,976 on-policy comparison graphs, we find that cyclic preferences are prevalent and persist throughout training. Naive pairwise reward aggregation based on average win rates discards the underlying comparison structures and treats each rollout independently, which may introduce unreliable advantage estimates for certain rollouts and consequently affect reinforcement learning optimization. Therefore, we propose the \emph{Reliability-Anchored Preference Walk} (\ours{}), a graph-aware advantage aggregation method that strengthens the global preference signal from win rates with reliable relational evidence from cyclic preference graphs through a reliability-guided preference walk. Under matched evaluator, comparison protocol, group size, prompt schedule, and query budget, \ours{} requires no additional evaluator queries beyond the observed comparisons within each rollout group and improves over Pairwise-GRPO across controlled comparisons, transfer settings, and blind human evaluations.

</details>

## 66. Complementary Future Token Pruning for World Action Models

**中文标题**: Complementary Future Token Pruning for World Action Models

**作者**: Baekseung Kim, Junhoo Lee, Sohyeon Hong, Suhyun Jeon, Minkyu Kim et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19957v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19957v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19957v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) jointly predict future visual states and robot actions, but processing dense future-frame tokens incurs substantial inference latency. We propose CF-Prune, a training-free framework for pruning future-frame tokens in pretrained WAMs. Our key insight is that future visual predictions support action generation and therefore need not preserve every visual detail required for high-fidelity video synthesis. CF-Prune combines instruction and action attention to identify task-relevant tokens during the initial denoising forward pass, even when future-frame and action representations remain highly noisy. An entropy-based phase identification mechanism further retains dense computation during precision phases to mitigate pruning-induced performance degradation. Experiments on RoboLab, RoboCasa, and real-world manipulation tasks demonstrate consistent inference speedups. At a 35% pruning ratio, CF-Prune achieves average speedups of 1.19-1.27x while improving task success rates. At a 50% pruning ratio, it achieves 1.29-1.33x average speedups on both simulation benchmarks, with overall success rates within 1.0 percentage point of the baseline.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) jointly predict future visual states and robot actions, but processing dense future-frame tokens incurs substantial inference latency. We propose CF-Prune, a training-free framework for pruning future-frame tokens in pretrained WAMs. Our key insight is that future visual predictions support action generation and therefore need not preserve every visual detail required for high-fidelity video synthesis. CF-Prune combines instruction and action attention to identify task-relevant tokens during the initial denoising forward pass, even when future-frame and action representations remain highly noisy. An entropy-based phase identification mechanism further retains dense computation during precision phases to mitigate pruning-induced performance degradation. Experiments on RoboLab, RoboCasa, and real-world manipulation tasks demonstrate consistent inference speedups. At a 35% pruning ratio, CF-Prune achieves average speedups of 1.19-1.27x while improving task success rates. At a 50% pruning ratio, it achieves 1.29-1.33x average speedups on both simulation benchmarks, with overall success rates within 1.0 percentage point of the baseline.

</details>

## 67. SAM3RL: Memory Control via Reinforcement Learning for Visual Object Tracking

**中文标题**: SAM3RL: Memory Control via Reinforcement Learning for Visual Object Tracking

**作者**: Tomáš Čížek, Matej Straka, Illia Volkov, Matěj Kripner, Ervin Macić et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19931v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19931v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19931v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Segment Anything Model 3 (SAM 3) is a foundation model for image and video segmentation that sets the state of the art in visual object tracking. Its tracker, inherited from SAM 2, stores information from past frames in a memory bank, managed by a simple First-In-First-Out rule, allowing temporal consistency in complex videos. Several recent methods improve SAM 2 with hand-crafted memory update rules, each targeting a specific failure mode, such as distractors, occlusions, or object motion. However, their gains transfer only partially to the stronger SAM 3 backbone. We instead present a more general, data-driven approach that frames memory control as a sequential decision-making task. SAM3RL is an extension of the SAM 3 tracker with a lightweight memory controller, trained by reinforcement learning, that decides which frames enter the memory bank in order to maximize tracking performance. SAM3RL sets a new state of the art on 10 of 12 tracking and video object segmentation benchmarks, outperforming all hand-crafted rules without any runtime overhead. The same approach applied to SAM 2 gives SAM2RL, which outperforms hand-crafted rules on 9 of the 12 benchmarks, showing the generality of our method across model generations.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Segment Anything Model 3 (SAM 3) is a foundation model for image and video segmentation that sets the state of the art in visual object tracking. Its tracker, inherited from SAM 2, stores information from past frames in a memory bank, managed by a simple First-In-First-Out rule, allowing temporal consistency in complex videos. Several recent methods improve SAM 2 with hand-crafted memory update rules, each targeting a specific failure mode, such as distractors, occlusions, or object motion. However, their gains transfer only partially to the stronger SAM 3 backbone. We instead present a more general, data-driven approach that frames memory control as a sequential decision-making task. SAM3RL is an extension of the SAM 3 tracker with a lightweight memory controller, trained by reinforcement learning, that decides which frames enter the memory bank in order to maximize tracking performance. SAM3RL sets a new state of the art on 10 of 12 tracking and video object segmentation benchmarks, outperforming all hand-crafted rules without any runtime overhead. The same approach applied to SAM 2 gives SAM2RL, which outperforms hand-crafted rules on 9 of the 12 benchmarks, showing the generality of our method across model generations.

</details>

## 68. CoDy: Codec Dynamics as Complementary Priors for Unsupervised Temporal Action Localization

**中文标题**: CoDy: Codec Dynamics as Complementary Priors for Unsupervised Temporal Action Localization

**作者**: Tianyu Yan, Qingyi Yang, Qin Yang, Xufei Guo, Yuan Tang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19880v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19880v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19880v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Unsupervised temporal action localization (UTAL) aims to identify action categories and localize their temporal boundaries without annotation supervision. Existing methods typically derive supervision for both tasks solely from RGB and optical-flow representations, often resulting in noisy category assignments and inaccurate temporal boundaries. In this work, we reveal an overlooked property of video codecs: group-of-pictures quantization patterns preserve category-discriminative dynamics, while motion-vector and residual variations exhibit pronounced responses around action transitions. Based on this insight, we propose CoDy, a codec dynamics-guided framework that exploits the codec as complementary information to the visual view for UTAL. CoDy integrates a codec-enhanced view with RGB and flow through confidence-aware consensus clustering, producing reliable soft pseudo-labels. It further converts codec dynamics into a class-agnostic boundary prior, which supervises visual boundary learning and guides proposal initialization and iterative refinement. The codec branches are only used during the training stage and introduce no extra codec-side computation at inference. Extensive experiments on THUMOS14 and ActivityNet v1.3 demonstrate that CoDy consistently outperforms existing UTAL methods and achieves state-of-the-art localization performance. These results establish codec signals as effective annotation-free priors for jointly inferring which actions occur and when they occur.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Unsupervised temporal action localization (UTAL) aims to identify action categories and localize their temporal boundaries without annotation supervision. Existing methods typically derive supervision for both tasks solely from RGB and optical-flow representations, often resulting in noisy category assignments and inaccurate temporal boundaries. In this work, we reveal an overlooked property of video codecs: group-of-pictures quantization patterns preserve category-discriminative dynamics, while motion-vector and residual variations exhibit pronounced responses around action transitions. Based on this insight, we propose CoDy, a codec dynamics-guided framework that exploits the codec as complementary information to the visual view for UTAL. CoDy integrates a codec-enhanced view with RGB and flow through confidence-aware consensus clustering, producing reliable soft pseudo-labels. These results establish codec signals as effective annotation-free priors for jointly inferring which actions occur and when they occur.

</details>

## 69. DeltaStreamer: Towards Human-Like Agentic Streaming Video Understanding

**中文标题**: DeltaStreamer: Towards Human-Like Agentic Streaming Video Understanding

**作者**: Boyuan Sun, Deshui Miao, Wenzhao Gao, Shaoyong Jia, Shaohui Jiao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19849v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19849v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19849v1)

**项目主页**: https://bbbbchan.github.io/DeltaStreamer/

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.HC

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We propose DeltaStreamer, a novel agentic framework for streaming video understanding inspired by how humans observe, remember, and investigate. Streaming video understanding requires a system to retain evidence before knowing which details future queries will need. Existing approaches often obscure event transitions and omit relevant details when compressing video history, and cannot recover missing visual evidence. DeltaStreamer addresses these limitations by combining change-aware memory with query-driven planning and targeted evidence gathering with replay. Before a query arrives, it continuously observes the stream and explicitly records visual changes in structured memory, preserving how events unfold rather than only what is present. When a query arrives, a query-driven planner identifies the required evidence and guides memory retrieval and targeted replay. By revisiting historical frames, the agent can recover visual details omitted during observation and ground its answer in evidence beyond the retained descriptions. This connects continuous observation with active investigation tailored to each question. Experiments on OVO-Bench and StreamingBench demonstrate improvements over representative streaming video understanding methods, with overall scores of 66.07% and 59.53%, respectively.   Project page: https://bbbbchan.github.io/DeltaStreamer/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Project page: https://bbbbchan.github.io/DeltaStreamer/

</details>

## 70. PhyMetric: Diagnosing Physical Plausibility in Text-to-Video Generation via Scene-Level QA

**中文标题**: PhyMetric: Diagnosing Physical Plausibility in Text-to-Video Generation via Scene-Level QA

**作者**: Siwei Meng, Xuerou Cai, Rui Hu, Ping Liu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19823v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19823v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19823v1)

**项目主页**: https://mengsiwei.github.io/PhyMetric/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Text-to-video (T2V) generation models can produce visually realistic videos that nevertheless violate the physical consequences implied by the prompt and scene. Existing benchmarks largely rely on holistic plausibility scores or broad quality dimensions, leaving it unclear which physical behavior is being tested and why a model fails. We introduce PhyMetric, a scene-level diagnostic benchmark that recasts physical plausibility evaluation as verifiable, scene-grounded question answering. PhyMetric contains 1{,}115 scenes spanning 50 physical categories across six domains, paired with 8{,}920 answer-balanced binary questions over 12 evaluation dimensions. Each scene is associated with eight targeted yes/no questions and a structured physical prior specifying the governing law, expected temporal sequence, observable signatures, and common failure modes. At evaluation time, a vision-language model (VLM) judge answers each question from question-aware sampled frames conditioned on the prior, and correctness is hierarchically aggregated into scene, dimension, physical category, and model scores. Experiments on four open-source T2V models and three VLM judges show that scene-specific binary QA is a critical component of the protocol: when each targeted question is replaced by a single generic plausibility query, evaluator accuracy drops to the chance level. Structured physical priors offer a complementary signal that improves human alignment of the resulting scores, with effects that vary across VLM judges of different capacity. These findings establish PhyMetric as a benchmark that turns physical plausibility evaluation from opaque holistic scoring into interpretable, scene-level inspection. Our project page is available at: https://mengsiwei.github.io/PhyMetric/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 71. StateCraft: Augmenting Video World Models with Abstract-State Reasoning

**中文标题**: StateCraft: Augmenting Video World Models with Abstract-State Reasoning

**作者**: Lorenzo Venturoli, Nedko Savov, Xi Wang, Marc Pollefeys, Luc Van Gool

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19795v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19795v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19795v1)

**项目主页**: https://insait-institute.github.io/StateCraft/.

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video world models aim to simulate complex environments with realistic appearance and dynamics. Maintaining consistency over long interactions requires tracking past actions and understanding how those actions affect the environment. Video world models can only generate partial observations of the entire world at a time. Remembering past observations alone may not be sufficient in every scenario, as they may no longer reflect the world after a sequence of actions. We introduce a paradigm that decouples a persistent, updatable abstract state from the visual state. The abstract state keeps track of the entities' changes as the world evolves, and is used to condition the visual state, which represents the current view. We propose StateCraft, a novel architecture that pairs a language model with a video diffusion model to evolve the dynamic world. We evaluate our approach on generative interfaces, which pose a substantial challenge because a single action may change parts of the state that are not currently visible. To support training and evaluation, we develop a data collection pipeline that produces informative, densely annotated trajectories at scale without human or agent supervision, together with a benchmark measuring generation quality, memory, and controllability. On this benchmark, our approach improves surpasses the state-of-the-art baselines. We demonstrate that StateCraft maintains high self-consistency and strong generation quality over long trajectories. Because the abstract state is explicit, it can be edited directly, and generation follows edits to states never observed in the data. Finally, a proof-of-concept 3D experiment, in which actions change the illumination of an unobserved room, shows that the paradigm can also be applied to spatial environments. Project page: https://insait-institute.github.io/StateCraft/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Project page: https://insait-institute.github.io/StateCraft/.

## 72. FPV-CRASH: A Dataset for Offline Crash Moment Localization in Drone First-Person-View Videos

**中文标题**: FPV-CRASH: A Dataset for Offline Crash Moment Localization in Drone First-Person-View Videos

**作者**: Jifan Zhang, Tingyu Wang, Qian Liu, Zhedong Zheng

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19785v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19785v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19785v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Crash Moment Localization aims to localize the major physical impact that interrupts controlled flight in an onboard first-person-view (FPV) video recorded by a drone, thereby providing a temporal reference for retrospective crash analysis in aerial robotics. However, existing crash-video datasets predominantly target surveillance or dash-camera footage, and their annotation schemes and task formulations are not designed for temporal localization of the major physical impact in UAV onboard FPV videos. To address this gap, we present FPV-CRASH, a dataset and fixed-protocol reference evaluation for offline Crash Moment Localization in drone FPV videos. FPV-CRASH contains 1,052 drone FPV clips (526 Crash and 526 Normal); each Crash clip has a single-frame reference annotation for the Crash Moment, and every clip is paired with four English captions automatically generated from sampled frames and the known Crash/Normal label. Normal clips carry no timestamp target and provide training-time background supervision. The captions support an auxiliary Text-to-Video Retrieval task, whereas localization inference remains video-only. We further provide a heatmap-based reference model that estimates a continuous Crash Moment timestamp from sparsely sampled frames. Under the fixed, non-source-disjoint clip-level protocol, the reference model reduces MAE from 1.12 s for the strongest fixed timing-prior baseline to 0.56 s (a 50% relative reduction) and achieves a median absolute error (MedAE) of 0.33 s; the fraction of clips localized within 0.5 s rises from 37.14% to 65.71%. The release includes annotations, available source video identifiers and URLs, clipping metadata, fixed split lists, and preprocessing code so that the reportable portion can be reconstructed without redistributing source footage. At submission time, all source-compilation URLs remained accessible.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

At submission time, all source-compilation URLs remained accessible.

</details>

## 73. Mitigating Video Hallucinations via Attention-Guided Representation Perturbation

**中文标题**: Mitigating Video Hallucinations via Attention-Guided Representation Perturbation

**作者**: Zhixin Cai, Ruoqi Yuan, Xinchen Yang, Zhuofan Chen, Jun Bai et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19728v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19728v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19728v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video large language models (Video LLMs) often generate responses unsupported by the input video. Contrastive decoding (CD) can mitigate these hallucinations, but its effectiveness depends on how the negative branch weakens visual evidence. Existing methods rely on predefined perturbations or masking guided by question reconstruction, offering limited control over the model's visual reliance during answer generation. We introduce Attention-guided Representation Perturbation for Contrastive Decoding (ARP-CD), a training-free framework for constructing contrastive negatives directly in representation space. ARP-CD uses the attention mass assigned to visual tokens at the answer-prediction position to guide bounded gradient updates of the language model's input visual hidden states. These updates reduce visual attention and produce a negative input, whose predictions are contrasted with those of the original branch throughout generation. Experiments across four Video LLMs and four benchmarks show that ARP-CD outperforms representative training-free baselines in most model--benchmark settings, reducing hallucinations while improving general video understanding. Further analyses show that attention-guided perturbation directions improve negative quality and that the resulting negatives provide effective contrastive references for correcting the original predictions.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video large language models (Video LLMs) often generate responses unsupported by the input video. Contrastive decoding (CD) can mitigate these hallucinations, but its effectiveness depends on how the negative branch weakens visual evidence. Existing methods rely on predefined perturbations or masking guided by question reconstruction, offering limited control over the model's visual reliance during answer generation. We introduce Attention-guided Representation Perturbation for Contrastive Decoding (ARP-CD), a training-free framework for constructing contrastive negatives directly in representation space. ARP-CD uses the attention mass assigned to visual tokens at the answer-prediction position to guide bounded gradient updates of the language model's input visual hidden states. These updates reduce visual attention and produce a negative input, whose predictions are contrasted with those of the original branch throughout generation. Experiments across four Video LLMs and four benchmarks show that ARP-CD outperforms representative training-free baselines in most model--benchmark settings, reducing hallucinations while improving general video understanding. Further analyses show that attention-guided perturbation directions improve negative quality and that the resulting negatives provide effective contrastive references for correcting the original predictions.

</details>
