## 74. Enhancing Latent World Models with Associative Memory

**中文标题**: Enhancing Latent World Models with Associative Memory

**作者**: Yuwei Sun, Siyu Zhu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19683v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19683v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19683v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World models trained via latent dynamics offer a scalable path for embodied planning, yet existing methods suffer from representational collapse, computational overhead, and limited long-horizon capability. We introduce a memory-enhanced latent world model that augments JEPA-style prediction with a content-addressable memory bank. Our approach learns high-level representations directly from observations, operating at multiple temporal scales within a shared latent space, and conditions predictions via adaptive layer normalization. To prevent collapse and ensure effective memory usage, we design two complementary losses that encourage slot diversity and balanced temporal utilization. Experiments on PushT, Tworoom, Reacher, and OGBCube demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance with a lightweight architecture, outperforming conventional latent world models on long-horizon planning tasks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World models trained via latent dynamics offer a scalable path for embodied planning, yet existing methods suffer from representational collapse, computational overhead, and limited long-horizon capability. We introduce a memory-enhanced latent world model that augments JEPA-style prediction with a content-addressable memory bank. Our approach learns high-level representations directly from observations, operating at multiple temporal scales within a shared latent space, and conditions predictions via adaptive layer normalization. To prevent collapse and ensure effective memory usage, we design two complementary losses that encourage slot diversity and balanced temporal utilization. Experiments on PushT, Tworoom, Reacher, and OGBCube demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance with a lightweight architecture, outperforming conventional latent world models on long-horizon planning tasks.

</details>

## 75. Mapping Arctic Polynyas from Sea Ice Concentration Data Using Hierarchical Partitioning and Differentiated Region Growing: Sensitivity to Threshold and Product Choices

**中文标题**: Mapping Arctic Polynyas from Sea Ice Concentration Data Using Hierarchical Partitioning and Differentiated Region Growing: Sensitivity to Threshold and Product Choices

**作者**: Tianyu Zhang, Haiyi Ren, Zhilun Zhang, Yang Hong, Chunlei Mi et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19647v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19647v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19647v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Accurately delineating Arctic coastal polynyas from sea ice concentration data remains challenging because of complex coastal geometry and spatially heterogeneous ice conditions. We develop a daily sea ice concentration (SIC) mapping framework based on hierarchical partitioning, connected-component analysis, and differentiated region growing for polynya and open-water regions. Applied to September-May ice seasons from 2002/03 to 2024/25, the framework achieves a mean overall accuracy of 96.74% and a mean kappa coefficient of 0.93 in the 63-scene visual assessment. Sensitivity experiments reveal a consistent pattern: the absolute magnitude of SIC-defined polynya extent is strongly configuration-dependent, whereas major temporal features are comparatively more stable. Relative to a 75% upper SIC threshold, seasonal extent integrals differ by 13.48%, 32.47%, and 65.04% at thresholds of 80%, 85%, and 90%, respectively, while the broad interannual structure remains recognizable. The response is also strongly regional and seasonal, with the largest threshold sensitivity in the Central Arctic Ocean, very weak sensitivity in the Gulf of St. Lawrence, and greater pan-Arctic curve separation from January onward. At an 80% threshold, product-comparison MAPE is 68.19% between the 6.25 and 25 km products and 11.47% between the 12.5 and 25 km products under their respective comparison periods and reference definitions. Regional product disagreement is likewise heterogeneous. Together, these results show that reproducible long-term polynya monitoring requires a fixed and explicitly documented combination of SIC product, threshold, and spatial classification rules, while broad temporal patterns can remain informative even when absolute extent differs substantially.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Accurately delineating Arctic coastal polynyas from sea ice concentration data remains challenging because of complex coastal geometry and spatially heterogeneous ice conditions. We develop a daily sea ice concentration (SIC) mapping framework based on hierarchical partitioning, connected-component analysis, and differentiated region growing for polynya and open-water regions. Applied to September-May ice seasons from 2002/03 to 2024/25, the framework achieves a mean overall accuracy of 96.74% and a mean kappa coefficient of 0.93 in the 63-scene visual assessment. Sensitivity experiments reveal a consistent pattern: the absolute magnitude of SIC-defined polynya extent is strongly configuration-dependent, whereas major temporal features are comparatively more stable. Relative to a 75% upper SIC threshold, seasonal extent integrals differ by 13.48%, 32.47%, and 65.04% at thresholds of 80%, 85%, and 90%, respectively, while the broad interannual structure remains recognizable. The response is also strongly regional and seasonal, with the largest threshold sensitivity in the Central Arctic Ocean, very weak sensitivity in the Gulf of St. Lawrence, and greater pan-Arctic curve separation from January onward. At an 80% threshold, product-comparison MAPE is 68.19% between the 6.25 and 25 km products and 11.47% between the 12.5 and 25 km products under their respective comparison periods and reference definitions. Regional product disagreement is likewise heterogeneous. Together, these results show that reproducible long-term polynya monitoring requires a fixed and explicitly documented combination of SIC product, threshold, and spatial classification rules, while broad temporal patterns can remain informative even when absolute extent differs substantially.

</details>

## 76. HyrCap: Hybrid Rank-Calibration of Action Proposals for Temporal Event Understanding

**中文标题**: HyrCap: Hybrid Rank-Calibration of Action Proposals for Temporal Event Understanding

**作者**: Rui Chen, Xingyu Chen, Pengxin Xu, Kai Chen, Junzhi Yu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19526v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19526v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19526v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Temporal action detection in long untrimmed videos suffers from inaccurate localization and low-quality proposals, especially with sparse annotations and ambiguous boundaries. For open-ended video understanding and LLM-driven event analysis, reliable proposals connect temporal localization, event annotation, and semantic understanding. However, conventional temporal action detection (TAD) pipelines lack explicit modeling of proposal localization quality, cross-representation support, and inter-proposal relations. We propose Hybrid Rank-Calibration of Action Proposals (HyrCap), a proposal-centric query-based TAD framework for temporal event understanding. Hybrid combines complementary video features with different localization cues, while Rank-Calibration adjusts proposal ranking confidence to reflect localization reliability and candidate competition. HyrCap treats dense query-based predictions as an over-complete temporal event hypothesis space. Cross-Representation Consensus Calibration, Localization Quality Estimation, and Proposal Redundancy Suppression improve proposal ranking reliability, localization quality, and candidate discrimination. Inspired by LLMs' capabilities in fine-grained semantic recognition, event description, and reasoning, we introduce HyrCap for Semantic Action Understanding, using calibrated proposals for standard TAD and as inputs to LLM-based semantic understanding. We define a staged evaluation protocol to separately measure event localization, segment-level semantic understanding, and combined event understanding performance. Experiments on public benchmarks show that HyrCap achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple metrics and provides a finer-grained paradigm and evaluation protocol for query-based event segmentation and annotation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Temporal action detection in long untrimmed videos suffers from inaccurate localization and low-quality proposals, especially with sparse annotations and ambiguous boundaries. For open-ended video understanding and LLM-driven event analysis, reliable proposals connect temporal localization, event annotation, and semantic understanding. However, conventional temporal action detection (TAD) pipelines lack explicit modeling of proposal localization quality, cross-representation support, and inter-proposal relations. We propose Hybrid Rank-Calibration of Action Proposals (HyrCap), a proposal-centric query-based TAD framework for temporal event understanding. Hybrid combines complementary video features with different localization cues, while Rank-Calibration adjusts proposal ranking confidence to reflect localization reliability and candidate competition. HyrCap treats dense query-based predictions as an over-complete temporal event hypothesis space. Cross-Representation Consensus Calibration, Localization Quality Estimation, and Proposal Redundancy Suppression improve proposal ranking reliability, localization quality, and candidate discrimination. Inspired by LLMs' capabilities in fine-grained semantic recognition, event description, and reasoning, we introduce HyrCap for Semantic Action Understanding, using calibrated proposals for standard TAD and as inputs to LLM-based semantic understanding. We define a staged evaluation protocol to separately measure event localization, segment-level semantic understanding, and combined event understanding performance. Experiments on public benchmarks show that HyrCap achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple metrics and provides a finer-grained paradigm and evaluation protocol for query-based event segmentation and annotation.

</details>

## 77. Robust Real-Time Monocular Human Pose Estimation with Insole-Based Sensor Fusion

**中文标题**: Robust Real-Time Monocular Human Pose Estimation with Insole-Based Sensor Fusion

**作者**: Liang Yu, Tao Zhu, Xiangqing Lu, Wenjian Luo, Liming Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19494v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19494v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19494v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Real-time monocular human pose estimation is an important sensing component for human-centered intelligent systems. While recent camera-based methods have achieved impressive accuracy, fundamental challenges persist, particularly when visual observations are incomplete, such as during lower-body occlusion or short out-of-frame intervals. In these challenging but common scenarios, vision-only systems often struggle to reconstruct physically plausible lower-limb motion and reliable foot-ground contact. Due to the highly unobtrusive and wearable nature of smart insoles, this paper investigates whether simple plantar pressure and acceleration sensors can effectively resolve these visually induced limitations. To systematically investigate this, we first collect a synchronized multimodal dataset and then propose a part-decoupled temporal fusion framework. The proposed model fuses foot-sensing features into the lower-body branch to compensate for incomplete visual evidence, while a lightweight coordinator exchanges information between the upper- and lower-body temporal representations to maintain whole-body consistency. The framework is evaluated with three representative real-time visual backbones based on 2D keypoints, 3D landmarks, and SMPL-based human mesh recovery. We evaluate our framework on three representative real-time visual backbones. Quantitative experiments demonstrate that incorporating simple foot sensors consistently improves pose accuracy, motion smoothness, and gait-related measurements over vision-only baselines, with the most significant gains observed under occlusion and short visual-dropout conditions. Ultimately, our results demonstrate that unobtrusive foot sensing provides vital and complementary physical observability when camera observations are temporarily unavailable.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Real-time monocular human pose estimation is an important sensing component for human-centered intelligent systems. While recent camera-based methods have achieved impressive accuracy, fundamental challenges persist, particularly when visual observations are incomplete, such as during lower-body occlusion or short out-of-frame intervals. In these challenging but common scenarios, vision-only systems often struggle to reconstruct physically plausible lower-limb motion and reliable foot-ground contact. Due to the highly unobtrusive and wearable nature of smart insoles, this paper investigates whether simple plantar pressure and acceleration sensors can effectively resolve these visually induced limitations. To systematically investigate this, we first collect a synchronized multimodal dataset and then propose a part-decoupled temporal fusion framework. The proposed model fuses foot-sensing features into the lower-body branch to compensate for incomplete visual evidence, while a lightweight coordinator exchanges information between the upper- and lower-body temporal representations to maintain whole-body consistency. The framework is evaluated with three representative real-time visual backbones based on 2D keypoints, 3D landmarks, and SMPL-based human mesh recovery. We evaluate our framework on three representative real-time visual backbones. Quantitative experiments demonstrate that incorporating simple foot sensors consistently improves pose accuracy, motion smoothness, and gait-related measurements over vision-only baselines, with the most significant gains observed under occlusion and short visual-dropout conditions. Ultimately, our results demonstrate that unobtrusive foot sensing provides vital and complementary physical observability when camera observations are temporarily unavailable.

</details>

## 78. VideoHarness-SRSI: Structured Recursive Self-Improvement for Video Understanding Harness

**中文标题**: VideoHarness-SRSI: Structured Recursive Self-Improvement for Video Understanding Harness

**作者**: Zihao Lin, Haoyu Dong, Haobo Xu, Chi-Chih Chang, Xuying Ning et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19424v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19424v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19424v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

LLM system capabilities depend not only on model parameters but also on the executable harnesses that organize information acquisition, tool use, reasoning, and memory. Recursive harness evolution can automate their design, but how it can reliably discover generalizable systems from limited, noisy, and repeatedly reused feedback remains unclear. We study this question in agentic video understanding, where rich multimodal evidence and coupled mechanisms make harness optimization challenging. We first introduce VideoHarness-BRSI, a recursive self-improvement framework that evolves video harnesses around a frozen vision-language model. Analyzing over 4,000 evolved candidates, we find it competitive with hand-engineered systems but identify three interconnected failure modes: validation overfitting, search-budget dilution, and the early-experience trap. We trace all three to a common root: the same imperfect validation evidence drives both harness revisions and the proposer's search strategy. We therefore propose VideoHarness-SRSI, an evidence-governed framework in which every search decision rests on evidence that is transferable, sufficient, and gathered before the search commits. It restricts the proposer to content-free feedback, informs exploration with instrumented warm-up experiments, and develops mechanisms in isolated probe-race branches before factorial composition and joint refinement. Across four video understanding benchmarks, VideoHarness-SRSI improves the equally weighted mean held-out score by 6.2% relative to VideoHarness-BRSI and reduces the mean validation-test gap by 37.9%. Reliable harness evolution thus depends on how search is organized and informed, not only on how well it optimizes validation performance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

LLM system capabilities depend not only on model parameters but also on the executable harnesses that organize information acquisition, tool use, reasoning, and memory. Recursive harness evolution can automate their design, but how it can reliably discover generalizable systems from limited, noisy, and repeatedly reused feedback remains unclear. We study this question in agentic video understanding, where rich multimodal evidence and coupled mechanisms make harness optimization challenging. It restricts the proposer to content-free feedback, informs exploration with instrumented warm-up experiments, and develops mechanisms in isolated probe-race branches before factorial composition and joint refinement. Reliable harness evolution thus depends on how search is organized and informed, not only on how well it optimizes validation performance.

</details>

## 79. VLX-Go: Robust Embodied Tracking through Multi-Stage Learning and On-Policy Refinement

**中文标题**: VLX-Go: Robust Embodied Tracking through Multi-Stage Learning and On-Policy Refinement

**作者**: Kyusong Lee, Heting Ying, Tiancheng Zhao

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19361v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19361v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19361v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Embodied visual tracking requires a robot to identify a designated target from visual observations and control its own motion to follow that target through an environment. Following an isolated person is a comparatively simple demonstration of embodied visual tracking, but following the intended person among distractors remains challenging. Existing methods exhibit a substantial drop in success from single-target to distractor tracking, motivating a focus on the shared spaces that person-following robots must navigate. We present \method{}, a vision--language waypoint policy built around the hypothesis that visual and language representations should be aligned before tracking-specific motion is learned. The policy combines complementary frozen visual encoders, multiscale temporal context, a transformer backbone, and a lightweight head for robot-centric waypoint prediction. Its training progresses through vision--language alignment, multimodal reasoning mid-training, and tracking specialization on 19,532 oracle episodes. Compared with 41.54\% distractor-tracking success for our earlier OmTrackVLA model, the complete multi-stage recipe achieves 55.80\%. We then explore on-policy refinement using filtered oracle labels for states visited by the policy. This preliminary study adds 2.42 percentage points of distractor-tracking success, reaching 58.22\%, while reducing collision rate by 1.99 pts. The final policy records the highest distractor- and ambiguous-tracking success rates among the compared methods. These results support staged multimodal learning as a promising direction for tracking among distractors and motivate further study of on-policy refinement for person following in shared spaces.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Embodied visual tracking requires a robot to identify a designated target from visual observations and control its own motion to follow that target through an environment. Following an isolated person is a comparatively simple demonstration of embodied visual tracking, but following the intended person among distractors remains challenging. Existing methods exhibit a substantial drop in success from single-target to distractor tracking, motivating a focus on the shared spaces that person-following robots must navigate. We present \method{}, a vision--language waypoint policy built around the hypothesis that visual and language representations should be aligned before tracking-specific motion is learned. The policy combines complementary frozen visual encoders, multiscale temporal context, a transformer backbone, and a lightweight head for robot-centric waypoint prediction. Its training progresses through vision--language alignment, multimodal reasoning mid-training, and tracking specialization on 19,532 oracle episodes. Compared with 41.54\% distractor-tracking success for our earlier OmTrackVLA model, the complete multi-stage recipe achieves 55.80\%. We then explore on-policy refinement using filtered oracle labels for states visited by the policy. This preliminary study adds 2.42 percentage points of distractor-tracking success, reaching 58.22\%, while reducing collision rate by 1.99 pts. The final policy records the highest distractor- and ambiguous-tracking success rates among the compared methods. These results support staged multimodal learning as a promising direction for tracking among distractors and motivate further study of on-policy refinement for person following in shared spaces.

</details>

## 80. SonoWorld: Towards Interactive Pose-Controllable Ultrasound World Models

**中文标题**: SonoWorld: Towards Interactive Pose-Controllable Ultrasound World Models

**作者**: Zefan Yang, Zheyuan Zhang, Boris Maihe, Yikang Liu, Hang Yu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19327v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19327v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19327v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robotic ultrasound can standardize image acquisition and reduce operator workload and variability, but policy learning remains limited by the lack of realistic, controllable simulation environments. While controllable world models have advanced rapidly in natural-image domains, ultrasound world modeling remains challenging because each frame captures only a cross-section of unobserved 3D anatomy, causing small probe motions to induce large appearance changes. Existing approaches rely on robot-tracked poses and latent-space prediction, but do not directly generate the observations required for interactive control. We propose SonoWorld, a causal ultrasound world model that learns video dynamics from unlabeled clinical videos and grounds them to 6-DoF control using a small set of freehand clinical scans tracked with an EM sensor. SonoWorld is trained in three stages: pretraining a latent-action-conditioned video diffusion model on around 6.9 million unlabeled multi-organ ultrasound frames; grounding the learned video dynamics to metric probe poses with a small EM-tracked thyroid dataset; and applying distribution matching distillation to enable causal, long-horizon autoregressive generation with selectively caching eviction tokens at inference time. We evaluate controllability through pose-following fidelity, round-trip consistency, and axis-specific control under synthetic actions on held-out tracked scans, and further validate it with a user study. In model-based action discrimination, SonoWorld correctly ranks the target-directed probe motion over an equal-magnitude opposite motion in up to 74.8% of trials. To our knowledge, SonoWorld is the first causal, pose-controllable ultrasound world model learned primarily from freehand clinical videos, providing a data-driven interactive environment for robotic ultrasound data acquisition and policy evaluation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Robotic ultrasound can standardize image acquisition and reduce operator workload and variability, but policy learning remains limited by the lack of realistic, controllable simulation environments. While controllable world models have advanced rapidly in natural-image domains, ultrasound world modeling remains challenging because each frame captures only a cross-section of unobserved 3D anatomy, causing small probe motions to induce large appearance changes. Existing approaches rely on robot-tracked poses and latent-space prediction, but do not directly generate the observations required for interactive control. We propose SonoWorld, a causal ultrasound world model that learns video dynamics from unlabeled clinical videos and grounds them to 6-DoF control using a small set of freehand clinical scans tracked with an EM sensor. SonoWorld is trained in three stages: pretraining a latent-action-conditioned video diffusion model on around 6.9 million unlabeled multi-organ ultrasound frames; grounding the learned video dynamics to metric probe poses with a small EM-tracked thyroid dataset; and applying distribution matching distillation to enable causal, long-horizon autoregressive generation with selectively caching eviction tokens at inference time. We evaluate controllability through pose-following fidelity, round-trip consistency, and axis-specific control under synthetic actions on held-out tracked scans, and further validate it with a user study. In model-based action discrimination, SonoWorld correctly ranks the target-directed probe motion over an equal-magnitude opposite motion in up to 74.8% of trials. To our knowledge, SonoWorld is the first causal, pose-controllable ultrasound world model learned primarily from freehand clinical videos, providing a data-driven interactive environment for robotic ultrasound data acquisition and policy evaluation.

</details>

## 81. SOMA: Stack-of-Objects Manipulation using a Quadruped Robot

**中文标题**: SOMA: Stack-of-Objects Manipulation using a Quadruped Robot

**作者**: Stergios E. Bachoumas, Panagiotis Artemiadis

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20968v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20968v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20968v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Executing heavy-duty physical manipulation in unpredictable environments remains a major challenge for quadrupedal robots lacking dedicated manipulators. This paper presents SOMA, a hierarchical reinforcement learning framework for whole-body non-prehensile manipulation of multi-object stacks using a quadruped robot. Unlike traditional methods restricted to two-dimensional planar pushing, SOMA integrates explicit robot base-height control, unlocking full three-dimensional pushing strategies. This capability enables the quadruped to manipulate complex payload stacks as a unified system while preventing relative sliding or tipping of upper layers. The architecture factorizes control into a high-level manipulation policy using a Simplified Structured State Space Sequence (S5) encoder to reason over long-horizon temporal history and predict bounded target tracking commands via Beta distributions, and a low-level locomotion policy that dynamically adapts leg stance configurations. In extensive simulation benchmarks, SOMA achieves a $90.3\%$ success rate for single-object reposing and up to $90.1\%$ for dynamic two-box stacks under stringent error margins ($0.05\text{ m}$ position error, $1^\circ$ orientation error). Finally, we validate zero-shot sim-to-real transfer on a Unitree Go1 quadruped robot across diverse payload masses and geometries, demonstrating active base-height adjustments to maintain traction and balance during heavy pushing tasks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Executing heavy-duty physical manipulation in unpredictable environments remains a major challenge for quadrupedal robots lacking dedicated manipulators. This paper presents SOMA, a hierarchical reinforcement learning framework for whole-body non-prehensile manipulation of multi-object stacks using a quadruped robot. Unlike traditional methods restricted to two-dimensional planar pushing, SOMA integrates explicit robot base-height control, unlocking full three-dimensional pushing strategies. This capability enables the quadruped to manipulate complex payload stacks as a unified system while preventing relative sliding or tipping of upper layers. The architecture factorizes control into a high-level manipulation policy using a Simplified Structured State Space Sequence (S5) encoder to reason over long-horizon temporal history and predict bounded target tracking commands via Beta distributions, and a low-level locomotion policy that dynamically adapts leg stance configurations. In extensive simulation benchmarks, SOMA achieves a $90.3\%$ success rate for single-object reposing and up to $90.1\%$ for dynamic two-box stacks under stringent error margins ($0.05\text{ m}$ position error, $1^\circ$ orientation error). Finally, we validate zero-shot sim-to-real transfer on a Unitree Go1 quadruped robot across diverse payload masses and geometries, demonstrating active base-height adjustments to maintain traction and balance during heavy pushing tasks.

</details>

## 82. Co-Evolving Acting and Forecasting for Language Agents

**中文标题**: Co-Evolving Acting and Forecasting for Language Agents

**作者**: Haofei Yu, Yixing Chen, Yining Zhao, Xuyan Ye, Yuxiang Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20597v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20597v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20597v2)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/ulab-uiuc/CoPE.

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Training language agents is bottlenecked by the high cost of long-horizon rollouts, yet standard agentic reinforcement learning (RL) learns only from a terminal scalar reward, leaving the supervision in intermediate steps largely unused. Prior work exploits this supervision through value targets that estimate per-step success probabilities or observation targets that fit environment response. Both are hard to construct well: (1) value targets come from the same single scalar signal for each step in the trajectory, and (2) observation targets, such as web pages and terminal logs, are long and noisy and reach only one step ahead. We propose CoPE, which adds a third kind of multi-step action targets. On successful on-policy trajectories, CoPE trains the policy backbone to generate the action sequences that follows each history. Each action is also supervised when forecast from the preceding histories, forming a temporal ensemble that the shared backbone absorbs during training, and this enables acting and forecasting to co-evolve. The policy still acts one step at a time at inference, with no overhead. Across five benchmarks (ALFWorld, ScienceWorld, WebShop, $τ^2$-bench, and AppWorld), CoPE (1) achieves stronger aggregate performance than GRPO and RL baselines with predictive auxiliary targets across simulated environments, while improving overall performance on both realistic applications; (2) delivers up to $1.9\times$ and $2.0\times$ higher sample efficiency than ECHO and GRPO, respectively, on ScienceWorld; and (3) achieves these gains with a single policy backbone, without additional models, separate pretraining, or inference overhead. The codebase is available at https://github.com/ulab-uiuc/CoPE.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Training language agents is bottlenecked by the high cost of long-horizon rollouts, yet standard agentic reinforcement learning (RL) learns only from a terminal scalar reward, leaving the supervision in intermediate steps largely unused. Prior work exploits this supervision through value targets that estimate per-step success probabilities or observation targets that fit environment response. Both are hard to construct well: (1) value targets come from the same single scalar signal for each step in the trajectory, and (2) observation targets, such as web pages and terminal logs, are long and noisy and reach only one step ahead. We propose CoPE, which adds a third kind of multi-step action targets. On successful on-policy trajectories, CoPE trains the policy backbone to generate the action sequences that follows each history. Each action is also supervised when forecast from the preceding histories, forming a temporal ensemble that the shared backbone absorbs during training, and this enables acting and forecasting to co-evolve. The policy still acts one step at a time at inference, with no overhead. Across five benchmarks (ALFWorld, ScienceWorld, WebShop, $τ^2$-bench, and AppWorld), CoPE (1) achieves stronger aggregate performance than GRPO and RL baselines with predictive auxiliary targets across simulated environments, while improving overall performance on both realistic applications; (2) delivers up to $1.9\times$ and $2.0\times$ higher sample efficiency than ECHO and GRPO, respectively, on ScienceWorld; and (3) achieves these gains with a single policy backbone, without additional models, separate pretraining, or inference overhead. The codebase is available at https://github.com/ulab-uiuc/CoPE.

</details>

## 83. CoWear: Towards Collaborative Wearable Inertial Localization with Multiple Smart Devices

**中文标题**: CoWear: Towards Collaborative Wearable Inertial Localization with Multiple Smart Devices

**作者**: Zhuoyuan She, Zhanpeng Zhang, Yapeng Chen, Jiale Wang, Xin Xie et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20596v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20596v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20596v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Consumer smart devices observe the same human motion from different body locations, yet existing inertial-localization benchmarks rarely provide synchronized heterogeneous wearables with device-specific motion ground truth. We present CoWear, a dataset and reference framework for collaborative inertial localization using a smartphone, smartwatch, and smart glasses. The dataset contains over 30 participants, over 300 walking sessions, and more than 30 hours and 100 km of aggregate device trajectories, with each device individually tracked by motion capture. To enable collaboration across distinct body motions, we train role-specific estimators to predict the displacement of a common reference device over synchronized temporal windows. Their predictions are transformed to the world frame using gyroscope integration and fused according to learned uncertainty. Experiments using phone, glasses, and watch as reference targets show that multi-device fusion consistently improves both displacement and trajectory rollout accuracy over individual-device estimation. CoWear provides a dataset and reproducible baseline for studying collaborative inertial localization across heterogeneous consumer wearables.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Consumer smart devices observe the same human motion from different body locations, yet existing inertial-localization benchmarks rarely provide synchronized heterogeneous wearables with device-specific motion ground truth. We present CoWear, a dataset and reference framework for collaborative inertial localization using a smartphone, smartwatch, and smart glasses. The dataset contains over 30 participants, over 300 walking sessions, and more than 30 hours and 100 km of aggregate device trajectories, with each device individually tracked by motion capture. To enable collaboration across distinct body motions, we train role-specific estimators to predict the displacement of a common reference device over synchronized temporal windows. Their predictions are transformed to the world frame using gyroscope integration and fused according to learned uncertainty. Experiments using phone, glasses, and watch as reference targets show that multi-device fusion consistently improves both displacement and trajectory rollout accuracy over individual-device estimation. CoWear provides a dataset and reproducible baseline for studying collaborative inertial localization across heterogeneous consumer wearables.

</details>

## 84. KinoGen: Customizable Humanoid Loco-Manipulation References via Kinematically Grounded Video Generation

**中文标题**: KinoGen: Customizable Humanoid Loco-Manipulation References via Kinematically Grounded Video Generation

**作者**: Qi Wu, Lulu Zhao, Sanghyun Hahn, Rishi Yennu, Emmy Yang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20552v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20552v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20552v1)

**项目主页**: https://kinogen-humanoid.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We present KinoGen, a framework that synthesizes customizable humanoid loco-manipulation references through kinematically grounded video generation. KinoGen conditions a pretrained video generator on calibrated renders of the target robot and object in a simulated task instance and recovers the reference by fitting the same models to the generated two-view video, so that the reference lies in the robot configuration space by construction without a human body proxy or retargeting. Body, hand, and object priors initialize the reconstruction, and direct optimization against cross-view evidence refines it. Since an edited keyframe is simply another rendered state, a user can prescribe a goal object pose, regenerate the video around the edited keyframe, and obtain a reference that satisfies the pose exactly. Across eight loco-manipulation tasks, KinoGen yields lower penetration and foot skating than video-based baselines and preserves over 90% of annotated contacts, and its customized references satisfy every prescribed target with substantially less penetration than direct interpolation. Tracking policies trained on the synthesized references are deployed on a Unitree G1 humanoid with dexterous hands. Project webpage: https://kinogen-humanoid.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

KinoGen conditions a pretrained video generator on calibrated renders of the target robot and object in a simulated task instance and recovers the reference by fitting the same models to the generated two-view video, so that the reference lies in the robot configuration space by construction without a human body proxy or retargeting. Body, hand, and object priors initialize the reconstruction, and direct optimization against cross-view evidence refines it. Since an edited keyframe is simply another rendered state, a user can prescribe a goal object pose, regenerate the video around the edited keyframe, and obtain a reference that satisfies the pose exactly. Across eight loco-manipulation tasks, KinoGen yields lower penetration and foot skating than video-based baselines and preserves over 90% of annotated contacts, and its customized references satisfy every prescribed target with substantially less penetration than direct interpolation. Tracking policies trained on the synthesized references are deployed on a Unitree G1 humanoid with dexterous hands. Project webpage: https://kinogen-humanoid.github.io/.

## 85. Physical Time as a Missing Dimension of Robot Policy Memory Dynamics

**中文标题**: Physical Time as a Missing Dimension of Robot Policy Memory Dynamics

**作者**: Yushen Liang, Yue Peng, Baosheng Jin, Tianluo Zhang, Xinyu Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20524v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20524v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20524v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Existing robot policy memories typically evolve with observation sequences, implicitly treating each update as the same amount of physical time. Yet identical observation histories can span different durations and require different actions, exposing physical time as a missing dimension of policy memory. We introduce Temporal Flow Policy (TFP), which learns memory dynamics to retain past events and evolve the memory state over measured time intervals. Observations and prior memory determine the content and time scales of each transition; elapsed time determines how far the state advances. TFP conditions diffusion and flow-matching policies through this memory state and is trained with their native imitation objectives. We evaluate TFP with Diffusion Policy and $π_{0.5}$ through controlled comparisons of memory designs. These experiments show that successful timing depends on both measured intervals and retained history. TFP achieves higher average success than the recurrent and state-space memories we equip with the same intervals and than a variant that feeds the interval to a learned gate. Memory readouts recover the time since an event and motion information after occlusion. Changing the supplied intervals systematically delays or advances actions, linking time inputs to behavior. Real-robot tasks requiring timing or memory, together with LIBERO evaluations, demonstrate benefits in physical and general manipulation. These results suggest viewing robot memory not as stored observation history, but as a state that retains past events and evolves with physical time.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing robot policy memories typically evolve with observation sequences, implicitly treating each update as the same amount of physical time. Yet identical observation histories can span different durations and require different actions, exposing physical time as a missing dimension of policy memory. We introduce Temporal Flow Policy (TFP), which learns memory dynamics to retain past events and evolve the memory state over measured time intervals. Observations and prior memory determine the content and time scales of each transition; elapsed time determines how far the state advances. TFP conditions diffusion and flow-matching policies through this memory state and is trained with their native imitation objectives. We evaluate TFP with Diffusion Policy and $π_{0.5}$ through controlled comparisons of memory designs. These experiments show that successful timing depends on both measured intervals and retained history. TFP achieves higher average success than the recurrent and state-space memories we equip with the same intervals and than a variant that feeds the interval to a learned gate. Memory readouts recover the time since an event and motion information after occlusion. Changing the supplied intervals systematically delays or advances actions, linking time inputs to behavior. Real-robot tasks requiring timing or memory, together with LIBERO evaluations, demonstrate benefits in physical and general manipulation. These results suggest viewing robot memory not as stored observation history, but as a state that retains past events and evolves with physical time.

</details>

## 86. Safe Reinforcement Learning with Receding-Horizon Safety Critic and Differentiable Action Shields

**中文标题**: Safe Reinforcement Learning with Receding-Horizon Safety Critic and Differentiable Action Shields

**作者**: Siwei Ju, Jan Peters, Oleg Arenz

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20255v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20255v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20255v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Despite the Markov assumption, safety failures in robot reinforcement learning rarely occur instantaneously; they typically emerge over several steps as the policy enters unfamiliar states under disturbances and domain shift. We address this limitation by learning a receding-horizon safety critic that predicts whether a state will remain non-terminating over the next receding horizon, enabling anticipatory detection of impending locomotion failures. The critic is trained from rollout-derived horizon labels with a composite objective that combines horizon classification, control-barrier-function-inspired temporal consistency, and smoothness regularization, and we aggregate an ensemble of critics with a conservative Conditional Value at Risk estimate to account for uncertainty. Building on the learned critic, we derive a closed-form, differentiable CBF-style safety filter that minimally modifies the policy action to improve predicted safety, and integrate it into PPO with an anticipation loss to reduce policy-filter mismatch during training. In simulated quadruped locomotion, our method improves robustness under action noise, external perturbations, and terrain shifts, reducing catastrophic failures while maintaining competitive returns.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Despite the Markov assumption, safety failures in robot reinforcement learning rarely occur instantaneously; they typically emerge over several steps as the policy enters unfamiliar states under disturbances and domain shift. We address this limitation by learning a receding-horizon safety critic that predicts whether a state will remain non-terminating over the next receding horizon, enabling anticipatory detection of impending locomotion failures. The critic is trained from rollout-derived horizon labels with a composite objective that combines horizon classification, control-barrier-function-inspired temporal consistency, and smoothness regularization, and we aggregate an ensemble of critics with a conservative Conditional Value at Risk estimate to account for uncertainty. Building on the learned critic, we derive a closed-form, differentiable CBF-style safety filter that minimally modifies the policy action to improve predicted safety, and integrate it into PPO with an anticipation loss to reduce policy-filter mismatch during training. In simulated quadruped locomotion, our method improves robustness under action noise, external perturbations, and terrain shifts, reducing catastrophic failures while maintaining competitive returns.

</details>

---

## 87. ObjDream: Guiding Vision-Language-Action Models with Object-Level Understanding and Future State Prediction

**中文标题**: ObjDream: Guiding Vision-Language-Action Models with Object-Level Understanding and Future State Prediction

**作者**: Huailiang Ma, Yuxuan Li, Ziyi Zhao, Xinkai Wang, Mingyu Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20223v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20223v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20223v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for robotic manipulation, enabling robots to generate actions from visual observations and language instructions. However, most existing VLA models still operate on image-level visual representations inherited from pretrained vision-language backbones, while lacking fine-grained object understanding for manipulation. As a result, their robustness can degrade under out-of-distribution object layouts, clutter, and background variations. To address these limitations, we introduce ObjDream, an object-centric VLA framework centered on object-level understanding and future object state prediction. ObjDream uses a spatio-temporal object encoder to organize visual observations into temporal object slots, where persistent object identity and dynamic object state are represented through separate channels. These structured object representations are inserted into the token sequence of the vision-language model (VLM), where identity-guided routing modulates VLM attention to the corresponding object tokens. In addition, an action-conditioned object dynamics module predicts compact future object states relevant to manipulation during training, explicitly supervising object state evolution under robot actions without requiring future prediction at inference. Experiments on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and RoboTwin 2.0 show that ObjDream improves generalization under diverse distribution shifts and scene perturbations. Real-world robot experiments further demonstrate that ObjDream transfers to physical manipulation settings with novel object arrangements and background changes.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for robotic manipulation, enabling robots to generate actions from visual observations and language instructions. However, most existing VLA models still operate on image-level visual representations inherited from pretrained vision-language backbones, while lacking fine-grained object understanding for manipulation. As a result, their robustness can degrade under out-of-distribution object layouts, clutter, and background variations. To address these limitations, we introduce ObjDream, an object-centric VLA framework centered on object-level understanding and future object state prediction. ObjDream uses a spatio-temporal object encoder to organize visual observations into temporal object slots, where persistent object identity and dynamic object state are represented through separate channels. These structured object representations are inserted into the token sequence of the vision-language model (VLM), where identity-guided routing modulates VLM attention to the corresponding object tokens. In addition, an action-conditioned object dynamics module predicts compact future object states relevant to manipulation during training, explicitly supervising object state evolution under robot actions without requiring future prediction at inference. Experiments on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and RoboTwin 2.0 show that ObjDream improves generalization under diverse distribution shifts and scene perturbations. Real-world robot experiments further demonstrate that ObjDream transfers to physical manipulation settings with novel object arrangements and background changes.

</details>

## 88. Atomic-WAM: Execution-Grounded Atomic-Motion Reasoning for World Action Models

**中文标题**: Atomic-WAM: Execution-Grounded Atomic-Motion Reasoning for World Action Models

**作者**: Shijun Shi, Zhe Liu, Zhi Hou, Yinan Chen, Tao Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20157v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20157v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20157v1)

**项目主页**: https://ssj9596.github.io/Atomic-WAM-project

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World Action Models (WAMs) typically adapt video foundation models to robotic control through joint video and action prediction. However, task-level instructions may leave the next physical interaction underspecified, requiring semantic grounding and state-dependent motion selection. To this end, we introduce Atomic-WAM, an understand-then-execute framework that connects task reasoning to action generation through atomic-motion descriptions. Specifically, a vision-language reasoner describes the next local motion from the task instruction and visual context, guiding a WAM to predict future video and an action chunk. Furthermore, an automatic annotation pipeline curates temporally aligned, contact-enriched supervision from multi-view DROID demonstrations. To make atomic-motion predictions executable by the WAM, we post-train the reasoner with Execution-Grounded GRPO (EG-GRPO). With the WAM fixed, EG-GRPO adapts the reasoner through rewards for motion appropriateness and execution success. Finally, Atomic-WAM achieves 39.6% task success under default instructions and 31.6% under vague instructions in a real-to-sim evaluation on RoboLab. Project page: https://ssj9596.github.io/Atomic-WAM-project

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World Action Models (WAMs) typically adapt video foundation models to robotic control through joint video and action prediction. However, task-level instructions may leave the next physical interaction underspecified, requiring semantic grounding and state-dependent motion selection. To this end, we introduce Atomic-WAM, an understand-then-execute framework that connects task reasoning to action generation through atomic-motion descriptions. Specifically, a vision-language reasoner describes the next local motion from the task instruction and visual context, guiding a WAM to predict future video and an action chunk. Furthermore, an automatic annotation pipeline curates temporally aligned, contact-enriched supervision from multi-view DROID demonstrations. To make atomic-motion predictions executable by the WAM, we post-train the reasoner with Execution-Grounded GRPO (EG-GRPO). Finally, Atomic-WAM achieves 39.6% task success under default instructions and 31.6% under vague instructions in a real-to-sim evaluation on RoboLab. Project page: https://ssj9596.github.io/Atomic-WAM-project

</details>

## 89. Refine Actions If Needed: Adaptive Action Chunk Refinement for Robot Manipulation

**中文标题**: Refine Actions If Needed: Adaptive Action Chunk Refinement for Robot Manipulation

**作者**: Hanlin Liu, Zichen Zhou, Jiacheng Zhang, Liuyuan Na, Jin Jin et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19765v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19765v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19765v1)

**项目主页**: https://rain-policy.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Computationally lightweight manipulation policies predict executable actions with low inference latency, but prediction errors can limit task success. Iterative generative policies can achieve higher task success rates, but repeated network evaluations increase inference latency. Refining action proposals from computationally lightweight policies can combine these strengths, but the amount of refinement needed varies across states. We introduce RAIN (Refine Actions If Needed), a framework that predicts action errors at different refinement levels to decide whether and how much to refine each executable proposal, before performing any refinement. We train an ensemble to predict these errors by comparing action proposals and their refined versions with expert actions from offline demonstrations, without requiring explicit labels of refinement needs. Calibration balances uncertainty, computation, and temporal consistency. We evaluate RAIN on eight ManiSkill tasks and four real-robot tasks. In simulation, our policy achieves 66.6% success, approaching the best evaluated diffusion-policy success rate with 5.71 times lower policy latency. On the real robot, success increases from 55.0% with Action Chunking with Transformers (ACT) to 72.5%, with comparable average successful completion time. Matched controls show that dynamic routing maintains the strongest observed fixed-refinement success level with 26.6% fewer refiner evaluations. Project page: https://rain-policy.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 90. Coordi-pi: Coordination-Aware Dual-Hand Action Representation for Bimanual Dexterous Manipulation

**中文标题**: Coordi-pi: Coordination-Aware Dual-Hand Action Representation for Bimanual Dexterous Manipulation

**作者**: Yiming Meng, Yanming Shao, Weijie Shen, Kunyang Lin, Jiayu Sun et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19753v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19753v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19753v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) models have shown strong potential for general-purpose robotic manipulation, but extending them to bimanual dexterous manipulation introduces a high-dimensional and structured action space. Existing bimanual VLA policies commonly concatenate right- and left-side arm--hand actions at each control timestep, leaving cross-hand interactions implicit within a single action vector. We introduce Coordi-$π$, a structured action representation for bimanual dexterous manipulation. Coordi-$π$ represents the right- and left-side arm--hand actions as individual action tokens and organizes them into an interleaved sequence, explicitly exposing the two sides within the action sequence. To explicitly encode hand identity and temporal synchronization in this interleaved representation, a learnable hand-identity embedding distinguishes the two sides, while sequence--control-timestep factorized rotary positional encoding (RoPE) separates sequence position from control timestep, allowing actions at the same control timestep to share a temporal coordinate while retaining distinct sequence positions. Across all five bimanual dexterous manipulation tasks in DexJoCo, Coordi-$π$ achieves state-of-the-art performance under both \textit{rand-obj} and \textit{rand-full}, improving the average success rate over $π_{0.5}$ from 31.9\% to 51.3\% and from 18.3\% to 32.3\%, respectively. Coordi-$π$ also consistently outperforms $π_{0.5}$ across four real-world bimanual dexterous manipulation tasks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Across all five bimanual dexterous manipulation tasks in DexJoCo, Coordi-$π$ achieves state-of-the-art performance under both \textit{rand-obj} and \textit{rand-full}, improving the average success rate over $π_{0.5}$ from 31.9\% to 51.3\% and from 18.3\% to 32.3\%, respectively. Coordi-$π$ also consistently outperforms $π_{0.5}$ across four real-world bimanual dexterous manipulation tasks.

</details>

## 91. Eye, Robot: Learning Expressive Gaze Dynamics via a VLM-Guided Transformer-VAE

**中文标题**: Eye, Robot: Learning Expressive Gaze Dynamics via a VLM-Guided Transformer-VAE

**作者**: Yuhang Hu, Junlin Lyu, Yuan Fang, Xiaoying Shen, Lechen Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19635v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19635v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19635v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Gaze coordinates attention in human interaction through coupled eye, head, and eyelid motion. A humanoid robot must choose where to look while generating a suitable movement toward that target; fixed tracking rules alone do not model the temporal structure of human gaze. We separate these tasks with an asynchronous vision-language model that selects a scene referent, a reactive perception module that maintains fixation, and a Realistic Gaze Transformer (RGT) that generates target-conditioned motion. RGT is a Transformer-VAE trained on markerless human recordings retargeted to the robot's 11-DoF joint space: self-supervised pretraining encodes recent full-head history, and autoregressive fine-tuning exposes the model to its own predictions during rollout. At inference, a four-dimensional binocular-eye trajectory conditions coordinated neck and eye motion; an auxiliary blink predictor triggers eyelid templates during playback. On a two-second benchmark in which all controllers receive the same ground-truth target, RGT-Base obtains the lowest reported overall seven-joint MSE, and physical demonstrations show the integrated targeting and control pipeline. The evidence establishes robot-space kinematic fidelity and real-time RGT inference; perceived naturalness and open-vocabulary target-selection accuracy remain questions for controlled evaluation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 92. ForceBench: Benchmarking Force Integration for Robotic Manipulation

**中文标题**: ForceBench: Benchmarking Force Integration for Robotic Manipulation

**作者**: Xuan Han, Zinuo Jin, Tianlin Li, Yunfan Lou, Xin Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19604v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19604v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19604v1)

**项目主页**: https://forcebench-platform.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Force feedback provides vision-language-action (VLA) policies with physical observations that can guide action selection and execution. However, differences in tasks, demonstrations, and control settings across existing systems hinder reliable comparisons of force integration designs. We introduce ForceBench, a simulation benchmark for evaluating six-axis wrist-force integration under a unified protocol. ForceBench combines eight tasks spanning physical-property inference, resistance-dependent interaction, and contact-rich assembly with 1,600 synchronized demonstrations and task-specific success criteria. We compare one no-force baseline and six force integration designs on a shared $π_{0.5}$ backbone. Prompt Fusion achieves the highest observed mean simulation success of 35.31%, compared with 23.25% for the baseline, and improves success rates across all three task categories. Predictive and temporal gains in simulation concentrate on physical-property inference, while both temporal designs underperform the baseline on assembly. Real-world evaluation of eight systems across five tasks supports the main qualitative trends: Prompt Fusion and Dual-Path Fusion achieve 63.00% mean success, compared with 40.00% for the baseline, but only Prompt Fusion improves every task. These findings support ForceBench's practical value for identifying the benefits and limitations of force integration across manipulation demands. The project page is available at https://forcebench-platform.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Force feedback provides vision-language-action (VLA) policies with physical observations that can guide action selection and execution. Predictive and temporal gains in simulation concentrate on physical-property inference, while both temporal designs underperform the baseline on assembly. Real-world evaluation of eight systems across five tasks supports the main qualitative trends: Prompt Fusion and Dual-Path Fusion achieve 63.00% mean success, compared with 40.00% for the baseline, but only Prompt Fusion improves every task. These findings support ForceBench's practical value for identifying the benefits and limitations of force integration across manipulation demands. The project page is available at https://forcebench-platform.github.io/.

</details>

## 93. RoboSpan: Learning Modular Coordination Policies for High-DoF Whole-Body Mobile Manipulation with Teleoperation

**中文标题**: RoboSpan: Learning Modular Coordination Policies for High-DoF Whole-Body Mobile Manipulation with Teleoperation

**作者**: Keqiang Ren, Xuecheng Ye, Hanyu Liu, Zhihua Wang, Tao Jian et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19594v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19594v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19594v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Whole-body mobile manipulation suffers from two fundamental challenges: inherent motion heterogeneity across locomotion, arm, and hand modules, and progressive error accumulation over long-horizon multi-stage tasks. Existing teleoperation platforms also lack integrated dexterous tactile sensing to support coordinated whole-body decision-making. This paper presents RoboSpan, a high-DoF whole-body teleoperation and demonstration platform, and the Hierarchical Modular Framework (HMF) for learning modular coordination policies. HMF functionally decouples the 29-DoF action space into fine hand manipulation, arm pose adjustment, and mobile-base spatial configuration branches, and introduces the Coordinated Modular Embodied Transformer (COMET) with inter-modular cross-attention to enable dynamic information exchange and action coordination. Vision-language model driven hierarchical task decomposition structurally suppresses temporal error propagation, while a full-palm 2D tactile representation encodes distributed contact signals for cross-module tactile empowerment. Experiments on a physical 29-DoF robot demonstrate that the proposed method achieves over 20\% higher task success rate than mainstream baselines across a range of long-horizon whole-body tasks, markedly improving stability and robustness in contact-rich manipulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Whole-body mobile manipulation suffers from two fundamental challenges: inherent motion heterogeneity across locomotion, arm, and hand modules, and progressive error accumulation over long-horizon multi-stage tasks. Vision-language model driven hierarchical task decomposition structurally suppresses temporal error propagation, while a full-palm 2D tactile representation encodes distributed contact signals for cross-module tactile empowerment. Experiments on a physical 29-DoF robot demonstrate that the proposed method achieves over 20\% higher task success rate than mainstream baselines across a range of long-horizon whole-body tasks, markedly improving stability and robustness in contact-rich manipulation.

## 94. Dual-arm Cooperation for Tabletop Wiping with Temporal Object Removal

**中文标题**: Dual-arm Cooperation for Tabletop Wiping with Temporal Object Removal

**作者**: Pengqi Zhang, Longchang Yu, Daohan Li, Liang Yan, Hangxin Liu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19527v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19527v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19527v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We devise a dual-arm cooperative planning framework for wiping table surface in the presence of scattered objects. Unlike conventional surface-cleaning strategies that only cover free areas and detour around obstacles, our framework explicitly assigns one arm to rearrange objects while the other performs coverage wiping, thereby enabling access to previously obstructed regions. Specifically, we first construct a set of wiping and object-rearrangement action primitives. Based on these primitives, we introduce Geo-MILP, a geometry-aware task planner formulated as Mixed-Integer Linear Programming (MILP). By integrating Logic-Based Benders Decomposition (LBBD), Geo-MILP iteratively optimizes action sequencingand timing to resolve geometric conflicts, promoting efficient cooperation between wiping and object rearrangement. We then employ K-Order Markov Optimization (KOMO) to generate smooth, collision-free motions to ensure feasible execution. Simulation and physical experiments demonstrate that the proposed framework not only increases reachable coverage by enabling access to obstructed areas, but also significantly improves time efficiency compared to Task and Motion Planning (TAMP) baselines, reducing planning time by up to 9 times and execution makespan by approximately 40%.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We devise a dual-arm cooperative planning framework for wiping table surface in the presence of scattered objects. Unlike conventional surface-cleaning strategies that only cover free areas and detour around obstacles, our framework explicitly assigns one arm to rearrange objects while the other performs coverage wiping, thereby enabling access to previously obstructed regions. Specifically, we first construct a set of wiping and object-rearrangement action primitives. Based on these primitives, we introduce Geo-MILP, a geometry-aware task planner formulated as Mixed-Integer Linear Programming (MILP). By integrating Logic-Based Benders Decomposition (LBBD), Geo-MILP iteratively optimizes action sequencingand timing to resolve geometric conflicts, promoting efficient cooperation between wiping and object rearrangement. We then employ K-Order Markov Optimization (KOMO) to generate smooth, collision-free motions to ensure feasible execution. Simulation and physical experiments demonstrate that the proposed framework not only increases reachable coverage by enabling access to obstructed areas, but also significantly improves time efficiency compared to Task and Motion Planning (TAMP) baselines, reducing planning time by up to 9 times and execution makespan by approximately 40%.

</details>

## 95. A VR Training System for Robot-Assisted Vascular Intervention with Dual-Mode Control and Model-Based Interaction Response

**中文标题**: A VR Training System for Robot-Assisted Vascular Intervention with Dual-Mode Control and Model-Based Interaction Response

**作者**: Yining Shang, Shuxiang Guo, Sheng Cao, Pengcheng Li, Bin Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19413v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19413v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19413v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Virtual reality training for robot-assisted vascular intervention requires accurate robotic control and effective feedback on guidewire--vessel interactions. This paper presents a VR training system combining asynchronous dual-mode command mapping with sensorless interaction estimation. The system supports velocity-controlled navigation and position-driven incremental manipulation. Guidewire deformation and a Hunt--Crossley-type contact response are combined and temporally filtered to estimate interaction intensity, while trajectory-based visualization supports post-procedure review. Tests confirmed distinct command-dispatch patterns across three velocity gears and the position mode. During hybrid-scene navigation, the estimated response remained low during straight insertion and increased as the guidewire bent within the vascular model. Trajectory analysis yielded a model-derived cumulative trauma work (CTW) value of 15.166 in normalized units and localized regions of elevated interaction. In a preliminary study with 15 novices, physical-phantom task success increased from 53.3% to 93.3% after VR training, while mean completion time decreased from 132.4 s to 96.7 s. These results demonstrate the system's dual-mode operation, deformation-sensitive feedback, and potential to improve subsequent physical-phantom task performance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 96. Seeing, Maintaining, and Learning: The Evolution of Multimodal Agent Memory

**中文标题**: Seeing, Maintaining, and Learning: The Evolution of Multimodal Agent Memory

**作者**: Minghao Guo, Qingyue Jiao, Xiujin Liu, Jianhong Pang, Ruimeng Yang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19366v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19366v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19366v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multimodal models and agents have emerged to enable AI systems to learn from and interact with the real world more like humans by integrating information across multiple modalities. However, enabling multimodal agents to continually acquire useful information from complex, heterogeneous multimodal data remains a significant challenge as they move toward long-horizon tasks and lifelong settings. Addressing this challenge requires effective memory mechanisms that can maintain continuity across sessions, adapt to changing users and environments, and refine memory-management policies through accumulated experience. We introduce a two-axis analytical framework for multimodal agent memory structured around two key questions: how multimodal information is formed and represented (Axis I, Formation and Representation), and how its management adapts with accumulated experience (Axis II, Memory Management). Axis I, Formation and Representation, characterizes what information is retained and how it is encoded (e.g., modality-specific embeddings, textual and structured records, latent and parametric representations) to support subsequent reasoning and action. Axis II, Memory Management, distinguishes three tiers: T1 Static Memory reuses fixed memory; T2 Dynamic Memory updates memory through a stable policy; and T3 Self-Evolving Memory updates memory through an evolving policy. Based on this framework, we synthesize recurring design patterns and derive principles for balancing source fidelity with abstraction, maintaining temporal validity and provenance, grounding memory use in recoverable evidence, and evolving memory policies over time. Furthermore, we discuss the challenges and potential future directions for the memory mechanism of multimodal agents.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

However, enabling multimodal agents to continually acquire useful information from complex, heterogeneous multimodal data remains a significant challenge as they move toward long-horizon tasks and lifelong settings. Furthermore, we discuss the challenges and potential future directions for the memory mechanism of multimodal agents.

</details>

## 97. Task-Centric Multimodal Learning for Cognitive Impairment Detection

**中文标题**: Task-Centric Multimodal Learning for Cognitive Impairment Detection

**作者**: Sankalp Vaish, Rishank Singh, Sumukh Veeramalla, Caroline Summerour, John A. Batsis et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20906v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20906v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20906v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.CL

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dementia affects millions of older adults worldwide, yet early detection remains difficult outside clinical settings. Traditional models primarily rely on text or voice-based responses, capturing only a limited view of human cognition despite the inherently multimodal nature of human expression. This work proposes a contactless, multimodal screening framework that leverages video, audio, and text signals collected during structured voice-based interactions with Amazon Alexa. By incorporating these modalities, our approach captures a more holistic representation of human behavior encompassing speech patterns, vocal characteristics, facial expressions, and linguistic structure, which may enable more accurate detection of cognitive decline. Different participants completed three conversational tasks designed to probe spontaneous speech, comprehension, and recall. We pursued three objectives: (1) evaluate which of the three Alexa tasks provides the most informative cognitive signals, (2) compare the effectiveness of unimodal models, and (3) assess the benefits of multimodal fusion models. Task-level analysis shows that the video model achieves its best performance on Command-Reading, with an accuracy of 0.65 (AUC 0.69), while the audio model performs best on Question-Answering, reaching an accuracy of 0.67 (AUC 0.73). In contrast, the multimodal model demonstrates consistently stable performance across tasks, achieving an accuracy of 0.76 (AUC 0.75) on Command-Reading and 0.76 (AUC 0.73) on Question-Answering. These findings demonstrate that multimodal behavioral cues captured through naturalistic interactions can reliably distinguish MCI from healthy aging, highlighting the limitations of traditional diagnostic paradigms and supporting the shift toward multimodal, real-world cognitive assessment frameworks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 98. CogDrift-4D: Hierarchical Multimodal Modeling of Temporal Behavioral Decay for MCI Detection

**中文标题**: CogDrift-4D: Hierarchical Multimodal Modeling of Temporal Behavioral Decay for MCI Detection

**作者**: Sankalp Vaish, Sumukh Veeramalla, Rishank Singh, Caroline Summerour, John A. Batsis et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20877v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20877v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20877v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.CL

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Early detection of mild cognitive impairment remains a critical bottleneck in dementia care, demanding scalable tools capable of capturing subtle behavioral decay. To capture the subtle temporal changes of cognitive decline, we introduce a behavioral dataset collected across seven unscripted multimodal interactions over an 18-month horizon. This dataset formalizes continuous, multi-session interactions into a dense 4D tensor (Batch * Session * Time * Feature) that explicitly preserves concurrent local dynamics alongside long-term changes. To effectively process these complex behavioral patterns, we propose CogDrift-4D: a novel, hierarchical 4D Transformer framework. To capture the complex trajectory of cognitive decline, CogDrift-4D implements a dual-level behavioral fatigue and drift modeling architecture. At the intra-session level, the model computes localized least-squares regression slopes to quantify the acute ``fading'' of frame-level facial action units and acoustic energy. At the inter-session level, a recursive cross-attention feedback encoder acts as a cumulative fatigue accumulator, mapping the patient's continuous behavioral drift across successive sessions. To mitigate environmental noise and track failures across sessions, a localized Quality Gate dynamically filters low-integrity features before final fusion, also an early fusion that integrates semantic insights with raw physical dynamics. Evaluated on structured verbal and linguistic interactions, our architecture successfully detects MCI with a classification accuracy of 0.733 +- 0.13 and an F1-score of 0.716 +- 0.14, outperforming advanced baselines. The framework provides a mathematically rigorous, scalable, and robust foundation for home-based, real-time dementia screening.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>
