To mitigate environmental noise and track failures across sessions, a localized Quality Gate dynamically filters low-integrity features before final fusion, also an early fusion that integrates semantic insights with raw physical dynamics. Evaluated on structured verbal and linguistic interactions, our architecture successfully detects MCI with a classification accuracy of 0.733 +- 0.13 and an F1-score of 0.716 +- 0.14, outperforming advanced baselines. The framework provides a mathematically rigorous, scalable, and robust foundation for home-based, real-time dementia screening.

</details>

---

## 99. Complete or Sparse: A Tale of Two Identifiabilities

**中文标题**: Complete or Sparse: A Tale of Two Identifiabilities

**作者**: Junze Zhou, David Klindt

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20851v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20851v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20851v1)

**类别**: stat.ML, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Understanding when neural networks learn interpretable representations is central to mechanistic interpretability. We study this through identifiability: when can supervised learning recover the true latent variables underlying the data? We introduce two data generating processes distinguished by their causal direction where either labels cause latents (generative) or latents cause labels (discriminative). When the representation has at least as many dimensions as the latent space, we prove that the optimal encoders are linear in the latents. Conversely, when dimensions are insufficient, identifiability generically fails and encoders become nonlinear. We then show that additional constraints yield recovery of individual latent variables, not just linear mixtures. In the generative setting, non-negative activations and energy efficiency force each neuron to encode exactly one latent, providing a precise account of selective grandmother cell-like responses. In the discriminative setting, when latents are sparse and the true measurement matrix satisfies the spark condition, the learned encoder inherits compressed sensing structure, enabling sparse dictionary learning to perfectly recover individual latents. This constitutes, to our knowledge, the first identifiability result for undercomplete nonlinear independent component analysis. Our framework unifies nonlinear ICA, compressed sensing, and neural network interpretability, delineating exactly when representations transition from nonlinear distortions to linear representations and, under additional constraints or steps, to clean, axis-aligned codes. Theoretical predictions are validated on synthetic benchmarks and on activations drawn from vision models, large language models, and macaque inferotemporal cortex.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Understanding when neural networks learn interpretable representations is central to mechanistic interpretability. We study this through identifiability: when can supervised learning recover the true latent variables underlying the data? We introduce two data generating processes distinguished by their causal direction where either labels cause latents (generative) or latents cause labels (discriminative). When the representation has at least as many dimensions as the latent space, we prove that the optimal encoders are linear in the latents. Conversely, when dimensions are insufficient, identifiability generically fails and encoders become nonlinear. We then show that additional constraints yield recovery of individual latent variables, not just linear mixtures. In the generative setting, non-negative activations and energy efficiency force each neuron to encode exactly one latent, providing a precise account of selective grandmother cell-like responses. In the discriminative setting, when latents are sparse and the true measurement matrix satisfies the spark condition, the learned encoder inherits compressed sensing structure, enabling sparse dictionary learning to perfectly recover individual latents. This constitutes, to our knowledge, the first identifiability result for undercomplete nonlinear independent component analysis. Our framework unifies nonlinear ICA, compressed sensing, and neural network interpretability, delineating exactly when representations transition from nonlinear distortions to linear representations and, under additional constraints or steps, to clean, axis-aligned codes. Theoretical predictions are validated on synthetic benchmarks and on activations drawn from vision models, large language models, and macaque inferotemporal cortex.

</details>

## 100. EEG-Based Prediction of Music Enjoyment Using Deep Learning with Explainable Temporal Analysis

**中文标题**: EEG-Based Prediction of Music Enjoyment Using Deep Learning with Explainable Temporal Analysis

**作者**: Parshav Singla, Simranjit Kaur, Maanya Walia,  Niharika

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20795v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20795v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20795v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.HC, cs.SD

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Understanding why listeners enjoy music is difficult because the experience is subjective and changes as a piece unfolds. Electroencephalography (EEG) offers a non-invasive way to study neural responses during listening, yet earlier work has mostly treated music emotion as a categorical label or relied on black-box models. We formulate enjoyment prediction as a regression problem on the public MUSIN-G dataset, which contains 128-channel EEG from 20 listeners together with song-level enjoyment ratings. Six deep learning models were compared under a participant-wise protocol, in which 15 listeners were used for training and validation and 5 were held out for testing. DeepConvNet, CNN-LSTM and GNN-CNN reached the lowest mean absolute error (0.97 to 0.99 on the 1 to 5 scale), while the temporal convolutional network (TCN) had the highest Pearson correlation with the ratings (r = 0.21). Gradient-based and occlusion-based explanations, averaged over all held-out windows, identify the time segments within each two-second window that most influence the TCN predictions. The study provides a reproducible cross-listener benchmark for EEG-based enjoyment regression and shows how explainability analysis complements standard metrics.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Understanding why listeners enjoy music is difficult because the experience is subjective and changes as a piece unfolds. Electroencephalography (EEG) offers a non-invasive way to study neural responses during listening, yet earlier work has mostly treated music emotion as a categorical label or relied on black-box models. We formulate enjoyment prediction as a regression problem on the public MUSIN-G dataset, which contains 128-channel EEG from 20 listeners together with song-level enjoyment ratings. Six deep learning models were compared under a participant-wise protocol, in which 15 listeners were used for training and validation and 5 were held out for testing. DeepConvNet, CNN-LSTM and GNN-CNN reached the lowest mean absolute error (0.97 to 0.99 on the 1 to 5 scale), while the temporal convolutional network (TCN) had the highest Pearson correlation with the ratings (r = 0.21). Gradient-based and occlusion-based explanations, averaged over all held-out windows, identify the time segments within each two-second window that most influence the TCN predictions. The study provides a reproducible cross-listener benchmark for EEG-based enjoyment regression and shows how explainability analysis complements standard metrics.

</details>

## 101. Revisiting Simple Baselines for Relational Deep Learning

**中文标题**: Revisiting Simple Baselines for Relational Deep Learning

**作者**: Viet The Nguyen, Vijay Prakash Dwivedi, Gunther Gust

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20534v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20534v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20534v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Relational deep learning has emerged as a powerful paradigm for learning end-to-end predictive models over relational databases by representing them as temporal heterogeneous graphs. Recent advances based on graph neural networks and Transformers have achieved strong performance across diverse domains. However, the effectiveness of simpler relational baselines remains underexplored, making it difficult to assess how much architectural complexity is needed to achieve strong predictive performance. Motivated by the observation that predictive signals arise from target attributes, relational attributes, and historical labels, we revisit how effectively these signals can be exploited using simple propagation methods. We adapt fixed low-pass feature propagation to relational entity graphs and train an MLP for prediction. The resulting predictions are further refined using Correct-and-Smooth, a graph-based label propagation algorithm that incorporates historical labels over task-specific meta-graphs connecting target entities. Across classification and regression tasks on RelBench, an MLP trained on neighborhood-filtered features outperforms one using only target-entity features on most tasks and recovers a substantial portion of the performance of heterogeneous GNNs. Correct-and-Smooth provides further gains from historical labels, although applying it requires a separately constructed meta-graph because connections among target entities are not directly specified by the relational entity graph. Together, our studies revisit the effectiveness of established propagation methods as simple RDL baselines while identifying canonical meta-graph construction as a key challenge for effective label propagation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Relational deep learning has emerged as a powerful paradigm for learning end-to-end predictive models over relational databases by representing them as temporal heterogeneous graphs. Recent advances based on graph neural networks and Transformers have achieved strong performance across diverse domains. However, the effectiveness of simpler relational baselines remains underexplored, making it difficult to assess how much architectural complexity is needed to achieve strong predictive performance. Motivated by the observation that predictive signals arise from target attributes, relational attributes, and historical labels, we revisit how effectively these signals can be exploited using simple propagation methods. We adapt fixed low-pass feature propagation to relational entity graphs and train an MLP for prediction. The resulting predictions are further refined using Correct-and-Smooth, a graph-based label propagation algorithm that incorporates historical labels over task-specific meta-graphs connecting target entities. Across classification and regression tasks on RelBench, an MLP trained on neighborhood-filtered features outperforms one using only target-entity features on most tasks and recovers a substantial portion of the performance of heterogeneous GNNs. Correct-and-Smooth provides further gains from historical labels, although applying it requires a separately constructed meta-graph because connections among target entities are not directly specified by the relational entity graph. Together, our studies revisit the effectiveness of established propagation methods as simple RDL baselines while identifying canonical meta-graph construction as a key challenge for effective label propagation.

</details>

## 102. Graph-Enhanced Disease Trajectory Discovery and Temporal Endpoint Forecasting from Longitudinal Electronic Health Records

**中文标题**: Graph-Enhanced Disease Trajectory Discovery and Temporal Endpoint Forecasting from Longitudinal Electronic Health Records

**作者**: Jin-Jia Zheng, Vincent S. Tseng

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20502v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20502v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20502v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Longitudinal electronic health records (EHRs) can reveal disease-progression structure, while useful forecasting requires leakage-controlled discovery, censor-aware outcomes, and inspectable trajectory evidence. We developed a graph-enhanced framework in MIMIC-IV v3.1 that splits patients before graph construction, learns train-only consecutive-admission transitions, and freezes endpoint-specific branching-motif policies selected on validation data. The final catalog contained 99,527 motifs, and a time-aware Transformer retrieved at most 24 patient-compatible motifs per endpoint through endpoint-specific cross-attention. Across five training seeds, micro-AUPRC increased from 0.9330 to 0.9357, while event-time MAE decreased from 676.9 to 657.7 days (reduction 19.2 days; 95% paired patient-cluster bootstrap CI, 13.8-24.8). Timing effects were heterogeneous: MAE worsened for events within 730 days but improved by 58.9 days for events occurring after 730 days. In an exploratory internal temporal split, branch conditioning reduced conditional event-time MAE by 26.9 days (95% CI, 23.0-30.8) without evidence of better micro-AUPRC. Corrected survival evaluation over the supported 0-2,555-day interval did not establish macro C-index or integrated Brier score superiority over a matched history-only discrete-hazard model. A directed, degree-matched GNN repair substantially improved unseen-edge discovery, yet replacing GNN-derived scores within fixed motif identities did not improve downstream forecasting. These results position branching motifs as structured trajectory context whose main value is long-range timing support rather than uniformly stronger endpoint discrimination, survival prediction, or causal explanation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Longitudinal electronic health records (EHRs) can reveal disease-progression structure, while useful forecasting requires leakage-controlled discovery, censor-aware outcomes, and inspectable trajectory evidence. We developed a graph-enhanced framework in MIMIC-IV v3.1 that splits patients before graph construction, learns train-only consecutive-admission transitions, and freezes endpoint-specific branching-motif policies selected on validation data. The final catalog contained 99,527 motifs, and a time-aware Transformer retrieved at most 24 patient-compatible motifs per endpoint through endpoint-specific cross-attention. Across five training seeds, micro-AUPRC increased from 0.9330 to 0.9357, while event-time MAE decreased from 676.9 to 657.7 days (reduction 19.2 days; 95% paired patient-cluster bootstrap CI, 13.8-24.8). Timing effects were heterogeneous: MAE worsened for events within 730 days but improved by 58.9 days for events occurring after 730 days. In an exploratory internal temporal split, branch conditioning reduced conditional event-time MAE by 26.9 days (95% CI, 23.0-30.8) without evidence of better micro-AUPRC. Corrected survival evaluation over the supported 0-2,555-day interval did not establish macro C-index or integrated Brier score superiority over a matched history-only discrete-hazard model. These results position branching motifs as structured trajectory context whose main value is long-range timing support rather than uniformly stronger endpoint discrimination, survival prediction, or causal explanation.

</details>

## 103. Kandinsky S2V 1.0: Audio-Driven Avatar Video Generation

**中文标题**: Kandinsky S2V 1.0: Audio-Driven Avatar Video Generation

**作者**: Nikolay Semenov, Artem Riabinin, Sergey Bassenko, Anna Averchenkova, Julia Agafonova et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19290v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19290v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19290v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We present Kandinsky S2V 1.0, an open family of speech-to-video models for controllable audio-driven human animation, comprising Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Lite (2B parameters) and Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro (21B parameters). From a single reference image, a speech waveform, and a text prompt, the models generate long-form 25 FPS video in which lip motion, expression, pose, and gesture follow the speech. The architecture draws text and audio conditioning from one Qwen2.5-Omni model and adds a reference-aware three-scale visual memory. Training starts from the Kandinsky 5.0 Video foundation models [1] and combines mixed reference-memory layouts with an area-adaptive face loss. The training corpus contains 1.17M single-speaker clips selected by active-speaker detection, with captions generated directly in English and Russian. A side-by-side human evaluation against the strongest open-source baseline and two proprietary commercial models shows that Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro performs comparably to or better than these models on average and is also preferred for lip synchronization. On common speech to video benchmarks, Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Lite and Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro rank among the top performing models according to mean rank over five reported metrics. Beyond the base models, we provide π-Flow distilled variants of both Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Lite and Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro. The distilled Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro model generates video in 16 sampling steps without classifier-free guidance, achieving a 5.4 times end-to-end speedup over the base model, while matching or exceeding it on nearly all reported benchmark metrics. To accelerate open research and deployment in speech-to-video generation, we release the code, model checkpoints, and diffusers integration under the MIT license.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We present Kandinsky S2V 1.0, an open family of speech-to-video models for controllable audio-driven human animation, comprising Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Lite (2B parameters) and Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro (21B parameters). From a single reference image, a speech waveform, and a text prompt, the models generate long-form 25 FPS video in which lip motion, expression, pose, and gesture follow the speech. The architecture draws text and audio conditioning from one Qwen2.5-Omni model and adds a reference-aware three-scale visual memory. Training starts from the Kandinsky 5.0 Video foundation models [1] and combines mixed reference-memory layouts with an area-adaptive face loss. The training corpus contains 1.17M single-speaker clips selected by active-speaker detection, with captions generated directly in English and Russian. A side-by-side human evaluation against the strongest open-source baseline and two proprietary commercial models shows that Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro performs comparably to or better than these models on average and is also preferred for lip synchronization. On common speech to video benchmarks, Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Lite and Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro rank among the top performing models according to mean rank over five reported metrics. Beyond the base models, we provide π-Flow distilled variants of both Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Lite and Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro. The distilled Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro model generates video in 16 sampling steps without classifier-free guidance, achieving a 5.4 times end-to-end speedup over the base model, while matching or exceeding it on nearly all reported benchmark metrics. To accelerate open research and deployment in speech-to-video generation, we release the code, model checkpoints, and diffusers integration under the MIT license.

## 104. Deep Learning for Atmospheric Turbulence Mitigation: A Comparison Between GANs and Transformers

**中文标题**: Deep Learning for Atmospheric Turbulence Mitigation: A Comparison Between GANs and Transformers

**作者**: Juan S. Carrillo, Jérôme Gilles

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19274v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19274v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19274v1)

**类别**: eess.IV, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Atmospheric turbulence creates complex spatial and temporal distortions that significantly reduce image quality. This work compares two deep learning methods for turbulence reduction: Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) and Transformer-based models. Both methods are trained and tested on the SOTIS dataset, which includes multi-frame sequences under different turbulence conditions with ground truth references. Our results show an important finding: GAN-based models perform better in severely degraded, low-quality situations, while Transformer-based models achieve better results in moderate to high-quality settings, especially in correcting geometric distortions. We also enhance the Turbulence Mitigation Transformer (TMT) by introducing a refined training strategy, an augmented input design that includes a temporally averaged frame as prior structural information, and a modified loss function that incorporates the Structural Similarity Index (SSIM). These improvements lead to consistent performance gains across various turbulence conditions. Overall, this study provides an in-depth evaluation of deep learning methods for turbulence reduction and offers practical guidance for selecting models in different imaging environments.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Atmospheric turbulence creates complex spatial and temporal distortions that significantly reduce image quality. This work compares two deep learning methods for turbulence reduction: Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) and Transformer-based models. Both methods are trained and tested on the SOTIS dataset, which includes multi-frame sequences under different turbulence conditions with ground truth references. Our results show an important finding: GAN-based models perform better in severely degraded, low-quality situations, while Transformer-based models achieve better results in moderate to high-quality settings, especially in correcting geometric distortions. We also enhance the Turbulence Mitigation Transformer (TMT) by introducing a refined training strategy, an augmented input design that includes a temporally averaged frame as prior structural information, and a modified loss function that incorporates the Structural Similarity Index (SSIM). These improvements lead to consistent performance gains across various turbulence conditions. Overall, this study provides an in-depth evaluation of deep learning methods for turbulence reduction and offers practical guidance for selecting models in different imaging environments.

</details>

## 105. Long-horizon Video Generation with Slot Memory Addressed By Region Tokens

**中文标题**: Long-horizon Video Generation with Slot Memory Addressed By Region Tokens

**作者**: Eren Cetin, Julius Erbach, Vukasin Bozic, Hyojun Go, Zixiang Zhao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19257v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19257v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19257v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Memory for long-horizon video generation must satisfy two competing requirements: it must remain bounded so generation can continue indefinitely, while still recovering what was observed at a place when the camera returns, regardless of how long ago it was seen. Reconstruction-based memories provide spatial recall by caching and reprojecting explicit geometry, but require growing storage and depend on estimated 3D structure. Recurrent memories keep a constant-size state, but retention is governed by temporal recurrence, causing old observations to fade with time. We introduce SMART, a slot memory addressed by region tokens, which combines bounded storage with spatially addressed recall. SMART stores content in a constant-size slot recurrence whose writes are addressed by the observing camera and whose reads are queried by the rendering camera, without constructing an explicit 3D representation or maintaining a growing cache. Consequently, recall is conditioned on inter-view geometry rather than on recency. Each read additionally provides a confidence estimate that conditions a chunk-causal diffusion transformer, allowing it to selectively trust retrieved content while relying on its generative prior elsewhere. On long trajectories in Memory Maze and Minecraft, SMART maintains high-fidelity recall far beyond its training horizon while using constant memory and per-step cost.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Memory for long-horizon video generation must satisfy two competing requirements: it must remain bounded so generation can continue indefinitely, while still recovering what was observed at a place when the camera returns, regardless of how long ago it was seen. Reconstruction-based memories provide spatial recall by caching and reprojecting explicit geometry, but require growing storage and depend on estimated 3D structure. Recurrent memories keep a constant-size state, but retention is governed by temporal recurrence, causing old observations to fade with time. We introduce SMART, a slot memory addressed by region tokens, which combines bounded storage with spatially addressed recall. SMART stores content in a constant-size slot recurrence whose writes are addressed by the observing camera and whose reads are queried by the rendering camera, without constructing an explicit 3D representation or maintaining a growing cache. Consequently, recall is conditioned on inter-view geometry rather than on recency. Each read additionally provides a confidence estimate that conditions a chunk-causal diffusion transformer, allowing it to selectively trust retrieved content while relying on its generative prior elsewhere. On long trajectories in Memory Maze and Minecraft, SMART maintains high-fidelity recall far beyond its training horizon while using constant memory and per-step cost.

</details>

## 106. Into the LidarVerse: A Real-Time Unified LiDAR World Model Across Tasks and Sensors

**中文标题**: Into the LidarVerse: A Real-Time Unified LiDAR World Model Across Tasks and Sensors

**作者**: Henry Che, Xuanchi Ren, Sanja Fidler, Shenlong Wang, Laura Leal-Taixé et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19201v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19201v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19201v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We present LidarVerse, a real-time unified LiDAR world model for sensor simulation across tasks and sensors. Existing LiDAR simulators largely follow one of two complementary paradigms: reconstruction-based methods preserve the geometry of a captured scene but require lengthy per-scene optimization, whereas generative methods amortize learning across data but lack the consistency needed for long-horizon rollouts. LidarVerse brings both paradigms into a single, unified conditional generative framework. A universal tokenizer maps LiDAR range sequences into a shared latent space, where a bidirectional teacher learns a common interface for conditioning on a LiDAR prefix, an HD-Map, a persistent spatial cache, and optionally, text. Varying the available scene evidence turns the same model into a generator of new worlds, a point-cloud forecaster, or a generalizable resimulator of observed scenes without per-scene optimization. We distill the teacher into a four-step autoregressive causal student and maintain both a rolling temporal K/V cache and a world-aligned spatial cache, enabling persistent long-horizon rollouts with real-time inference at 11 FPS on a single H100 GPU. LidarVerse achieves state-of-the-art performance on most reported metrics across all three evaluation protocols, while its tokenizer supports 32-, 64-, and 128-beam LiDAR and generalizes to an unseen scanning pattern. These results demonstrate that generative and reconstruction-grounded LiDAR simulation can be realized within one model, providing the core capabilities for future closed-loop sensor simulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We present LidarVerse, a real-time unified LiDAR world model for sensor simulation across tasks and sensors. Existing LiDAR simulators largely follow one of two complementary paradigms: reconstruction-based methods preserve the geometry of a captured scene but require lengthy per-scene optimization, whereas generative methods amortize learning across data but lack the consistency needed for long-horizon rollouts. LidarVerse brings both paradigms into a single, unified conditional generative framework. A universal tokenizer maps LiDAR range sequences into a shared latent space, where a bidirectional teacher learns a common interface for conditioning on a LiDAR prefix, an HD-Map, a persistent spatial cache, and optionally, text. Varying the available scene evidence turns the same model into a generator of new worlds, a point-cloud forecaster, or a generalizable resimulator of observed scenes without per-scene optimization. We distill the teacher into a four-step autoregressive causal student and maintain both a rolling temporal K/V cache and a world-aligned spatial cache, enabling persistent long-horizon rollouts with real-time inference at 11 FPS on a single H100 GPU. LidarVerse achieves state-of-the-art performance on most reported metrics across all three evaluation protocols, while its tokenizer supports 32-, 64-, and 128-beam LiDAR and generalizes to an unseen scanning pattern. These results demonstrate that generative and reconstruction-grounded LiDAR simulation can be realized within one model, providing the core capabilities for future closed-loop sensor simulation.

</details>

## 107. The Gap Is in the Geometry: A Convergent Meta-Analysis of Multi-Facet Aerial and Remote Sensing Video Understanding

**中文标题**: The Gap Is in the Geometry: A Convergent Meta-Analysis of Multi-Facet Aerial and Remote Sensing Video Understanding

**作者**: Georgios Angelidis, Aggelos Psiris, Maria Vakalopoulou, Georgios Th. Papadopoulos, Giorgos Sfikas et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19102v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19102v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19102v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Aerial and remote sensing video is increasingly evaluated like web video, by posing multiple-choice questions to multimodal large language models (MLLMs) over sampled frames and reporting accuracy. This paper argues that this recipe measures the wrong aspect, and supports the claim with a convergent meta-analysis of a broad set of published results, organized in four evidence tiers, in which a claim becomes a position only when collected evidence agrees. Parameter scale exhibits a median of +3.4 accuracy points per tenfold increase on aerial benchmarks, far below a human gap of at least 19 points, whereas acquisition conditions control perception, as 315 of 318 evaluations in 19 within-paper contrasts lose accuracy when conditions such as altitude, view, object size or time horizon become harder. No aerial benchmark in the corpus combines video input with per-task human and random baselines, competence collapses from answering to acting in closed-loop navigation, and the same MLLMs score higher on the aerial benchmark than on ground spatial ones, but lower on studies that test cross-view geometry. From these findings the current study derives five falsifiable principles and proposes the AVU-Card, a reporting card for aerial video benchmarks

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Aerial and remote sensing video is increasingly evaluated like web video, by posing multiple-choice questions to multimodal large language models (MLLMs) over sampled frames and reporting accuracy. This paper argues that this recipe measures the wrong aspect, and supports the claim with a convergent meta-analysis of a broad set of published results, organized in four evidence tiers, in which a claim becomes a position only when collected evidence agrees. Parameter scale exhibits a median of +3.4 accuracy points per tenfold increase on aerial benchmarks, far below a human gap of at least 19 points, whereas acquisition conditions control perception, as 315 of 318 evaluations in 19 within-paper contrasts lose accuracy when conditions such as altitude, view, object size or time horizon become harder. No aerial benchmark in the corpus combines video input with per-task human and random baselines, competence collapses from answering to acting in closed-loop navigation, and the same MLLMs score higher on the aerial benchmark than on ground spatial ones, but lower on studies that test cross-view geometry. From these findings the current study derives five falsifiable principles and proposes the AVU-Card, a reporting card for aerial video benchmarks

</details>

## 108. WorldMemBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Memory of Video World Models

**中文标题**: WorldMemBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Memory of Video World Models

**作者**: Nan Zhou, Jianxiong Gao, Qiuyu Wang, Wen Wang, Jiahao Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19063v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19063v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19063v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

As video models enable longer and more realistic interactive experiences, maintaining a world consistent with its history becomes essential. This requires both preserving previously observed content and ensuring coherent world evolution over time. World memory is a critical capability to evaluate alongside visual fidelity and video duration. We introduce WorldMemBench, a benchmark for systematically evaluating world memory in video world models. Across 260 test cases, it assesses static memory through the retention of human identity and appearance, object appearance and geometry, and environmental content and geometry, and dynamic memory through event progression and state persistence. Using controlled camera departures and multi-revisit trajectories, the benchmark evaluates static content against fixed initial references and dynamic events against expected state transitions. It combines appearance and geometry metrics with event-specific assessments, and reports video quality and camera-following accuracy as complementary diagnostics. Evaluation of nine representative models shows that strong static retention does not imply reliable dynamic state evolution, while repeated revisits reveal memory degradation beyond the first return. Human studies support the alignment between our automatic evaluations and human judgments. WorldMemBench provides a systematic testbed for diagnosing memory failures and guiding the development of persistent, temporally coherent video worlds.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

As video models enable longer and more realistic interactive experiences, maintaining a world consistent with its history becomes essential. This requires both preserving previously observed content and ensuring coherent world evolution over time. World memory is a critical capability to evaluate alongside visual fidelity and video duration. We introduce WorldMemBench, a benchmark for systematically evaluating world memory in video world models. Across 260 test cases, it assesses static memory through the retention of human identity and appearance, object appearance and geometry, and environmental content and geometry, and dynamic memory through event progression and state persistence. Using controlled camera departures and multi-revisit trajectories, the benchmark evaluates static content against fixed initial references and dynamic events against expected state transitions. It combines appearance and geometry metrics with event-specific assessments, and reports video quality and camera-following accuracy as complementary diagnostics. Evaluation of nine representative models shows that strong static retention does not imply reliable dynamic state evolution, while repeated revisits reveal memory degradation beyond the first return. Human studies support the alignment between our automatic evaluations and human judgments. WorldMemBench provides a systematic testbed for diagnosing memory failures and guiding the development of persistent, temporally coherent video worlds.

</details>

## 109. Focus, Align, and Diffuse: Time-Series-Aware Keyframe Diffusion for Cardiac Dynamic Synthesis from Sparse Observations

**中文标题**: Focus, Align, and Diffuse: Time-Series-Aware Keyframe Diffusion for Cardiac Dynamic Synthesis from Sparse Observations

**作者**: Junkai Liu, Haofan Wu, Nay Aung, Joao A. C. Lima, Steffen E. Petersen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19050v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19050v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19050v1)

**类别**: eess.IV, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dense cardiac dynamics are essential for assessing cardiac function, yet in practice clinicians often observe only extremely sparse measurements. However, recovering full dynamics from such sparsity is a highly ill-posed problem due to the lack of temporal cues. While auxiliary time-series signals like ECG provide physiological guidance, effectively exploiting them is challenging due to the cross-modal representation gap and phase misalignment. In this work, we present CardioFAD, a Focus--Align--Diffuse paradigm that reformulates sparse cardiac dynamic synthesis as a recoverability--alignability anchor discovery problem. Our core insight is that resolving extreme temporal ambiguity requires a compact set of anchor states that are both sufficient to recover the visual trajectory and reliable for ECG--visual alignment. We therefore learn motion-salient keyframes under two complementary principles: trajectory sufficiency, which preserves trajectory-critical intra-modal motion content, and anchor alignment, which promotes consistent inter-modal correspondence at these anchors. We implement the Focus and Align modules optimized by the two aspects. Diffuse then performs two-stage generation by first synthesizing keyframes and subsequently interpolating the remaining frames. Extensive experiments on cardiac MRI and echocardiography videos demonstrate superior visual quality, temporal coherence, and improved functional consistency under the extreme one-shot regime.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Dense cardiac dynamics are essential for assessing cardiac function, yet in practice clinicians often observe only extremely sparse measurements. However, recovering full dynamics from such sparsity is a highly ill-posed problem due to the lack of temporal cues. While auxiliary time-series signals like ECG provide physiological guidance, effectively exploiting them is challenging due to the cross-modal representation gap and phase misalignment. In this work, we present CardioFAD, a Focus--Align--Diffuse paradigm that reformulates sparse cardiac dynamic synthesis as a recoverability--alignability anchor discovery problem. Our core insight is that resolving extreme temporal ambiguity requires a compact set of anchor states that are both sufficient to recover the visual trajectory and reliable for ECG--visual alignment. We therefore learn motion-salient keyframes under two complementary principles: trajectory sufficiency, which preserves trajectory-critical intra-modal motion content, and anchor alignment, which promotes consistent inter-modal correspondence at these anchors. We implement the Focus and Align modules optimized by the two aspects. Diffuse then performs two-stage generation by first synthesizing keyframes and subsequently interpolating the remaining frames. Extensive experiments on cardiac MRI and echocardiography videos demonstrate superior visual quality, temporal coherence, and improved functional consistency under the extreme one-shot regime.

</details>

## 110. Perceptual Characterization of Scan-induced Artifacts in Dynamic 3D Meshes

**中文标题**: Perceptual Characterization of Scan-induced Artifacts in Dynamic 3D Meshes

**作者**: Huong Hoang, Truong Nguyen, Pamela Cosman

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19173v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19173v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19173v1)

**类别**: eess.IV, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dynamic meshes reconstructed from real-world scenes often exhibit scan-induced artifacts such as surface jitter and missing geometry, which strongly impact perceived visual quality. Objective assessment remains challenging due to the absence of artifact-free ground truth and limited perceptual understanding of artifact interactions. This paper presents a systematic and controlled perceptual study of scan-induced artifacts in dynamic meshes, focusing on surface jitter and holes. To enable systematic analysis, we introduce a physically grounded artifact synthesis framework in which holes are synthesized using a reconstruction uncertainty model derived from multi-view geometry. The study reveals asymmetric cross-artifact interactions, non-additive integration, and motion-dependent temporal masking. Based on these findings, we propose a compact, perceptually grounded no-reference quality assessment framework that encodes the observed perceptual mechanisms.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Dynamic meshes reconstructed from real-world scenes often exhibit scan-induced artifacts such as surface jitter and missing geometry, which strongly impact perceived visual quality. Objective assessment remains challenging due to the absence of artifact-free ground truth and limited perceptual understanding of artifact interactions. This paper presents a systematic and controlled perceptual study of scan-induced artifacts in dynamic meshes, focusing on surface jitter and holes. To enable systematic analysis, we introduce a physically grounded artifact synthesis framework in which holes are synthesized using a reconstruction uncertainty model derived from multi-view geometry. The study reveals asymmetric cross-artifact interactions, non-additive integration, and motion-dependent temporal masking. Based on these findings, we propose a compact, perceptually grounded no-reference quality assessment framework that encodes the observed perceptual mechanisms.

</details>

## 111. OmniCompass: A Holistic Benchmark for Omni-Modal Video Grounding and Understanding

**中文标题**: OmniCompass: A Holistic Benchmark for Omni-Modal Video Grounding and Understanding

**作者**: Xuyang Liu, Shihao Wang, Xinyu Wei, Minghan Li, Zeqing Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18829v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18829v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18829v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

With the rapid development of multimodal large language models (MLLMs), evaluating their video understanding capabilities has become increasingly important. Existing video understanding benchmarks cover only part of the evaluation dimensions, limiting systematic diagnosis of omni-modal video understanding. We introduce OmniCompass, a holistic benchmark for omni-modal video grounding and understanding. OmniCompass defines two evaluation tracks: OmniCompass-VL assesses vision-language models on tasks requiring visual evidence, while OmniCompass-AV assesses omni-modal models on tasks requiring joint audio-visual evidence. Each track includes three complementary tasks: temporal grounding (TG), spatial grounding (SG), and question answering (QA), assessing when a referenced target appears, where it is, and what happens. QA covers five perception and three reasoning categories in OmniCompass-VL, and three perception and two reasoning categories in OmniCompass-AV. OmniCompass-VL contains 820 videos and 2,460 instances across domains and three duration types: short, medium, and long. A narrative- and audio-rich subset forms OmniCompass-AV with 240 videos and 720 instances. Each instance is evaluated under two paired settings: text-only and text+image, specifying the same target through text and visual-language guidance. All instances are manually annotated with multi-round quality verification. Evaluations of 50 MLLMs show that open-source models still lag behind proprietary counterparts, particularly in audio-visual understanding. Precise temporal and spatial grounding remains challenging even for leading models, and their predictions still exhibit inconsistencies across the paired target specifications.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

With the rapid development of multimodal large language models (MLLMs), evaluating their video understanding capabilities has become increasingly important. Existing video understanding benchmarks cover only part of the evaluation dimensions, limiting systematic diagnosis of omni-modal video understanding. OmniCompass defines two evaluation tracks: OmniCompass-VL assesses vision-language models on tasks requiring visual evidence, while OmniCompass-AV assesses omni-modal models on tasks requiring joint audio-visual evidence. Each track includes three complementary tasks: temporal grounding (TG), spatial grounding (SG), and question answering (QA), assessing when a referenced target appears, where it is, and what happens. QA covers five perception and three reasoning categories in OmniCompass-VL, and three perception and two reasoning categories in OmniCompass-AV. A narrative- and audio-rich subset forms OmniCompass-AV with 240 videos and 720 instances. All instances are manually annotated with multi-round quality verification. Evaluations of 50 MLLMs show that open-source models still lag behind proprietary counterparts, particularly in audio-visual understanding. Precise temporal and spatial grounding remains challenging even for leading models, and their predictions still exhibit inconsistencies across the paired target specifications.

</details>

## 112. DualAffect: Dual-Path Paralinguistic Integration for Generative Audiovisual Emotion Understanding

**中文标题**: DualAffect: Dual-Path Paralinguistic Integration for Generative Audiovisual Emotion Understanding

**作者**: Yibo Wang, Jisheng Dang, Bimei Wang, Junhong Liu, Jiongpei Hou et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18073v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18073v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18073v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/YiboWang2002/DualAffect.

**类别**: cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Understanding human emotion is essential for multimodal systems that interpret both the content of an interaction and the speaker's expressive state. However, connecting paralinguistic cues to generated answers remains challenging, particularly when vocal expression diverges from visual appearance. We propose DualAffect, a dual-path architecture that integrates visually conditioned acoustic features and temporally organized acoustic tokens into a shared language decoder. The two pathways reuse the same acoustic encoding. The fusion pathway associates sound with visual content through visual queries, while the direct pathway compresses the valid acoustic sequence into ordered tokens through an independent projector. This design gives the decoder access to both joint audiovisual context and acoustic evidence organized along its own timeline. Mixed-task adaptation with task-dependent output preservation supports emotion recognition and general audiovisual question answering within the same model. DualAffect achieves an average metric of 67.89 on CA-MER and accuracies of 62.88% on the EmotionTalk test set and 49.87% on Daily-Omni. Pathway interventions show that the trained decoder uses direct acoustic evidence alongside existing fusion. With model parameters and fusion inputs fixed, zeroing the direct tokens reduces EmotionTalk validation accuracy by 9.75 percentage points and the CA-MER average metric by 2.66 points. The largest CA-MER subset effect occurs when the overall emotion agrees with audio but differs from vision. These findings identify acoustic representation organization as a practical design axis for connecting paralinguistic perception to unified audiovisual generation. Code is available at https://github.com/YiboWang2002/DualAffect.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

However, connecting paralinguistic cues to generated answers remains challenging, particularly when vocal expression diverges from visual appearance. ## 113. EgoExplorer: A Vision-Motion-Touch Observatory for Human Loco-Manipulation In Situ

**中文标题**: EgoExplorer: A Vision-Motion-Touch Observatory for Human Loco-Manipulation In Situ

**作者**: Yunsong Zhou, Xuekun Jiang, Haiqi Shen, Hangxu Liu, Chaojian Shi et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18872v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18872v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18872v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Learning humanoid loco-manipulation from human demonstrations rests on observing how locomotion, posture, and object interaction evolve together as tasks unfold. Scaling collection across diverse environments, in turn, favors lightweight, portable capture systems. Yet, existing datasets provide only partial views of this coordination. Thus, we introduce EgoExplorer, a 100-hour vision-motion-touch observatory of human loco-manipulation built with a wearable system. With an emphasis on professional activities, its continuous recordings align egocentric video with reconstructed whole-body motion, fine-grained finger articulation, and tactile observations, accompanied by step-level descriptions. To achieve this coverage with sparse body sensing, we develop the Calibrate-Complete-Constrain pipeline. Concretely, reusable calibration fixes participant geometry and sensor attachments before motion inference to disentangle mounting offsets from articulation. A temporal prior then completes unobserved joint motion, while layered constraints promote measurement agreement, temporal continuity, and geometric consistency, properties that motion plausibility alone does not guarantee. Against tracker-fitted references, the method reduces body-point error by 71.9% relative to the strongest baseline; matched evaluations also show gains in hand stability and contact estimation. Preliminary studies explore multimodal generation and conditioning alongside humanoid retargeting with whole-body control in simulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Learning humanoid loco-manipulation from human demonstrations rests on observing how locomotion, posture, and object interaction evolve together as tasks unfold. Scaling collection across diverse environments, in turn, favors lightweight, portable capture systems. Yet, existing datasets provide only partial views of this coordination. Thus, we introduce EgoExplorer, a 100-hour vision-motion-touch observatory of human loco-manipulation built with a wearable system. With an emphasis on professional activities, its continuous recordings align egocentric video with reconstructed whole-body motion, fine-grained finger articulation, and tactile observations, accompanied by step-level descriptions. To achieve this coverage with sparse body sensing, we develop the Calibrate-Complete-Constrain pipeline. Concretely, reusable calibration fixes participant geometry and sensor attachments before motion inference to disentangle mounting offsets from articulation. Against tracker-fitted references, the method reduces body-point error by 71.9% relative to the strongest baseline; matched evaluations also show gains in hand stability and contact estimation. Preliminary studies explore multimodal generation and conditioning alongside humanoid retargeting with whole-body control in simulation.

</details>

## 114. Contact Trajectory Prompting: In-Context Transfer of Contact-Rich Behaviors from a Single Demonstration

**中文标题**: Contact Trajectory Prompting: In-Context Transfer of Contact-Rich Behaviors from a Single Demonstration

**作者**: Xian Nie, Yujie Zang, Yuhang Zheng, Yupeng Zheng, Songen Gu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18848v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18848v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18848v1)

**项目主页**: https://ctp-robotics.github.io/project_page.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In-context robot learning enables robots to acquire new behaviors from demonstrations without test-time parameter updates. However, visual and kinematic specifications alone may not distinguish contact-rich behaviors that share similar motions but require different interaction intensities or patterns. We propose Contact Trajectory Prompting (CTP), a framework for one-shot transfer of contact-rich manipulation behaviors. CTP encodes motion and contact information from a single demonstration into an ordered contact trajectory prompt that captures their temporal evolution and specifies the desired physical interaction behavior. During execution, the policy uses online sensory feedback to retrieve interaction-relevant information from the prompt, conditioning closed-loop action generation on the demonstrated contact behavior. Across real-world wiping, rotation, and insertion tasks on objects excluded from training, CTP achieves 78.7% overall success, outperforming the strongest evaluated baseline by 13.0 percentage points. Changing only the reference demonstration enables CTP to follow contact specifications for both intensity and direction, including an intermediate wiping force level and clockwise or counterclockwise cap rotation. These results demonstrate that contact trajectories serve as effective behavioral specifications, extending in-context robot learning beyond motion imitation to physical interaction transfer. Project website: https://ctp-robotics.github.io/project_page.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 115. WAVE-0: Bridging VLA Policies and WAMs with Lightweight Latent Future Prediction

**中文标题**: WAVE-0: Bridging VLA Policies and WAMs with Lightweight Latent Future Prediction

**作者**: Haomin Wang, Anqiong Ji, Ke Wang, Zhongshan Liu, Ru Ying et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18568v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18568v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18568v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) policies excel at generalist robotic manipulation but generate actions without explicitly modeling their environmental consequences. World action models (WAMs) incorporate future prediction, yet most rely on costly video generation, while latent alternatives either use predicted futures only as auxiliary targets or infer latent actions from suboptimal visual transitions through costly large-scale video pretraining. We introduce WAVE-0 (World-model-Augmented VLA Execution), a framework that bridges VLAs and WAMs through a lightweight latent world model. By connecting the semantic understanding of pretrained VLAs with explicit future prediction, WAVE-0 allows anticipated action consequences to directly guide action generation. Built on a pretrained VLA, its 257M world model translates VLM-generated latent action plans into predicted future representations that condition the action expert, unifying latent planning, future prediction, and action generation. The world model is trained directly on ground-truth robot actions with a counterfactual ranking objective, requiring neither pixel reconstruction nor large-scale video pretraining. WAVE-0 achieves success rates of 91.47% on RoboTwin 2.0 and 98.7% on LIBERO, along with 88.0%, 61.4%, and 75.0% on LIBERO-Plus, LIBERO-Pro, and LIBERO-Para, respectively. On real-world tasks, it improves the success rate of $π_{0.5}$ from 38.3% to 73.3%. These results demonstrate strong manipulation performance and generalization across diverse distribution shifts.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) policies excel at generalist robotic manipulation but generate actions without explicitly modeling their environmental consequences. World action models (WAMs) incorporate future prediction, yet most rely on costly video generation, while latent alternatives either use predicted futures only as auxiliary targets or infer latent actions from suboptimal visual transitions through costly large-scale video pretraining. We introduce WAVE-0 (World-model-Augmented VLA Execution), a framework that bridges VLAs and WAMs through a lightweight latent world model. Built on a pretrained VLA, its 257M world model translates VLM-generated latent action plans into predicted future representations that condition the action expert, unifying latent planning, future prediction, and action generation. The world model is trained directly on ground-truth robot actions with a counterfactual ranking objective, requiring neither pixel reconstruction nor large-scale video pretraining. WAVE-0 achieves success rates of 91.47% on RoboTwin 2.0 and 98.7% on LIBERO, along with 88.0%, 61.4%, and 75.0% on LIBERO-Plus, LIBERO-Pro, and LIBERO-Para, respectively. On real-world tasks, it improves the success rate of $π_{0.5}$ from 38.3% to 73.3%. These results demonstrate strong manipulation performance and generalization across diverse distribution shifts.

</details>

## 116. Behavioural self-monitoring for autonomous earth observation agents: The attribute reliability monitor (ARM)

**中文标题**: Behavioural self-monitoring for autonomous earth observation agents: The attribute reliability monitor (ARM)

**作者**: Alejandro D. Mousist

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18548v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18548v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18548v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Language and vision language models can support autonomous systems, but changing operating conditions may compromise the reliability of their semantic outputs. This work introduces the Attribute Reliability Monitor (ARM), a framework that accumulates evidence of suspicion over time to assess individual semantic attributes. ARM accommodates different detectors and temporal monitoring methods, while leaving intervention policies to the consuming system. An initial implementation was evaluated using a specialized vision language model developed for satellite thermal infrared imagery. Monitoring followed a black box approach, using only observable responses without access to internal model states, confidence scores, or external reference answers. Under controlled input degradation, disagreement between responses provided evidence for selective suspension of attributes. The results illustrate how external behavioral monitoring can inform continued use of model outputs, while distinguishing operational suspicion from semantic correctness.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 117. MemoryVLN: From Ego-Aware Memory Representation to Cross-Context Memory Reuse in Vision-Language Navigation

**中文标题**: MemoryVLN: From Ego-Aware Memory Representation to Cross-Context Memory Reuse in Vision-Language Navigation

**作者**: Xueyao Wei, Yifei Wang, Delun Li, Shaoan Wang, Junzhi Yu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18478v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18478v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18478v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Large vision-language models (LVLMs) have shown strong potential for embodied vision-language navigation (VLN), yet their performance degrades on long-horizon trajectories due to context-length limits, redundant historical observations, and unstable spatial alignment across time. These issues make it difficult to both compress long navigation histories and reliably reuse past spatial experience. To address these limitations, we propose \textbf{MemoryVLN}, a unified memory-driven navigation system built around memory construction and management. During memory construction, \emph{Long Sequence Compression} employs forgetting-curve-inspired temporal sampling to preserve dense recent evidence and sparse yet representative distant observations under a limited token budget. \emph{Ego-Aware Memory Representation} further integrates 2D semantic tokens, 3D geometric tokens, and 6D agent poses, allowing each memory to encode both what was observed and where it was observed. During memory management, \emph{Hierarchical Memory Bank} maintains in-episode and context-level memories through cross-context warm-start and confidence-controlled write-back. \emph{Memory Enhancement} then retrieves the Top-$K$ relevant memories according to the current navigation state and fuses them with the current visual tokens. Together, these mechanisms transform raw navigation history into compact, spatially grounded, selectively retrievable, and reusable navigation experience. Extensive experiments on R2R-CE and RxR-CE demonstrate consistent improvements over the state-of-the-art baselines by a substantial margin (i.e., 2\% and 6.5\% in successful rate).

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Large vision-language models (LVLMs) have shown strong potential for embodied vision-language navigation (VLN), yet their performance degrades on long-horizon trajectories due to context-length limits, redundant historical observations, and unstable spatial alignment across time. These issues make it difficult to both compress long navigation histories and reliably reuse past spatial experience. To address these limitations, we propose \textbf{MemoryVLN}, a unified memory-driven navigation system built around memory construction and management. During memory construction, \emph{Long Sequence Compression} employs forgetting-curve-inspired temporal sampling to preserve dense recent evidence and sparse yet representative distant observations under a limited token budget. \emph{Ego-Aware Memory Representation} further integrates 2D semantic tokens, 3D geometric tokens, and 6D agent poses, allowing each memory to encode both what was observed and where it was observed. During memory management, \emph{Hierarchical Memory Bank} maintains in-episode and context-level memories through cross-context warm-start and confidence-controlled write-back. \emph{Memory Enhancement} then retrieves the Top-$K$ relevant memories according to the current navigation state and fuses them with the current visual tokens. Together, these mechanisms transform raw navigation history into compact, spatially grounded, selectively retrievable, and reusable navigation experience. Extensive experiments on R2R-CE and RxR-CE demonstrate consistent improvements over the state-of-the-art baselines by a substantial margin (i.e., 2\% and 6.5\% in successful rate).

</details>

## 118. Assessing the Quality of Concrete Scenarios Generated from Scenario Classes for Automated Driving

**中文标题**: Assessing the Quality of Concrete Scenarios Generated from Scenario Classes for Automated Driving

**作者**: Lina Putze, Thies de Graaff, Jan Steffen Becker, Christian Neurohr

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18169v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18169v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18169v1)

**类别**: cs.SE, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

A scenario-based development approach has become state of the art for automated driving systems. Once a set of scenario classes covering the operational design domain is available, concrete scenarios are generated from these scenario classes, e.g., to be used for hazard analysis and risk assessment or for the system's verification and validation. For such applications, a high diversity among the generated concrete scenarios is paramount. The question arises how the quality of collections of concrete scenarios can be assessed in such a setting. In this work, we aggregate scenario distance measures into so-called quality functions that quantify the diversity of concrete scenario collections in the context of automated driving. In particular, we consider the case where scenario classes are formalized as constraint systems and the associated concrete scenario space is not explicitly known. Based on a set of desired properties we construct candidates for such quality functions and give proofs for their property fulfillment. To show the practical relevance of quality functions, we perform a variety of experiments in the context of an abstract scenario-based virtual verification toolchain for automated driving systems. More specifically, we use quality functions to compare the effectiveness of variation methods for sampling, examine the choice of different scenario distance measures, and investigate the effects of temporal aspects on concrete scenario collection quality.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

A scenario-based development approach has become state of the art for automated driving systems. Once a set of scenario classes covering the operational design domain is available, concrete scenarios are generated from these scenario classes, e.g., to be used for hazard analysis and risk assessment or for the system's verification and validation. For such applications, a high diversity among the generated concrete scenarios is paramount. The question arises how the quality of collections of concrete scenarios can be assessed in such a setting. In this work, we aggregate scenario distance measures into so-called quality functions that quantify the diversity of concrete scenario collections in the context of automated driving. In particular, we consider the case where scenario classes are formalized as constraint systems and the associated concrete scenario space is not explicitly known. Based on a set of desired properties we construct candidates for such quality functions and give proofs for their property fulfillment. To show the practical relevance of quality functions, we perform a variety of experiments in the context of an abstract scenario-based virtual verification toolchain for automated driving systems. More specifically, we use quality functions to compare the effectiveness of variation methods for sampling, examine the choice of different scenario distance measures, and investigate the effects of temporal aspects on concrete scenario collection quality.

</details>

---

## 119. RESTA: Time-Scaling Augmentation for Data-Efficient Robot Imitation Learning

**中文标题**: RESTA: Time-Scaling Augmentation for Data-Efficient Robot Imitation Learning

**作者**: Yunpeng Jiang, Jianshu Hu, Han Fang, Zhiyuan Guan, Paul Weng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18163v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18163v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18163v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robot manipulation policies trained through imitation learning commonly rely on costly demonstrations recorded in one task direction and at one temporal resolution. We propose Reversal and Stride-based Trajectory Augmentation (RESTA), which exploits time-scaling data augmentation to improve data efficiency through trajectory reversal and temporal resampling. Trajectory-reversal augmentation reverses and relabels demonstrations between paired tasks with opposite objectives, reusing recorded rollouts without additional environment interaction. Because contact-rich manipulation is not exactly reversible, we treat reversal as an approximate paired-task prior rather than a physical symmetry. Temporal-stride augmentation varies observation and action sampling strides and conditions the policy on the resulting offsets, enabling fixed or randomized temporal schedules. Experiments across 16 paired bimanual manipulation tasks show that trajectory reversal is particularly effective in the low-data regime and that temporal-stride augmentation improves success under randomized temporal schedules. With 25 and 50 independently collected demonstrations per task, RESTA achieves 78.4% and 85.2% average success, respectively, matching or exceeding baselines trained with twice as many demonstrations (77.3% and 85.4%). These results show that RESTA can improve data efficiency in robot imitation learning.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Robot manipulation policies trained through imitation learning commonly rely on costly demonstrations recorded in one task direction and at one temporal resolution. We propose Reversal and Stride-based Trajectory Augmentation (RESTA), which exploits time-scaling data augmentation to improve data efficiency through trajectory reversal and temporal resampling. Trajectory-reversal augmentation reverses and relabels demonstrations between paired tasks with opposite objectives, reusing recorded rollouts without additional environment interaction. Because contact-rich manipulation is not exactly reversible, we treat reversal as an approximate paired-task prior rather than a physical symmetry. Temporal-stride augmentation varies observation and action sampling strides and conditions the policy on the resulting offsets, enabling fixed or randomized temporal schedules. Experiments across 16 paired bimanual manipulation tasks show that trajectory reversal is particularly effective in the low-data regime and that temporal-stride augmentation improves success under randomized temporal schedules. With 25 and 50 independently collected demonstrations per task, RESTA achieves 78.4% and 85.2% average success, respectively, matching or exceeding baselines trained with twice as many demonstrations (77.3% and 85.4%). These results show that RESTA can improve data efficiency in robot imitation learning.

</details>

## 120. Data-Free Continual Learning for World Action Model with Rectified Generative Replay

**中文标题**: Data-Free Continual Learning for World Action Model with Rectified Generative Replay

**作者**: Kaipeng Fang, Hangxu Liu, Chaojian Shi, Yunsong Zhou, Hongzi Zhu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18038v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18038v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18038v2)

**项目主页**: https://kaipengfang.github.io/r2-wam/

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) built on pretrained video generation models adapt quickly to new robotic tasks, but they forget previously learned tasks under sequential fine-tuning. Experience replay (ER) mitigates such forgetting by rehearsing data of previous tasks, yet this data is often unavailable in robotics, where a policy is deployed as weights only and its training data is not shared along with it. In this work, we ask whether a WAM can achieve data-free continual learning by regenerating its own past. Our key observation is that a WAM can indeed autoregressively regenerate complete rollouts of previously learned tasks and replay them to mitigate forgetting. However, replaying such raw rollouts still lags far behind replaying real data, which we attribute to two issues. First, the WAM is unable to recognize task completion and keeps generating purposeless motion after the task is done. Second, under open-loop generation, the generated actions gradually drift away from the generated observations. To address both issues, we present rectified generative replay (R$^2$-WAM), which truncates generated rollouts at task completion with an agentic evaluator and filters out rollouts whose actions are physically inconsistent with the observations. On the four LIBERO suites and five real-world tasks, R$^2$-WAM reduces forgetting by over 90% relative to sequential fine-tuning and nearly matches ER without accessing any data from previous tasks. Our analysis further reveals that forgetting in WAMs concentrates in inverse dynamics modeling: a forgotten WAM still produces visually plausible observations of previous tasks while its generated actions fail. Project page: https://kaipengfang.github.io/r2-wam/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) built on pretrained video generation models adapt quickly to new robotic tasks, but they forget previously learned tasks under sequential fine-tuning. Experience replay (ER) mitigates such forgetting by rehearsing data of previous tasks, yet this data is often unavailable in robotics, where a policy is deployed as weights only and its training data is not shared along with it. In this work, we ask whether a WAM can achieve data-free continual learning by regenerating its own past. Our key observation is that a WAM can indeed autoregressively regenerate complete rollouts of previously learned tasks and replay them to mitigate forgetting. However, replaying such raw rollouts still lags far behind replaying real data, which we attribute to two issues. First, the WAM is unable to recognize task completion and keeps generating purposeless motion after the task is done. Second, under open-loop generation, the generated actions gradually drift away from the generated observations. To address both issues, we present rectified generative replay (R$^2$-WAM), which truncates generated rollouts at task completion with an agentic evaluator and filters out rollouts whose actions are physically inconsistent with the observations. On the four LIBERO suites and five real-world tasks, R$^2$-WAM reduces forgetting by over 90% relative to sequential fine-tuning and nearly matches ER without accessing any data from previous tasks. Our analysis further reveals that forgetting in WAMs concentrates in inverse dynamics modeling: a forgotten WAM still produces visually plausible observations of previous tasks while its generated actions fail. Project page: https://kaipengfang.github.io/r2-wam/

</details>

## 121. GLST: Global-Local Spatio-Temporal Memory Decomposition Framework for Online Chained-Goal Navigation

**中文标题**: GLST: Global-Local Spatio-Temporal Memory Decomposition Framework for Online Chained-Goal Navigation

**作者**: Yousef Liang, Ryan Ardalan, Yarden Goraly, Chenhao Zhu, Yue Yin et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17927v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17927v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17927v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autonomous navigation in real-world environments often requires long-horizon operation under partial observability and evolving goals. Existing mapless navigation approaches predominantly assume fixed goals throughout an episode, limiting applicability to practical scenarios where goals change online during execution. We study online chained-goal navigation, where goals evolve through existing-goal redirection or new-goal emergence without explicit transition signals. Under this setting, we find navigation may require either continued exploration or strategic revisitation, creating conflicting memory requirements. We propose Global-Local Spatio-Temporal Belief Learning (GLST), a memory-decomposition framework that separates latent beliefs into global-local spatial and long-short temporal representations for long-horizon reasoning. We further introduce goal-conditioned rewards to balance exploration and exploitation under evolving goals. Extensive experiments in simulation across diverse terrains demonstrate improved chained-goal navigation performance over baselines. Real-robot experiments using the Spot quadruped further validate our memory decomposition framework.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Autonomous navigation in real-world environments often requires long-horizon operation under partial observability and evolving goals. Existing mapless navigation approaches predominantly assume fixed goals throughout an episode, limiting applicability to practical scenarios where goals change online during execution. We study online chained-goal navigation, where goals evolve through existing-goal redirection or new-goal emergence without explicit transition signals. Under this setting, we find navigation may require either continued exploration or strategic revisitation, creating conflicting memory requirements. We propose Global-Local Spatio-Temporal Belief Learning (GLST), a memory-decomposition framework that separates latent beliefs into global-local spatial and long-short temporal representations for long-horizon reasoning. We further introduce goal-conditioned rewards to balance exploration and exploitation under evolving goals. Extensive experiments in simulation across diverse terrains demonstrate improved chained-goal navigation performance over baselines. Real-robot experiments using the Spot quadruped further validate our memory decomposition framework.

</details>

## 122. WoVR+: World Model as Reliable Simulator for Versatile VLA Policy Optimization with RL

**中文标题**: WoVR+: World Model as Reliable Simulator for Versatile VLA Policy Optimization with RL

**作者**: Shangqing Zhou, Zhennan Jiang, Ning Ma, Xin Fu, Yupeng Zheng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17922v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17922v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17922v1)

**项目主页**: https://youmo445.github.io/WoVR_Plus.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Action-conditioned world models can reduce costly real-robot interaction in reinforcement learning for Vision-Language-Action models (VLA-RL), but hallucinations can mislead policy optimization. Extending them to richer VLA interfaces and long-horizon tasks raises challenges in spatiotemporal and cross-modal consistency, exploration-reliability balance in RL, and compounding rollout errors. We present WoVR+, extending WoVR's hallucination-aware WM-RL framework by redesigning the learned environment and imagined interaction protocol: A VLA-interface-aligned world model provides multi-view prediction and proprioceptive updates while mitigating hallucinations. Chunk-wise noise preserves flow-based policy exploration while limiting prediction-degrading action jitter. For long-horizon tasks, stage-local imagined training combines stage-entry resets, stage-specific rewards, local horizons, and controllable sampling to shorten generated prefixes and balance training across stages. WoVR+ improves success over SFT by 21.09% and 16.7% on RoboTwin and 6 real-robot tasks. Long-horizon phone-packaging success rises from 30% to 75%. Videos are available at https://youmo445.github.io/WoVR_Plus.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Action-conditioned world models can reduce costly real-robot interaction in reinforcement learning for Vision-Language-Action models (VLA-RL), but hallucinations can mislead policy optimization. Extending them to richer VLA interfaces and long-horizon tasks raises challenges in spatiotemporal and cross-modal consistency, exploration-reliability balance in RL, and compounding rollout errors. We present WoVR+, extending WoVR's hallucination-aware WM-RL framework by redesigning the learned environment and imagined interaction protocol: A VLA-interface-aligned world model provides multi-view prediction and proprioceptive updates while mitigating hallucinations. Chunk-wise noise preserves flow-based policy exploration while limiting prediction-degrading action jitter. For long-horizon tasks, stage-local imagined training combines stage-entry resets, stage-specific rewards, local horizons, and controllable sampling to shorten generated prefixes and balance training across stages. WoVR+ improves success over SFT by 21.09% and 16.7% on RoboTwin and 6 real-robot tasks. Long-horizon phone-packaging success rises from 30% to 75%. Videos are available at https://youmo445.github.io/WoVR_Plus.github.io/.

</details>

## 123. Agentic Robot Creation through Generation, Verification, and Revision

**中文标题**: Agentic Robot Creation through Generation, Verification, and Revision

**作者**: Xuebing Li, Yibo He, Sitong Liu, Ran Ji, Jiayuan Gu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17881v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17881v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17881v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Can an AI agent turn high-level user intent into a functioning robot and teach it how to behave? We study this question from two complementary aspects: embodiment and behavior creation. For embodiment creation, we investigate how well current multimodal models capture mechanical requirements, and introduce computational metrics for evaluating generated designs before fabrication. For behavior creation, we present Roboforge, an agentic framework that turns task specifications into executable policies through motion generation, policy learning, and execution-grounded revision. Given a robot embodiment and task, the agent generates a reference motion, configures the learning objective, and trains a policy. Crucially, motion remains in the loop: rollout feedback can revise the reference motion, reward, or both. We evaluate a generated embodiment across multiple MLLMs and behavior creation across four diverse robots against reward-refinement and one-shot motion baselines. Our computational metrics expose recurring mechanical failures in generated designs, while behavior experiments show that execution-grounded refinement improves task-level behavior and execution quality. Finally, we fabricate an agent-generated Gorilla and directly deploy learned behaviors on the physical robot.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Can an AI agent turn high-level user intent into a functioning robot and teach it how to behave? We study this question from two complementary aspects: embodiment and behavior creation. For embodiment creation, we investigate how well current multimodal models capture mechanical requirements, and introduce computational metrics for evaluating generated designs before fabrication. For behavior creation, we present Roboforge, an agentic framework that turns task specifications into executable policies through motion generation, policy learning, and execution-grounded revision. Given a robot embodiment and task, the agent generates a reference motion, configures the learning objective, and trains a policy. Crucially, motion remains in the loop: rollout feedback can revise the reference motion, reward, or both. We evaluate a generated embodiment across multiple MLLMs and behavior creation across four diverse robots against reward-refinement and one-shot motion baselines. Our computational metrics expose recurring mechanical failures in generated designs, while behavior experiments show that execution-grounded refinement improves task-level behavior and execution quality. Finally, we fabricate an agent-generated Gorilla and directly deploy learned behaviors on the physical robot.

</details>
